2026-08-03 W1-04a done — PPU background: replaced `system::ppu_stub::PpuStub` with a real `crate::ppu::Ppu` (new `rf-core-api` dep, exempt from arch rule 1 as the shared contract crate). Loopy `v`/`t`/`x`/`w` implemented exactly per nesdev.org/wiki/PPU_scrolling's quoted pseudocode ($2000/$2005/$2006 write formulas, coarse-X/Y increment, hori(v)/vert(v) copies); `$2002` clears vblank+`w` (not sprite0/overflow, which only clear at the pre-render line's dot 1, per nesdev.org/wiki/PPU_registers "automatically cleared on dot 1 of the prerender scanline"); `$2007` implements the delayed-read buffer with the palette-bypass quirk (buffer refilled from `addr & 0x2FFF`, the common underneath-nametable-read approximation nesdev names but doesn't formula-ize). Fetch pipeline (nesdev.org/wiki/PPU_rendering dot diagram): NT/AT/pattern-lo/pattern-hi over 2-cycle-per-access 8-dot tiles, shift-register reload at dots 9,17,...,257,329,337 (nesdev's own quoted dot list, extended into the 321-336 prefetch window), coarse-X increment derived one dot earlier (8,16,...,256,328,336) since the dot-9 reload's NT fetch needs `v` already advanced; transparent (pattern=00) pixels forced to address 0 rather than `attr<<2|0` (the classic bug nesdev's "always $3F00" note calls out). `palette_index` is a **public commitment**: the resolved 6-bit palette-RAM *value* at render time, not the 0-31 address — snapshot-immune to mid-frame palette writes, binding for W1-04b/W3-xx. `CoreSink` emission: `Ppu` buffers completed scanlines in a queue preallocated for one frame (240 rows) but NOT capped — an undrained second frame accumulates to 480 rather than silently dropping the first (tested) — and `NesBus::drain_video(&mut dyn CoreSink)` flushes it — decoupled from `Ppu::tick` because `CpuBus::read`/`write` have no sink parameter by design. Criterion 3 is tested past the trivial backdrop-only case too: a uniform-nametable/uniform-attribute/uniform-pattern fixture proves a real palette-RAM value + `Background(0)` layer resolve correctly once the pipeline's ~16-pixel warm-up passes, and a second fixture proves a transparent (pattern=00) pixel under a non-zero attribute reads the universal backdrop, never `attr<<2|0`. Bus seam: added one `tick_master(cycles)` helper (the only place `master_cycle` is now mutated) which also ticks the PPU 3 dots/cycle, including through OAM DMA's stolen cycles — `crates/rf-nes/src/cpu/` still has zero PPU/tick references (grep-verified), seam intact. NESTEST CANARY: re-confirmed 8991/8991 byte-exact after the swap (real fetched ROM/log) — the real nestest.log has zero `$20xx`-`$3Fxx` operand lines (grep-verified), so the PPU register path is provably never exercised by it; `NesBus::peek`'s new PPU arm calls a side-effect-free `Ppu::peek_register` (mirrors the existing `Controller::peek_bit`/`read_bit` split) rather than the real, side-effecting `read_register`, so `peek`'s no-side-effects contract holds unchanged — no `peek` semantics changed, so the canary's premise (peek's `$FF` APU-stub arm) is untouched. Scope fence (all explicitly deferred, documented in `crate::ppu`'s module doc): sprite evaluation (W1-05a); odd-frame dot-339 skip and VBL/NMI edge-suppression timing, i.e. `ppu_vbl_nmi` (W1-04b/W1-05b per conductor brief) — `NesBus::nmi_line`/`irq_line` stay at the `CpuBus` default, deliberately not wired to the new (real but edge-unverified) vblank/NMI-enable state; catch-up scheduling + the CI diff EMULATION_CORES.md §1 requires against lock-step (explicit debt, lock-step-only built here); Mapper trait/CHR banking (next mapper ticket, W1-02 precedent — CHR is a flat `Vec<u8>` mapper-0 owns directly). Zero `unsafe`. cpu_step bench unaffected (drives its own `FlatBus` mock, never `NesBus`). workspace: 281 passing / 0 failed / 1 ignored, same with `RF_NES6502_VECTORS` pointed at a nonexistent path and nestest ROM/log moved aside and restored; rf-nes 103 (up from 57). 2026-08-03 W1-04b done — PPU frame-timing edges + golden frame, the oracle W1-04a never had. HEADLINE: W1-04a's shift-register/reload pipeline is CORRECT — a new tick-driven test (ppu/tests/frame_timing.rs) starts from `Ppu::new`'s real pre-render-line position (no mid-scanline poke), ticks through the pre-render line's real dots 321-336 prefetch into scanline 0, and pixels 0-15 land on the exact tiles the nametable names; the synthetic-ROM golden frame (below) independently confirms this across a full 256x240 frame. Found instead, while building that golden frame: a genuine, independent bug in W1-04a's `write_scroll` (ppu/scroll.rs) — the second `$2005` write's clear-mask was `0x8FFF`, which fails to clear `t`'s coarse-Y bits (5-9) before OR-ing new ones in, so a scroll-reset sequence starting from a nonzero `t` (e.g. right after pointing `$2006` at a palette address, as any real init routine and this ticket's fixture both do) leaves stale coarse-Y bits behind; invisible in every pre-existing test because they all started from `t=0`, where OR and replace agree. Re-derived the correct mask (`0x8C1F`) against nesdev.org/wiki/PPU_scrolling's literal second-write diagram (fetched live, not from memory), added a regression test that fails without the fix, and confirmed by reverting the one-line fix and re-running the golden-frame test: it fails with a precise `pixel (144, 0): expected ... got ...` diagnostic, then passes again once restored — the fixture demonstrably discriminates. Odd/even-frame idle-dot skip implemented (`frame_is_odd` field, `advance_counters`): dot 339 of the pre-render line jumps straight to (0,0) on odd frames with rendering enabled (dot 340 never happens), verified both directions (skip fires / doesn't fire without rendering enabled) plus an end-to-end 3-frame dot-count check (341*262, then one less, then back to 341*262). Golden frame (`crates/rf-harness/tests/golden_frame_bg.rs`): a synthetic NROM built from a hand-assembled 6502 program (LDA #imm / STA $abs / JMP $abs only — no branches, no relative-offset arithmetic to get wrong) that pokes 960 nametable bytes, 64 attribute bytes, and 16 palette bytes through the real $2006/$2007 protocol; the expected 256x240 frame is computed analytically (`tile_index(row,col) = row*37 + col*7 + 11 mod 256`, both 37 and 7 coprime with 256, so no two of the fixture's rows/columns collide) rather than by running the emulator and saving whatever came out — a one-tile shift or a wrong tile at a scanline-row boundary changes the expected value instead of aliasing to another valid answer. Compared pixel-by-pixel (exact mismatch location on failure) and via `rf_harness::hash_frame_palette_indices` (reused, no second hashing impl), plus a same-hash check across two consecutive captured frames (odd/even parity must not change a static scene's pixels). Deliberately NOT added to scripts/validate-evidence.mjs — fully synthetic, no gitignored ROM, runs in ordinary CI. Re-validated the three facts W1-04a derived rather than quoted (coarse-X-increment dots, attribute-quadrant bit-shift, `$2007` buffer refill via `addr & 0x2FFF`) — all three exercised correctly by the golden frame, no changes needed. workspace: 288 passing / 0 failed / 1 ignored (rf-nes 103 -> 109: +1 scroll-mask regression test, +5 frame_timing tests; rf-harness +1 golden-frame integration test), same with `RF_NES6502_VECTORS` pointed at a nonexistent path and nestest ROM/log moved aside and restored. NESTEST CANARY: re-confirmed 8991/8991 byte-exact, live re-run (ppu/** changes don't touch CPU code — as expected, unaffected). Full gate green: fmt/clippy(--all-targets, -D warnings)/test/arch/plan/traceability/evidence all exit 0 (evidence file untouched — ppu/** is not a covered path for the nes6502/nestest evidence, confirmed still passing).

2026-08-03 W1-04b conductor verification — full seven-command gate re-run INDEPENDENTLY (RF-L-08): fmt 0 · clippy 0 · cargo test --workspace 0 (288 passed / 0 failed / 1 ignored; rf-nes 103 -> 109) · validate-arch 0 · validate-plan 0 · validate-traceability 0 · validate-evidence 0. Nestest canary re-run by conductor: 8991/8991 byte-exact. Evidence correctly NOT regenerated — crates/rf-nes/src/ppu is deliberately outside every suite's covered-paths and nothing here affects nes6502 or nestest. THE ORACLE PAID FOR ITSELF: W1-04a's shift-register pipeline was CORRECT (the flagged one-tile-shift risk did not materialise — a new test drives the real dots 321-336 prefetch from the actual pre-render position and proves pixels 0-15 land right), but the golden frame caught a DIFFERENT genuine bug W1-04a had shipped — write_scroll's second-$2005 mask 0x8FFF fails to clear t's coarse-Y bits 5-9 before OR-ing, so a scroll reset from nonzero t left stale coarse-Y behind. Invisible to every prior test because they all started from t=0, where OR and replace agree. The conductor RE-DERIVED the correct mask independently from nesdev's second-write bit layout (keep coarse-X bits 0-4 + nametable bits 10-11 only) and got 0x8C1F, matching the fix, and confirmed 0x8FFF provably leaves bits 5-9 set. MUTATION-TESTED TWICE: reverting the mask fix made the golden-frame test fail; forcing the odd-frame skip condition false made both frame-timing tests fail; restored 288 green. THE GOLDEN IS ANALYTIC, NOT RECORDED — expected_pixel(x,y) is closed-form (tile_index = row*37 + col*7 + 11 mod 256, conductor verified gcd(37,256)=gcd(7,256)=1 so nothing aliases) and the file holds ZERO hash constants, so it cannot have been fitted to buggy output. That was the specific failure mode this ticket was briefed against, and it held.

2026-08-03 W1-05a done — PPU sprites: secondary OAM evaluation, the 8-sprite limit, and the buggy overflow-flag diagonal scan, plus BG/sprite compositing (new `crates/rf-nes/src/ppu/sprites.rs`). RULING APPLIED (Brad, this ticket's pre-flight): the sink stays accuracy-exact — `PpuPixel::dropped_by_limit` is always `false` on the NES path (a limit-dropped sprite is simply never copied into `secondary_oam` in the first place, so it never reaches `output_pixel` to be flagged); `rf-core-api/src/video.rs`'s doc was pre-corrected by the conductor and left untouched. Evaluation modeled as two buffers per nesdev.org/wiki/PPU_OAM's "sprite data is delayed by one scanline" fact: `evaluate_sprites` (dot 65, visible lines only) fills `secondary_oam` using the CURRENT scanline as the in-range comparison; `load_sprite_units` (dot 257, visible AND pre-render — the same window `OAMADDR` resets to 0 in) LATCHES each found sprite's CHR pattern bytes (not a re-derived row at arbitrary render time — advisor caught that this only holds if rendering stayed continuously enabled since the preceding scanline) into `active_sprites`, read by the scanline that follows; pre-render's dot 1 unconditionally clears both buffers, which is what makes scanline 0 render zero sprites without a special case. Overflow bug: nesdev's numbered algorithm (wording normalized, retrieved 2026-08-03 — NOT claimed verbatim, two independent fetches of the live page returned different numbering) implemented so a miss during the post-8-found search increments BOTH n and m (m wrapping mod 4, never reset) — cross-checked against nesalizer's `oam_addr = ((oam_addr+4)&0xFC)|((oam_addr+1)&3)`. Two discriminating unit tests prove the actual bug, not a "9th sprite exists" shortcut: a false-negative (a genuine 9th in-range sprite's real Y byte is skipped because the drift has m=1, not 0, by the time n reaches it) and a false-positive (a misaligned tile byte numerically in-range sets the flag despite no real 9th sprite) — both fail against a naive "always check real Y" implementation (verified by deliberately substituting it and re-running). MUTATION-TESTED five ways before closing (cutoff removed, overflow scan naive'd, OAMADDR reset disabled, sprite-vs-sprite priority reversed — each confirmed to fail exactly the tests designed to catch it, then restored clean; diff'd byte-identical against a pre-mutation backup after each restore). Oracle (criterion 1 has no test ROM until W1-05b, W1-04a's exact prior position): `crates/rf-harness/tests/golden_frame_sprites.rs`, a synthetic-NROM fixture in the `golden_frame_bg.rs` style — 10 sprites via real $2003/$2004 writes (OAMADDR ends non-zero, the ticket's own trap), closed-form `expected_pixel_at_target_row(x)`, zero hash constants. Three specific pixels a bug changes instead of aliasing: x=50 (two in-limit sprites overlap — wrong priority flips the value), x=150 (an in-limit sprite is overlapped by the dropped 9th — wrong cutoff flips the value), x=200 (only the dropped 10th covers it — any leak turns backdrop into a sprite color). All three independently confirmed to fail under the matching mutation above. Also asserts `bus.peek(0x2002)&0x20` (overflow flag, straightforward for this fixture's genuinely-in-range 9th sprite) and frame-to-frame hash stability via `find_first_divergence`. Sprite-0 hit (STATUS_SPRITE0_HIT) untouched, left for W1-05b. workspace: 305 passing / 0 failed (rf-nes 111 -> 125: +14 `ppu/tests/sprite_evaluation.rs`, +2 `sprites.rs` inline; rf-harness +1 golden-frame integration test), same with `RF_NES6502_VECTORS` pointed at a nonexistent path and nestest ROM/log moved aside and restored. CANARIES: nestest 8991/8991 byte-exact (unchanged — ppu/** doesn't touch cpu/**); `golden_frame_bg.rs` (W1-04b's background oracle) unchanged and still green — sprite work did not perturb background rendering. Full gate green: fmt/clippy(--all-targets, -D warnings)/test/arch/plan/traceability/evidence all exit 0. STALE DOC FLAGGED FOR THE CONDUCTOR, NOT FIXED (out of write scope): `docs/design/EMULATION_CORES.md` §2.2 lines 72-73 still say "in Enhanced mode the scanline emit includes dropped sprites" — superseded by this ticket's ruling; only `rf-core-api/src/video.rs` was corrected at the source.

2026-08-03 W1-05a follow-up — self-review (advisor pass) before handoff caught two real issues in the just-committed work, fixed here: (1) `golden_frame_sprites.rs`'s overflow-flag assertion was VACUOUS — `Ppu::new`'s zero-filled OAM left primary indices 10-63 at Y=0, which is trivially "in range" at scanline 0, so phantom sprites already tripped `STATUS_SPRITE_OVERFLOW` before the fixture's real 9th sprite was ever evaluated; the assertion would have passed even with the real 8-sprite-cap code deleted (confirmed: stubbing `evaluate_sprites`'s phase 2 with `if false && ...` left it passing). Fixed by filling all 54 unused primary-OAM entries with `$FF` (never in range for any scanline) via a real `$2003`/`$2004` two-pass write (fill first at OAMADDR=40, then the 10 real sprites last at OAMADDR=0, so OAMADDR still ends non-zero — the ticket's trap, now satisfied for a real reason instead of an accidental one); the same `if false` stub now correctly fails ONLY the overflow assertion, pixels unaffected — re-verified. Also switched from a single post-hoc peek to periodic sampling across the run, since the flag legitimately clears every pre-render dot 1 and a single fixed-step peek could land in that window by chance (did, once, during this fix). (2) `load_sprite_units`'s row computation (`self.scanline - sprite.y`) assumed `evaluate_sprites` had just run on the same scanline; if rendering is toggled off between dot 65 and back on before dot 257 of a visible scanline (a normal mid-frame raster-effect pattern), that invariant doesn't hold — `secondary_oam` is stale from an earlier scanline, and the row could be `>= height` (wrong CHR address, `pattern_lo==pattern_hi`) or `sprite.y > self.scanline` (`u16` underflow) — silently wrong pixels, the exact W1-04a failure shape. Fixed with a one-line `sprite_in_range` re-check before latching (skip, don't crash or misdraw, on failure); no oracle exists for this specific mid-scanline-toggle case, so it's asserted defensively rather than test-covered — noted here rather than left silent. Re-ran the full seven-command gate after both fixes: fmt 0 · clippy(--all-targets, -D warnings) 0 · test 0 (305 passed / 0 failed, same with vectors absent and nestest moved aside) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0 · evidence 0. Canaries unchanged: nestest 8991/8991, `golden_frame_bg.rs` green.

2026-08-03 W1-05a follow-up 2 — conductor's own independent mutation pass found a THIRD gap, more severe than either of the first two: `sprites.rs`'s sprite/background priority resolution (`output_pixel`'s `s.behind_background` branches, the attribute-byte bit 5 decision) had ZERO test coverage that actually exercised it. Mutating `behind_background: sprite.attr & 0x20 != 0` to `== 0` (inverting front/behind) passed the full workspace suite — 305/305, unchanged. Root cause: the one existing test for this (`priority_bit_puts_the_sprite_behind_an_opaque_background_pixel`) evaluated its sprite at scanline 0 and checked the render at scanline 0 too — but `sprites.rs`'s own one-scanline pipeline delay means scanline 0 NEVER renders any sprite (no evaluation happens on the pre-render line to populate it), so `active_sprite_count` was 0 for the entire scanline the test checked, and the mutated branch was never even reached. The SAME failure class the ticket's own W1-04a-lesson warns about (start-from-zero-state hides bug classes) and the SAME class this agent had already caught once in the harness's overflow-flag assertion — priority resolution just wasn't on that mutation list. Fixed: (1) rewrote the rf-nes unit test as `priority_bit_full_truth_table_at_opaque_and_transparent_background`, evaluating on scanline 0 and checking the render on scanline 1 (the real delay, not a shortcut), covering all three truth-table cells that matter — front-over-opaque-BG (sprite wins), behind-over-opaque-BG (BG wins), behind-over-TRANSPARENT-BG (sprite still wins, since "behind" loses only to an opaque BG pixel, not unconditionally — the naive-implementation discriminator). Caught its own bug while writing it: the BG tile's CHR was only filled at row 0, but scanline 1 (where the sprites actually render) reads the tile's row 1 -- fixed by filling all 8 rows. (2) Extended `golden_frame_sprites.rs` with a "BG stripe": background rendering turned ON (previously off for the whole file) and the nametable made transparent everywhere except one tile column (x=112-119), where sprite 6 (repurposed from a plain anchor to a behind-priority sprite) now sits — the correct expected value there is the BG stripe's own color, not sprite 6's; `expected_pixel_at_target_row`/`expected_frame` extended to a real 2-input (BG opacity x sprite) priority table, still closed-form, still zero recorded hashes. Verified: with the fix reverted (mutation reapplied), `priority_bit_full_truth_table...` fails at the opaque-BG assertion, and `golden_frame_sprites.rs` fails at pixel (112, 100) (`left: 1, right: 5` -- sprite 6's color where the BG stripe's should be) -- both independently, both restored clean and diff-verified against the pre-mutation state afterward. Re-ran the full seven-command gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (305 passed / 0 failed, same with vectors absent and nestest moved aside) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0 · evidence 0. Canaries unchanged: nestest 8991/8991, `golden_frame_bg.rs` green.

2026-08-03 W1-05b done — PPU sprite-0 hit + VBlank/NMI wiring, plus TWO blockers found and resolved beyond the conductor's pre-flight (RF-D-002 self-blocking-scope pattern, sixth occurrence). Sprite-0 hit (`sprites.rs::output_pixel`): reuses `background_pixel`/`sprite_pixel`'s ALREADY-masked opacity for both the x=255 exclusion and nesdev's "either left-8 bit clear suppresses" rule, so both fall out of one `x != 255 && bg_addr.is_some() && sprite.oam_index==0` check rather than duplicated logic; priority-blind by construction since `sprite_pixel`'s search never consults `behind_background`. NMI (`Ppu::nmi_line`, `NesBus::CpuBus::nmi_line`): a pure `$2000.7 AND $2002.7` level, forwarded straight through — the CPU's existing W1-01b edge-detector turns that level into "retriggerable by toggling NMI_output during VBlank" for free, verified at the level layer (toggle-while-set test). VBlank-read suppression (`scroll.rs::read_status`, `suppress_vblank_this_frame`): the reachable half of nesdev's $2002-read race — a read landing exactly one PPU dot before the set (scanline 241 dot 0, given this crate's tick-AFTER-register-read ordering) latches a per-frame flag that skips the set entirely, reset each pre-render dot 1. BLOCKER 1 (criterion 1, NOT flagged by conductor pre-flight): tagging `sprite_hit_tests` as blargg's `$6000` protocol was wrong — verified against the real fetched `readme.txt` ("reports the result on screen and by beeping") and `source/runtime/validation.a` (zero `6000`/`DE B0 61` matches; a real RAM byte, `result = $f8`, instead) — empirically, all 11 ROMs timed out `TimeoutBeforeSignatureValid` every run under the wrong protocol. `blargg_evidence::run_ram_result` reads `$00F8` instead, with a "run the full frame budget unconditionally, read once at the end" design after an EARLIER "stable across 2 consecutive frame polls" heuristic produced false `Failed(2)`/`Failed(3)` results on every ROM (root cause, found by tracing `test_for_hit`'s real assembly: each sub-test's own error code sits in `result` for ~1 frame BEFORE that sub-test's check even runs, indistinguishable from genuine completion at that granularity). Once fixed: 11/11 real sprite_hit ROMs pass, including a from-behind-background hit (test code 3) and left-8/x=255 exclusions this ticket's own unit tests independently pin down (`sprite_zero_hit.rs`, 10 tests — two of which caught real bugs in the TESTS themselves during authoring: the x=255 isolation sprite also covered x=248-254, making the exclusion vacuous, and a custom single-opaque-column tile was only painted at CHR row 0 while the one-scanline delay renders row 1 — both fixed, not the implementation). BLOCKER 2 (criterion 2, MMC1, WAS conductor-flagged and pre-resolved): swapped the combined `ppu-vbl-nmi` artifact for the 10 real `rom_singles/` NROM ROMs per the pre-flight notes; all 10 fetched and sha256-verified clean. RESULT (investigated, not guessed — advisor explicitly redirected two theories back to measurement): 4/10 pass (`01-vbl_basics`, `03-vbl_clear_time`, `04-nmi_control`, `09-even_odd_frames`); 6/10 fail at a sub-CPU-cycle timing ceiling this crate's architecture cannot reach without CPU-crate changes (out of write scope) — `02-vbl_set_time`'s own table shows the suppression boundary landing one CPU cycle (3 PPU dots) earlier than the real ROM's expected table (verified two ways: disabling suppression entirely still fails a DIFFERENT way, proving it isn't simply "wrong logic"; and the "fails at row 03 instead of 04" signature matches another emulator author's independently reported symptom, forums.nesdev.org/viewtopic.php?t=17682), and `05/06/07/08` show the identical one-cycle-early signature; `10-even_odd_timing` fails separately and specifically at "Clock is skipped too late, relative to enabling BG" (sub-test 3 of 4; 2 and 4 pass), a plausible but NOT A/B-isolated write-side twin of the same ceiling. All 6 recorded as ticketed waivers (`crates/rf-harness/waivers.toml`, expiry 2026-11-01) with per-ROM justification, not silently dropped — `crate::ppu`'s module doc "Scope fence" section carries the full derivation. Evidence pipeline: `sprite_hit_tests`/`ppu_vbl_nmi` are the 3rd/4th Tier-A-local suites (`blargg_evidence.rs`, new — `run`/`run_ram_result`, both real-`NesBus`-driven since no `EmulatorCore` exists yet, both with their own hand-derived unit tests); `local_gate_evidence.rs` gained per-ROM `AccuracyRow`s (`run_suite`/`run_six_thousand_suite`/`run_ram_result_suite`) and per-suite JSON summaries; `validate-evidence.mjs` switched its per-suite check from `.find()` to `.filter()` (nes6502/nestest have one row each; these two have 10/11) plus expected-count anti-placeholder guards. `crates/rf-nes/src/ppu/tests/blargg_roms.rs` is the cargo-test peer (skip-if-`roms/`-absent): asserts sprite_hit_tests 11/11, and asserts only the 4 known-good `ppu_vbl_nmi` ROMs (a regression there is a real bug; the other 6 are reported, not asserted, so a future partial fix doesn't need this test edited). workspace: 330 passing / 0 failed (rf-nes 125 -> 146: +10 `sprite_zero_hit.rs`, +9 `nmi.rs`, +2 `blargg_roms.rs`; rf-harness 74 -> 78: +4 `blargg_evidence.rs`), same with vectors/nestest/these-ROMs all absent (`roms/` moved aside and restored) and with `RF_NES6502_VECTORS` pointed at a nonexistent path. CANARIES: nestest 8991/8991 byte-exact (re-run live after wiring NMI — the highest-risk canary per this ticket's own brief, since NMI is CPU-visible; unaffected, as expected, since nestest never touches `$2000-$3FFF`); `golden_frame_bg.rs` and `golden_frame_sprites.rs` (W1-04b/W1-05a's background/sprite oracles) both still green, unperturbed by the sprite-0-hit addition. Full gate green: fmt/clippy(--all-targets, -D warnings)/test/arch/plan/traceability/evidence all exit 0 (evidence regenerated on a clean tree; see commit trailer for the refreshed retroforge_commit pin). OUT OF SCOPE, FLAGGED NOT FIXED: `sprite_overflow_tests` almost certainly shares `sprite_hit_tests`' pre-`$6000` runtime and the same protocol mistag — noted in `tests/rom-manifest.toml` and `docs/TESTING.md` §4 for whichever ticket implements that suite.

2026-08-03 W1-05b conductor verification + Brad's ruling — CLOSED, with the ppu_vbl_nmi ceiling split into a new ticket W1-05c. Full seven-command gate re-run INDEPENDENTLY: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · cargo test --workspace 0 (330 passed / 0 failed / 1 ignored) · validate-arch 0 · validate-plan 0 · validate-traceability 0 · validate-evidence 0 (now FOUR Tier-A-local suites: nes6502 2,560,000 cases, nestest 8991 lines, sprite_hit_tests 11/11, ppu_vbl_nmi 4/10 — the shortfall is recorded in the evidence file itself, not hidden). All three canaries re-verified by conductor: nestest 8991/8991 byte-exact (the highest-risk one, since wiring NMI is CPU-visible), golden_frame_bg green, golden_frame_sprites green. RESULT: sprite_hit 11/11 pass; ppu_vbl_nmi 4/10 with 6 waived. The shortfall is architectural and was diagnosed against the real fetched ROMs, not guessed: cpu::exec::CountingBus samples nmi_line() once per whole bus op and NesBus ticks 3 PPU dots strictly after a register read completes, so nesdev's sub-CPU-cycle "read $2002 on the very clock the flag sets" race is unreachable; the one-clock-early case IS reachable and is implemented. Five of six failures share one signature (suppression boundary exactly one CPU cycle / 3 dots early), cross-checked against an independently reported matching symptom (forums.nesdev.org/viewtopic.php?t=17682) — one root cause, not six bugs. 10-even_odd_timing fails at a separate $2001-write-timing sub-test and was honestly NOT claimed to share that cause. CONDUCTOR MECHANICAL CHECK: build_report refuses a waiver naming a non-open ticket — verified by temporarily marking W1-05b done, which made local-gate.sh fail with "waiver for ppu_vbl_nmi/02-vbl_set_time names ticket W1-05b, which is not open". So the ticket could NOT be closed by waiving harder; per Brad's ruling the 6 waivers were repointed to a new W1-05c that owns the sub-cycle timing work, guarded by the 2,560,000-vector and 8991-line suites, with waiver expiry 2026-11-01 forcing it red if ignored. Two blockers the agent resolved beyond pre-flight: sprite_hit_tests' manifest protocol tag was wrong (that ROM generation predates blargg's $6000 runtime — uses a $00F8 RAM result byte, verified against the fetched readme.txt and source/runtime/validation.a), and sprite_overflow_tests almost certainly shares the same mistag, flagged in the manifest and TESTING.md for a future ticket rather than silently fixed.

2026-08-03 W1-06 done — frontend shell: winit+egui window (eframe 0.35.0, NO direct wgpu dep — confirmed exactly one `wgpu v29.0.4` in the whole lockfile via `cargo tree -d`/grep), CPU-blit rendering (`rf-renderer`'s new `palette`/`frame` modules resolve `PpuPixel::palette_index` to RGBA using a 64-entry NES 2C02 table sourced from TetaNES's `ntscpalette.pal`, this project's own cited proof-of-stack), pause/step-frame/step-scanline controls (`retroforge::stepper::EmuStepper`, windowless and unit-tested — 13 tests covering pause/resume transitions, step_frame advancing exactly one frame, step_scanline advancing exactly one scanline in the ordinary case, exactly 240 visible scanlines per steady-state frame boundary, and (advisor-caught, see below) a defensive per-step cycle budget), ROM open dialog (`rfd` 0.17.2, native picker + `rf_cart::Cartridge::load` console-agnostic validation before ever touching `rf-nes`), and FM-01 core-panic containment (`retroforge::core_thread`: real OS thread, `catch_unwind` + a custom panic hook that force-captures a backtrace regardless of `RUST_BACKTRACE` so the trace tail is deterministic in CI, structured `CoreCrashReport` sent over a channel, error dialog with message/location/trace-tail; injected-panic test written so it would fail if `catch_unwind` were removed — asserts the crash report actually arrives AND `handle.join().is_ok()`, not just that the process survived; re-ran that test in isolation with `--test-threads=1` to confirm it doesn't depend on another test having already installed the panic hook). API-drift traps hit and resolved by reading the pinned 0.35 source directly (not training data): `eframe::App::ui` now takes `&mut egui::Ui` (not `&Context`); `TopBottomPanel`/`SidePanel` no longer exist, replaced by unified `egui::Panel::top/bottom/left/right(id).show(ui, ...)`; `ui.close_menu()` renamed `ui.close()` — all three noted in `app.rs`'s module doc for the next agent. ADVISOR REVIEW CAUGHT TWO REAL BUGS BEFORE CLOSE (fixed, not just noted): (1) `step_scanline`/`step_frame`'s shared loop had NO iteration bound — it runs `Cpu::step` until a PPU boundary fires, which `rf-nes`'s public API never actually guarantees is reachable (a `JAM`/`KIL` opcode re-executes forever across repeated `Cpu::step` calls per that crate's own doc), and this loop runs *inside* the core thread's guarded closure, so an unbounded hang there would also block `CoreCommand::Shutdown` from ever being drained — worse than the failure FM-01 was written to contain. Fixed with a `CYCLE_BUDGET` (four NTSC frames' worth of master cycles) defensive cutoff on both loops, `tick_running` changed to report `false` (not a fresh frame) when the budget fires rather than the boundary, plus three new tests proving termination with a tiny test-only budget override (not just that the constant exists in source) — one of which caught its own test bug on first write (the initial budget chosen was large enough to accidentally satisfy the very first, short "boot artifact" frame boundary rather than proving anything, same trap as the earlier 240-scanline test; fixed by using a budget below even that boundary). (2) The crash dialog left `self.core` populated after `CoreEvent::Crashed`, so the Run/Step Frame/Step Scanline buttons stayed enabled and quietly sent into a channel nobody was reading anymore — fixed by clearing `self.core` the moment a crash is latched, which naturally disables the transport buttons until the user reopens a ROM (FM-01's own documented recovery path). workspace: 358 passing / 0 failed / 1 ignored (unchanged rf-state fixture-regenerator; was 330 at W1-05b close) — +28 net new (rf-renderer 1 -> 9, retroforge 0 -> 20; the crate's prior single `crate_is_wired` marker tests are the "1"/"0" baselines, not zero-vs-zero). CANARIES unperturbed: nes6502 2,560,000/2,560,000, nestest 8991/8991 byte-exact, `golden_frame_bg` green, `golden_frame_sprites` green. Full mechanical gate green: fmt/clippy(--all-targets, -D warnings)/test/arch/plan/traceability/evidence all exit 0. LICENCE FINDING, REPORTED NOT SILENTLY FIXED (per this ticket's own instruction): `cargo deny check licenses` FAILS with 21 rejections — `egui-winit`'s default features (`clipboard`→arboard→clipboard-win/error-code, Windows-only, BSL-1.0, 2 crates; `links`→webbrowser→url→idna→the icu_*/zerovec/yoke/litemap/writeable/tinystr/potential_utf family, Unicode-3.0, 18 crates) plus egui's bundled `epaint_default_fonts` (1 entry, `(MIT OR Apache-2.0) AND OFL-1.1 AND Ubuntu-font-1.0`) pull three permissive-but-not-yet-allowlisted licence families past NFR-011's `deny.toml`. 2026-08-05 W1-05c BLOCKED at 9/10 (not done — honest status, see below) — sub-cycle VBL/NMI suppression timing. `ppu_vbl_nmi` 4/10 → **9/10**: 02-vbl_set_time, 05-nmi_timing, 06-suppression, 07-nmi_on_timing, 08-nmi_off_timing all now pass; only 10-even_odd_timing remains. THE LEADING HYPOTHESIS WAS REFUTED, NOT ADOPTED: the pre-flight ranked "register access sequenced before the cycle's 3 dots" as most likely, and the `02-vbl_set_time`-alone discriminator showed a uniform `CpuBus::read` ordering swap is **self-cancelling** on that ROM — its own `sync_vbl` convergence reads shift by exactly as much as the deliberate test read. Changing all six ROMs at once would have hidden that. TWO SEPARABLE MECHANISMS were actually needed, and the conductor's mutation testing proves they are separable rather than one change described two ways: (1) `Ppu::read_status` now answers more rows of nesdev's "VBL Flag Timing" table — the same-clock (241,1) case plus its (261,1) clear-side mirror; reverting this arm alone breaks 02/03/05/06/07/08. (2) `NesBus::nmi_level_latch`, a one-PPU-**dot**-delayed NMI-level snapshot, because `CountingBus` samples after the whole bus op and a VBlank set can land in a cycle that is not a `$2002` access at all, so `read_status` never gets to intervene; reverting this alone breaks only 05/06/07/08. A whole-cycle-wide latch was tried first and measured exactly 2 rows too wide. THE (261,1) ARM IS NOT FROM THE WIKI (which describes no read-vs-clear race) and says so: it is justified against `03-vbl_clear_time`'s own table per MASTER_PROMPT's "the test ROM wins", after fixing only the set side passed 02 and **broke a previously-passing 03**. GUARDRAIL PREDICTION HELD: the pre-flight argued nestest and the vector suite are structurally immune because `trace.rs`'s `ppu_columns()` derives the PPU columns arithmetically from `master_cycle` and the vector suite runs on a PPU-free mock — both unchanged, and `master_cycle` still advances exactly once per bus op (only the dot loop was split). NO EXPECTATION WAS WEAKENED: `blargg_roms`' `must_pass` flags went false→true for five ROMs (tightening the suite), and `ppu/tests/nmi.rs` is purely additive — verified by the conductor, since re-baselining a red PPU suite was this ticket's single largest risk. BLOCKED, NOT DONE: acceptance criterion 1 requires 10/10 and the deletion of every waiver naming this ticket, so `done` would be a false claim. W1-05b's "one shared root cause across all six" hypothesis is now PARTLY REFUTED and `waivers.toml`'s header was corrected rather than left to rot — it held for the five read-side ROMs, not for 10-even_odd_timing, which needs a `$2001` write to land EARLIER while every mechanism here moves effects LATER. Five waivers DELETED (not re-dated); the one remaining is rewritten with the refuting evidence and a "do not retry by globally re-sequencing register access — measured not to work" note. THIS BLOCKS NOTHING IMMEDIATELY: ROADMAP's Phase 1 exit criteria do not mention `ppu_vbl_nmi`; "all pass headless" is a **Phase 2** exit criterion, so the real deadline is Phase 2's exit or the 2026-11-01 waiver expiry. A scope-split decision (amend criterion 1 to the five read-side ROMs and open a successor) is recorded in the ticket notes for Brad — deliberately not taken unilaterally, since amending acceptance to match what was achieved is goalpost-moving.

## PHASE 1 EXIT GATE — 2026-08-05 — **NOT PASSED** (criteria → evidence)

Run per PLAYBOOK's wave-gate loop (D-002), iteration 1 of a cap of 3. Conductor did inventory + verify; a **challenger** agent that implemented none of Phase 1 (maker ≠ verifier) independently re-derived the criteria from ROADMAP/TESTING and attacked the evidence. The challenger reproduced all five conductor-found gaps without being shown them, and added four more. This is the first Phase 1 gate section ever recorded — PLAYBOOK requires one at every phase gate, and the challenger's finding #2 is that none existed; every entry since the 2026-07-15 Phase 0→1 design gate has been a per-ticket close note.

**Inventory:** 11 tickets (W1-01a/b, W1-02, W1-03, W1-04a/b, W1-05a/b/c, W1-06, W1-07) — 10 `done`, W1-05c `blocked` at 9/10. 10 distinct FR/NFR: FR-CORE-002/003/020/021/022/023, FR-DBG-003/004, FR-FE-003, FR-STATE-006. Stories E1-S1, E1-S2, E1-S4, E5-S1.

**THE TWO SOURCE DOCS DISAGREE ABOUT WHAT PHASE 1 REQUIRES** — reported, not silently resolved by picking the convenient one. ROADMAP lists 4 bullets; TESTING §8's Phase-1 row lists 5 items, adding `instr_test-v5 pass` and `roundtrip ... green`. ROADMAP places `instr_test-v5` under **Phase 2**. Both readings fail, so the verdict is unchanged either way — but a project with two authoritative docs specifying different gates can "pass" against whichever is convenient, which is the actual defect.

| # | Criterion | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | nes6502 vectors 100% (official + illegal) | **MET** | re-ran independently: `cargo test -p rf-nes vectors -- --nocapture` → 151 opcodes/1,510,000 cases + 105 opcodes/1,050,000 cases, 0 failed — exit 0 |
| 2 | nestest byte-exact | **MET** | re-ran independently: `cargo test -p rf-nes -- --nocapture nestest` → 8991 compared, 8991 matched, 0 mismatched — exit 0 |
| 3 | Two NROM homebrew titles boot (Alter Ego + neslib fixture) | **CONTRADICTED** | Nothing loads or boots either title — `grep -rn "alter_ego\|neslib" crates/` → 0 hits outside comments. `roms/nes/alter-ego/alter_ego.zip` is fetched but never unzipped or executed. Owned by **W2-10 + W2-11, both Phase 2, both `todo`** |
| 4 | Frame stepping works | **UNVERIFIABLE** | Headless step-frame/step-scanline tests pass (within the 409). Visual confirmation in a real window remains undone — same sandbox limit (no screen-recording/assistive-access grant) recorded for W1-06 and W1-07; the challenger has it too and did not re-confirm |
| 5 | Determinism: 2× **10k**-frame runs, identical per-frame **state** hashes | **CONTRADICTED, two axes** | (a) SCALE: the only double-run test uses `build_script(24, None)` — 24 frames, >400× short; no 10k-frame test exists. (b) COVERAGE: the hash excludes PPU internals and APU by design (rf-nes implements no `EmulatorCore::save_state`), so it **cannot see a PPU/APU divergence at any frame count** — raising the count alone does not close this. Full state is owed by **W2-04** (Phase 2, `todo`) |

Its only `.rs` appearances are JSON-shape unit tests using the string as a literal. Absent from `local_gate_evidence.rs` (4 suites) and from `local-gate.json`. TESTING §4 tags it Tier **A ("every PR")** — that label is also false; **no ticket owns it** |
| 7 | TESTING §8 only: roundtrip suite green | **CONTRADICTED** | No NES state-roundtrip suite exists; FR-STATE-002 is owned entirely by **W2-04** (`todo`). W0-05's roundtrip tests cover the generic TLV container with synthetic payloads, not NES machine state |
| 8 | Full seven-command gate | **MET** | re-ran independently by both conductor and challenger: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**409 passed / 0 failed / 1 ignored**) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0 (101/101, zero dangling) · evidence 0 (nes6502 2,560,000/2,560,000 · nestest 8991/8991 · sprite_hit 11/11 · ppu_vbl_nmi 9/10) |
| 9 | Does W1-05c (blocked, 9/10) block Phase 1? | **No** | Re-derived independently from ROADMAP by the challenger: Phase 1's bullets never mention `ppu_vbl_nmi`; it appears only under **Phase 2**'s exit criteria. TESTING §8's Phase-1 row omits it too. Holds under both readings |

**Mechanisms attacked, not just read.** The challenger checked out `4b97cf1` (code landed, evidence not yet regenerated) into a disposable worktree and confirmed `validate-evidence.mjs` genuinely fails there with 3 STALE errors — the staleness gate works, rather than merely being claimed to. It also found that **a skipped ROM suite is indistinguishable from a passing one in default `cargo test` output** (`RF_NES6502_VECTORS=/nonexistent` still prints `test ... ok`; the SKIP diagnostic needs `--nocapture`). That is mitigated by design — the evidence file is the real gate for those suites, per TESTING §4 — but it means the workspace pass count, read alone, proves nothing about whether ROM-backed suites ran.

**Finding with the longest reach: R-05's phase gate is not enforced anywhere in code.** RISKS.md R-05 names its mitigation verbatim as "plan.json phase gates — no Phase-N ticket starts before N-1 exit criteria pass", and PLAYBOOK states the same rule. But `validate-plan.mjs`'s claimable computation is purely `depends_on`-derived plus a manual `hold` flag. Live proof from this very session: it lists W2-01a, W2-02, W2-06…W2-11, **W3-01 (Phase 3), and W4-01/02/06a/07/08 (Phase 4)** as "claimable now" while Phase 1 has not exited. The control is honour-system only, which is precisely how a phase gate gets skipped without anyone deciding to skip it.

**Also found:** only 4 of ~24 fetched NES test-ROM suites are executed by any code path (nes6502, nestest, sprite_hit_tests, ppu_vbl_nmi) — most of the rest are legitimately Phase 2+ scope, but `instr_test-v5` carries a Phase 1 claim. And DESIGN_REVIEW's G-34 records this exact ROADMAP/TESTING drift as already "fixed" — that was a wording alignment only; the implementation gap was never closed, so the conflict has looked resolved on paper since July while staying open in fact.

**BOTTOM LINE: Phase 1 does not exit.** Two of four ROADMAP criteria are solidly met on independent re-run; two are contradicted, and both contradicted ones depend on Phase 2 tickets (W2-04, W2-10, W2-11) that have not been started. **No amount of Phase 1 work can close them** — this needs a board decision from Brad, recorded as HANDOFF notes on W1-07, W2-04, W2-10 and W2-11. Per the wave-gate cap, this is iteration 1; the gap set is now recorded, so iteration 2 should only re-check flagged rows, never re-run the phase.

2026-08-05 PHASE 1 GATE — ITERATION 2, board+doc correction pass (Brad approved "do recommended"). Acting on iteration 1's verdict, which found two Phase 1 exit criteria unsatisfiable by any Phase 1 work: satisfying them as written required **31 of Phase 2's 71 points first** (W2-01a→W2-01b→W2-04 for save-state, plus W2-10+W2-11 for the fixtures), inverting the phase order the gate exists to enforce (R-05). **ROADMAP amended** (now explicitly AUTHORITATIVE for phase exits): Phase 1 keeps nes6502 100%, nestest byte-exact, frame stepping, and 2×10k-frame determinism over **reachable** state; "two NROM homebrew titles boot" and full-machine-state determinism moved DOWN to Phase 2. That loses no coverage — Phase 2 already required both in stronger form ("RF-Scroller plays start-to-finish and Alter Ego plays by hand"; "save-state roundtrip + replay determinism green"). The 10k-frame *scale* was kept, since that is the half that catches drift a short run hides; only the *state scope* is staged, because a hash cannot cover PPU/APU before the core can serialize them. **TESTING §8 no longer restates the criteria** — it now declares ROADMAP authoritative and summarizes, which removes the duplication that let the two drift (DESIGN_REVIEW G-34 "fixed" that drift in wording only, in July, and it reopened). **TESTING §4 now marks `instr_test-v5`, `cpu_timing_test6`, `instr_timing`, `branch_timing_tests` and `cpu_interrupts_v2` as "NOT WIRED (W2-12)"** — they were labelled Tier A ("every PR") while executed by nothing; a tier label that overstates coverage is worse than an honest gap. THREE TICKETS CREATED: **W1-08** (10k-frame double-run, 3pts, Phase 1 — closes the scale half only; the coverage half is explicitly fenced to W2-04), **W1-05d** (odd-frame `$2001` write timing, 3pts, Phase 1 — inherits W1-05c's remaining waiver and its "do not retry by globally re-sequencing register access, measured not to work" warning), **W2-12** (wire the unexecuted Tier-A CPU suites, 5pts, Phase 2). **W1-05c CLOSED done** under Brad's option-B scope split: its criterion 1 is amended to the five read-side ROMs, which is exactly what the evidence supports — the ticket bundled two defects that the work itself proved independent, so leaving it "not done" would have mis-described a spec error as a delivery failure. The `[a-c]` ticket-id suffix class was widened to `[a-z]` in BOTH `crates/rf-harness/src/accuracy.rs` and `scripts/validate-plan.mjs` (two independent copies of the same pattern — the second was found only by running the validator) so `W1-05d` validates; `ticket_id_pattern_accepts_documented_shapes` was updated WITH a doc comment stating why the expectation changed and keeping every case the pattern actually protects against. Board: 67→**70 tickets, 379→390 pts**, 18 done. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (409 passed / 0 failed / 1 ignored) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0 · evidence 0 · `cargo deny check licenses` "licenses ok". **Phase 1 still does NOT exit** — W1-08 and the frame-stepping visual check remain; the difference is that both are now closable within Phase 1, which was the point of the correction.

2026-08-06 W1-08 done — determinism at scale: 10k-frame double-run. Closes the SCALE half of ROADMAP's amended Phase 1 exit criterion ("2× 10k-frame runs, identical per-frame hashes over reachable state"); W1-07 built the mechanism but ran 24 frames, over 400× short. MEASURED, NOT GUESSED (conductor pre-flight, by temporarily raising the existing test's frame count, timing, restoring, and verifying byte-identical by checksum): debug ~4.7ms/frame ⇒ 2×10k ≈ 93s, which would nearly triple the ~45s `cargo test --workspace`; release is 10.5× faster ⇒ ~9s. So both new tests are `#[ignore]`d and invoked in **release** from a dedicated CI step and from `scripts/local-gate.sh`. That measurement also forced amending an acceptance clause written the day before, which had assumed a debug framing and mandated an evidence row — corrected in the claim commit rather than discovered mid-ticket. EVIDENCE-ROW DECISION: **against**, deliberately, with the reasoning written into `docs/TESTING.md` §6 rather than only into a ticket note. The evidence mechanism exists for suites CI **cannot run at all** (gitignored fetched ROM corpora, where a skip is indistinguishable from a pass); this fixture is a synthetic in-code NROM — nothing to fetch, nothing to skip, nothing to fake — and at ~9s it runs directly, in full, in CI on every PR, which is a *strictly stronger* guarantee than a staleness-triggered row that only re-fires when `coveredPaths` changes. Adding one would have cost `rf-harness` a dependency on the `retroforge` lib (pulling eframe/wgpu into the harness build) for no coverage gain. THE MOST VALUABLE RESULT IS A WEAKNESS THE IMPLEMENTING AGENT FOUND AND REPORTED UNPROMPTED: it ran a third, unrequested mutation — replacing `state_hash()` with a constant — and found that criterion 1's test **passes vacuously on its own** (two runs of a constant are trivially identical). The conductor reproduced this exactly: the divergence test FAILS under that mutation while the identical-runs test PASSES. So criterion 1 derives its force only from being paired with the divergence test over the same `run_script`/`state_hash` path, and that coupling is now documented permanently in the test's own doc comment so a future edit cannot silently gut it. Conductor gate re-run INDEPENDENTLY (RF-L-08): fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**409 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored**; wall 41.4s vs ~45s before — no regression, the whole point of the `#[ignore]`) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0 · evidence 0; the two 10k tests re-run in release at 9.34s, matching the pre-flight extrapolation. TWO CONDUCTOR RULINGS: (1) the two "scope widenings" the agent flagged were the conductor's own defect — acceptance criterion 2 names `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `scripts/local-gate.sh` explicitly while `write_scope` listed neither, a self-blocking write_scope (the board's most common defect, six instances now); fixed by adding the two EXACT paths, never a glob. The agent handled it correctly: flagged both, appended a HANDOFF note before editing, touched nothing else. (2) the determinism check was moved to run **before** local-gate.sh's four ROM-directory pre-checks — because both tests are `#[ignore]`d, that script is the only local path that runs them, and the suite needs no ROMs at all, so ordering it behind the ROM checks meant a contributor without a fetched `roms/` could never run it; ~9s spent before a missing-ROM run reports its missing directory is the cheaper mistake. **PHASE 1 IS NOW ONE HUMAN CHECK FROM EXIT**: nes6502 100%, nestest byte-exact, and 10k determinism are all met and independently verified; only "frame stepping works" remains, and it needs a desktop session this sandbox cannot provide.

2026-08-06 W2-13 done — open a zipped ROM (requested directly by Brad, out of phase order, recorded plainly rather than renumbered: the phase gate stops scope drift in unattended builds, it does not veto the product owner). Opening a `.zip` now resolves the ROM inside it. PLACEMENT RULING — this lives in `crates/retroforge` ONLY, **not** `rf-cart`, after the conductor was talked out of the wrong answer: the tempting case was that `rf-cart` owns cartridge identity and RA convention hashes the ROM *inside* an archive, with W2-11 (Alter Ego ships as `alter_ego.zip`) and W2-07 as future consumers. Two things kill it — `rf-nes` depends on `rf-cart`, so an unconditional zip dep drags flate2/zlib-rs and **zopfli, a compressor**, into the NES core, and the default-off-feature mitigation is itself the tell that the placement fights the architecture; and this project already recorded the same reasoning in `crate::system`'s "Mapper scope" note, deferring an abstraction until a second consumer exists to inform its shape. W2-11 can unzip in the fetch script; promoting a tested 40-line function later is cheap, unwinding a core-crate charter expansion is not. PRE-FLIGHT compile-probed `zip 8.6.0` in a scratch crate rather than reading docs, and **pre-cleared licences** — all 16 transitive crates are MIT/Apache-2.0/Zlib/Unlicense/0BSD, every one already on `deny.toml`'s allowlist, so **no new per-crate exception was needed** (W1-06 lost a round-trip to exactly this class of surprise); `cargo deny check licenses` re-run in the real tree after `Cargo.lock` moved → "licenses ok". DESIGN: entries are identified by **sniffing bytes through `rf_cart::Cartridge::load`, never by extension** — the happy-path test names its entry `some_game_v1.0 (PD).bin` so an extension-matching implementation fails it; a zip with no ROM, or with two, is refused with a diagnostic naming what was found, never a silent pick, since zip entry order is an artifact of how the archive was written. UNTRUSTED-INPUT POSTURE matches `rf-state`'s zstd precedent: caps bound bytes **actually read** (`Read::take`), never the central directory's attacker-controlled `size()`, plus a cumulative cap because a per-entry cap bounds nothing across thousands of entries; nested archives are skipped rather than recursed; nothing is ever written to disk — which is *why* path traversal is a non-issue, stated in the module doc so a future edit adding real extraction must re-open the question. `zip` was added to the **first** dialog filter alongside `nes`, not a separate one, since that is the default selection — a loader that can read archives the picker cannot select is a feature nobody reaches. MUTATION: making the zip branch return the raw archive bytes failed 7 of 8 new tests including the anti-vacuity `zip_with_no_rom_is_refused`; restored byte-identical. Implemented by the conductor directly rather than delegated (3 files, ~40 lines — briefing costs more than doing; departure from the standing subagent instruction recorded, not silently taken). Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**417 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored**, was 409) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0 · evidence 0 · licences ok. UNVERIFIED, not claimed: the native picker actually showing/accepting a `.zip` needs a desktop session — fold into the same manual pass as Phase 1's outstanding frame-stepping check.

2026-08-06 W2-14 done — FIX: a stepped frame never reached the UI. Reported by Brad from the first real desktop session ("run works and can play game, the step frame and scanline seem to pause and do nothing"). Diagnosed before touching code, per the bug-fix discipline. **ROOT CAUSE 1 (primary):** `core_thread.rs` sent `CoreEvent::Frame` from exactly one place — inside `if stepper.tick_running_with_input(...)`, which returns false whenever Paused. So Step Frame/Step Scanline advanced the machine, rendered into the sink, force-paused, and then **silently discarded the frame**. The emulator really was stepping; the user simply never saw it. **ROOT CAUSE 2 (compounding):** `app.rs`'s `pump_core_events` only requested a repaint while `self.running`, so even once the frame was sent it would arrive milliseconds *after* the click's own repaint completed and sit unconsumed until an unrelated event woke the UI — fixing only root cause 1 would have produced an intermittent "works if you jiggle the mouse" bug. Both fixed together: a `stepped` flag on the core thread, and an `awaiting_stepped_frame` flag that keeps the UI repainting until the frame lands. **NOT A BUG, recorded so it isn't "fixed" later:** `FrameBuffer` has no clear step, so a scanline step paints the new line over the previous frame — correct debugger behaviour, and why a single scanline step is nearly imperceptible by design. **WHY IT SHIPPED, worth more than the fix:** `EmuStepper`'s state machine had 13 tests and the FM-01 panic path was covered, but *nothing tested the seam between them* — that a `CoreCommand` produces a `CoreEvent::Frame`. W1-06's own notes predicted this exactly, warning "do not let it is a GUI ticket become a reason for zero tests", then tested the state machine and the panic path while leaving the command→frame wiring to a manual pass that could not run in a sandbox; the W1-07 conductor fix touched this very arm and its mutation proved the *latch* worked, not that a frame was *delivered*. Lesson recorded: when a manual checklist is known to be unrunnable, the mechanically-testable seam beside it deserves MORE coverage, not the same amount. Three new tests close it, including the negative case. MUTATION-TESTED BOTH DIRECTIONS: reverting to `if ran` reproduces the reported bug (both seam tests time out); widening to `if true` fails the paused-core test, proving the fix doesn't over-correct into busy-spinning. Gate: all seven exit 0, **420 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored** (was 417). **FIELD VERIFICATION (Brad, same session)** — the evidence W1-06's checklist has awaited since 2026-08-03: window opens, paints live frames, ROM loads, game is PLAYABLE, keyboard input drives it; and separately, a "mapper 2 is not implemented" message reaching the status line confirmed the native picker, `rf-cart` parsing, and FR-CORE-013's diagnostic path all work end to end. W1-06 criteria 1 and 3 and W1-07's live-input path are DISCHARGED. Outstanding: one look to confirm stepping is now visibly working, since this fix landed after the report.

2026-08-06 W2-15 done — transport position readout (`frame N · scanline M` in the status bar). Raised by Brad immediately after W2-14: "what does step scanline do? because i dont see that with the rom" — a fair question with a real answer. Step Scanline advances 1/240th of a frame, `rf_renderer::FrameBuffer` deliberately has no clear step so the new line paints over the previous frame, and nothing in the UI reported position. The instrument was correct; its display had never been built, which made a working control indistinguishable from a dead one. PRE-FLIGHT TOOK THE CHEAP PATH, and it mattered: the obvious implementation exposes the PPU's scanline, but `rf-nes` has no public accessor, and `crates/rf-nes/src/{ppu,system}` sit in `validate-evidence.mjs`'s `coveredPaths` — so a 2-point status-bar ticket would have invalidated `docs/evidence/local-gate.json` and forced a full `local-gate.sh` regeneration (2.56M vectors + 8991 trace lines + 21 ROMs + the 10k determinism run) merely to go green. Instead `rf_core_api::CoreSink::video_scanline` **already carries `y`**, and `stepper.rs` already wrapped the sink to count scanlines — widening that wrapper gave the readout with **zero `rf-nes` change and zero evidence churn**, confirmed by `validate-evidence` still reporting the unchanged `retroforge_commit=9af46c2`. Position rides on `FrameMsg` itself rather than a new event, since W2-14 made every advance path send exactly one frame. DOCUMENTED LIMITATION, not papered over: `video_scanline` fires only for the 240 visible lines, so the readout does not tick through vblank (240-260) — which matches what `step_scanline` actually does, since it runs until a *visible* line completes and therefore advances through the rest of vblank into line 0 on a single press. That quirk is `step_scanline`'s and predates this ticket. MUTATION: dropping the position propagation from `run_until_next_frame` — tracking only the single-step path — fails `running_keeps_the_reported_scanline_updating`; that is the regression worth guarding, because a readout frozen during Run would survive a casual eyeball test while single-stepping still looked right. A TEST WAS NAIVE, NOT THE CODE: the first draft asserted after one `tick_running` and failed, because a fresh `EmuStepper` starts mid pre-render and its first `frame_count` tick is the documented boot artifact with zero visible lines drawn — the same trap this module's 240-scanline test and W1-06's cycle-budget test already record. Reasoning kept in the test's doc comment so nobody "fixes" the readout to invent a 0. Gate: all seven exit 0, **423 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored** (was 420).
