2026-08-06 W2-02 done — Mapper trait + MMC1(1), UxROM(2), CNROM(3). The trait lands per `docs/design/EMULATION_CORES.md` §2.4, NROM is extracted into it from its inline home in `system/mod.rs`, and the three new mappers implement PRG banking, CHR banking and MMC1's mirroring control. **Built to what these mappers exercise, not to the full §2.4 signature:** `ppu_a12`/`irq_pending` are MMC3's (**W2-03**) and `state_chunk` is the MAPR save-state chunk (**W2-04**) — left out rather than guessed at, following W1-02's precedent of declining to build this trait when only NROM existed because it would have been unvalidated scaffolding. CHR banking uses a materialize/push design (the mapper exposes its current 8 KiB window; the bus pushes it into the PPU's flat buffer) because §2.4's per-access PPU routing would have needed files outside scope; CHR-RAM banking is a documented narrow gap. **NO THIRD-PARTY ORACLE EXISTS**, established by evidence not assumption: Holy Diver Batman is unobtainable (W0-03's manifest note, plus the conductor walking the pinned aggregator's full recursive tree — 1448 entries, 290 ROMs, 64 suites, no such directory), and that aggregator holds nothing for UxROM or CNROM. Verification is therefore hand-assembled synthetic ROMs, the technique W1-07/W1-08 established, each written so a no-op mapper that ignores its registers is *caught*. **CONDUCTOR FIX — without it this ticket shipped dead code:** `system/cartridge.rs`'s gate still read `if header.mapper != 0`, rejecting every real MMC1/UxROM/CNROM file *before* `NesBus::new`'s new dispatch could run, so Brad's mapper-2 ROM would still have failed. The implementing agent found it, correctly declined to work around it (the file was outside its scope, and both workarounds it weighed would have created a hidden vacuity hole), and reported it blocked-with-evidence — that restraint is why this was a clean 3-line fix rather than an unpicking job. Fixed by adding the exact path plus an explicit `EMULATED_MAPPERS = [0,1,2,3]`, deliberately kept separate from rf-cart's `SUPPORTED_MAPPERS`, which answers the different question of which headers it can *parse* (it includes MMC3 for identification). That was the **eighth** self-blocking write_scope on this board; the **ninth** was the conductor's own — closing the agent's reported zero-coverage hole needed `system/tests/`, which write_scope omitted beside `system/mod.rs`. Recorded plainly: the pattern is not careless implementers, it is that write_scope is authored before anyone knows which files the work touches, and omitting a unit's own tests is its most repeatable instance. **Two new tests** close the hole through the real `NesRom::from_ines_bytes` entry point — the one `EmuStepper` and the app use — rather than a test-only constructor, exercising every dispatch arm. **A pre-existing test's premise expired and was retargeted, not deleted:** `unimplemented_mapper_is_rejected_even_though_rf_cart_parses_its_header` used *mapper 1* as its example ("rf-nes implements only mapper 0 in this ticket" — true at W1-02, false now), so it moved to mapper 4 with a comment saying why and to retarget it again when MMC3 lands. That distinction is what the re-baseline rule turns on: a premise that expired for a documented reason, versus an expectation edited to match whatever the code now does. MUTATIONS, both re-run independently by the conductor: reverting the gate to `!= 0` fails the production-path test (the defect reproduced mechanically); neutering `UxRom::cpu_write` fails 3 tests including the real-`Cpu`-driven integration fixture. **Canaries unregressed, from freshly regenerated evidence at `a608219` on a clean tree:** nes6502 2,560,000/2,560,000 · nestest 8991/8991 byte-exact, `first_divergence: null` · golden_frame_bg · golden_frame_sprites · sprite_hit_tests 11/11 · ppu_vbl_nmi 9/10 (`10-even_odd_timing` waived to W1-05d, untouched). Gate: all seven exit 0, **456 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored** (was 423), `cargo deny check licenses` "licenses ok".

2026-08-06 W2-16 done — two misleading load diagnostics, both reported by Brad from a real session, both cases of the app lying about why it refused a file. (1) The unimplemented-mapper message still read "(NROM/mapper 0 only)" — false the moment W2-02 landed MMC1/UxROM/CNROM the same day. Now derived from `EMULATED_MAPPERS` instead of restated in prose, so it cannot drift again. (2) THE BIGGER ONE WAS THE CONDUCTOR'S OWN: W2-13's zip resolver sniffed entries through `Cartridge::load` and kept only `Ok(Nes)`, **discarding the error** — so an archive whose single entry is a real NES image with an unsupported mapper fell through to the generic "no recognizable ROM". The precise diagnostic had already been generated and was thrown away one layer up, which reads to a user as "your file is junk" when the truth is "this emulator doesn't support that mapper yet". DIAGNOSED FROM THE ACTUAL ARTIFACT, not guessed: `Marble Madness (USA).zip` holds one entry that is a valid **NES 2.0** image (flags7 & 0x0C == 0x08) declaring **mapper 7 (AxROM)** — flags6=0x70, PRG 8×16K, CHR-RAM, 131088 bytes exactly matching its header. rf-cart was correct throughout. The message now reads *"the zip archive's only NES image, 'Marble Madness (USA).nes', could not be loaded: unsupported mapper: 7 (AxROM)"*, **verified by running the real file through the real code path**, not only the synthetic fixture. NEITHER CHANGE ADDS MAPPER SUPPORT, and that is deliberate: mapper 7 is outside FR-CORE-025's planned set (0/1/2/3/4) and mapper 4 is W2-03. The generic no-ROM path is separately tested so the specific and generic cases stay distinguishable — without that, "always report the specific reason" could quietly collapse them. NFR-006 upheld: tests use a synthetic image declaring mapper 7, `git check-ignore` confirms the supplied ROM is covered by `/roms/`, and no copyrighted title appears in engine code. Gate: all seven exit 0, **458 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored** (was 456); evidence regenerated on a clean tree at `194b3b0` — nes6502 2,560,000/2,560,000, nestest 8991/8991, sprite_hit 11/11, ppu_vbl_nmi 9/10.

2026-08-06 W2-03 done — MMC3 + A12 IRQ filtering. **`mmc3_test_2` 6/6** (1-clocking, 2-details, 3-A12_clocking, 4-scanline_timing, 5-MMC3, 6-MMC3_alt). Mapper coverage goes ~63% → **~91.5%** of the licensed NA library, completing FR-CORE-025's set. Implements bank-select, PRG/CHR modes incl. the A12-inversion bit, mirroring, PRG-RAM protect, and the four IRQ registers; adds A12 rising-edge detection filtered on a ≥3-CPU-cycle low period (nesdev — exactly 9 dots in this crate's lock-step architecture, cross-checked against Mesen2's `IsA12RisingEdge`); wires `irq_line` through the bus, finally replacing the trait default whose comment read *"A later ticket overrides this once a real IRQ source exists"*; and adds `4` to `EMULATED_MAPPERS` alongside its dispatch arm, deliberately not repeating W2-02's dead-code trap. **ZERO-REGRESSION PROOF IS THE FOUR PPU-TIMING-SENSITIVE CANARIES, not the two insensitive ones:** `ppu/sprites.rs` was substantially reworked — collapsing the eight sprite-fetch slots into a single instant at dot 257 put the first real A12 edge 3–4 dots early and failed `4-scanline_timing`, fixed by modelling hardware's actual per-dot cadence (8 slots × 8 dots, "garbage NT, garbage NT, PT-lo, PT-hi") — so `golden_frame_bg`, `golden_frame_sprites`, `sprite_hit_tests` 11/11 and `ppu_vbl_nmi` 9/10 all holding at their exact prior rates is what proves it, while nestest and the vector suite are structurally insensitive to PPU-internal change. The scanline IRQ is **analytic, not a recorded golden** (W1-04b's law): latch 5 must fire on scanline 4, with the value chosen so plausible off-by-ones land on 3 or 5 rather than coincidentally on 4. **THE IMPLEMENTING AGENT CORRECTED THIS TICKET'S BRIEF AND WAS RIGHT** — worth recording as the opposite of what a green-report culture produces. The brief asserted disabling the A12 filter would fail `3-A12_clocking`; it does **not**, re-verified independently by the conductor, because that sub-ROM disables rendering and clocks A12 only via widely spaced manual `$2006`/`$2007` access, never producing a short low period. The mutation *is* caught — by `2-details` (code `$08`) and `4-scanline_timing` (code `$04`), which exercise real per-tile rendering. The filter is genuinely tested; the brief named the wrong sub-ROM. Second mutation also re-run independently: forcing `irq_line()` false fails both analytic IRQ tests, proving delivery is wired rather than merely computed inside the mapper. **CONDUCTOR FIX:** the agent retired a test whose premise had expired (with MMC3 landed, `EMULATED_MAPPERS` and rf-cart's `SUPPORTED_MAPPERS` are now identical, so `UnimplementedMapper` is unreachable from parsing) and flagged that this left the variant with zero coverage. That mattered more than it looked — the variant's *message* is what a user reads when a ROM is refused, and W2-16 had just fixed it with no test of its own; added one constructing the variant directly and asserting the message names both the offending mapper and every emulated one, so it also fails if a hard-coded list is reintroduced. A stale doc reference to the removed test was fixed too. **KNOWN GAP, reported not hidden:** `mmc3_irq_tests` is now manifest-wired (6 artifacts, same already-pinned aggregator commit) and fetches/hash-verifies clean, but is not asserted in-crate — it uses an older devcart result protocol (zero-page result byte + infinite print loop) rather than blargg's `$6000`/DE-B0-61 signature the harness expects. Not an emulator defect; MMC3 is covered 6/6 by `mmc3_test_2`. Wiring it needs a small harness variant — a natural fit for W2-12. Gate: all seven exit 0, **481 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored** (was 458); evidence regenerated on a clean tree at `96d8262`.

2026-08-06 W2-17 done — **AxROM (mapper 7)**; Marble Madness runs. A whole 32 KiB PRG window at `$8000-$FFFF` with **no fixed bank** (the difference from UxROM most likely to be got wrong by analogy — it has its own test asserting *both* halves follow the bank), plus single-screen mirroring from register bit 4. Requested by Brad after a real mapper-7 title was refused. **Small only because W2-02 already paid the cost:** `Mirroring::OneScreenLower`/`OneScreenUpper` come from no iNES header field and exist solely because MMC1 needed runtime mirroring control; they were already implemented *and* tested in `ppu/mem.rs`, so this mapper needed **no PPU change at all** — unlike CNROM (which forced CHR routing) or MMC3 (which forced A12 detection and a sprite-fetch rework). **THE TWO-LIST TRAP WAS CAUGHT THIS TIME RATHER THAN SHIPPED:** a mapper is reachable only if *both* rf-cart's `SUPPORTED_MAPPERS` (which gates first) and rf-nes's `EMULATED_MAPPERS` contain it — W2-02 shipped three mappers dead because only one moved. Both move here, and the mutation proves the requirement is genuinely tested: reverting rf-cart's list alone fails `every_emulated_mapper_loads_through_the_production_path` with the exact user-visible message. **SRS AMENDED, flagged not slipped in:** FR-CORE-025 gains AxROM(7) (coverage ≈91.5% → ≈93%) in the *same commit* as the citing ticket, per `validate-traceability`'s F2 ordering law — and an SRS amendment is a bigger act than amending a ticket's acceptance, so it is recorded as such. **FIELD-EQUIVALENT VERIFICATION against the real artifact**, not only synthetic fixtures: `Marble Madness (USA).zip` resolves out of the archive (131,088 bytes), loads into `EmuStepper`, and runs 120 frames rendering real output — it boots and draws, not merely parses. No ROM bytes entered git (`/roms/` gitignored, NFR-006). **A THIRD TEST EXPIRED THE SAME WAY, and the pattern is now named:** *"the currently unsupported mapper" is a premise that dies every time a mapper lands.* W2-02 retargeted one off mapper 1, W2-03 retired one off mapper 4, and this broke W2-16's off mapper 7. Retargeted to **mapper 5 (MMC5)**, which `EMULATION_CORES.md` §2.4 lists as *explicitly deferred* alongside MMC2/4 — out of scope by design rather than merely not-yet-done, while rf-cart still *names* it, which is what that test needs. Should not require a fourth lap. Gate: all seven exit 0, **487 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored** (was 481), `cargo deny` "licenses ok"; evidence regenerated on a clean tree.

2026-08-06 W2-18 done — **frame pacing**; the emulator now runs at NTSC speed. Raised by Brad ("what about speed do we keep the global timers accurate"), and the honest answer was that two different things had diverged: **emulated** timing was already accurate and proven (nestest 8991/8991 byte-exact, nes6502 2,560,000/2,560,000, `mmc3_test_2` 6/6 incl. the A12 filter), while **wall-clock pacing did not exist at all** — the core thread emulated a frame, sent it, and immediately began the next; the loop's only sleep was the *paused* idle path. Measured on Alter Ego: **113 fps debug (1.9× real speed** — matching Brad's "playable but quick" report**) and 1132 fps release (18.8×**, unusable**)**. After: **60.0 fps debug, 60.3 fps release — 1.00× NTSC both**, measured through the real core thread rather than the unit. **PACING WAS ASSUMED BY FOUR DESIGN DOCS AND IMPLEMENTED IN NONE:** FM-07 throttles a plugin to "protect frame pacing", PLUGINS.md repeats it, RENDERER.md prefers Fifo/vsync, and **FR-ENH-008 literally defines loading-skip as "disabling frame pacing without altering simulation"** — you cannot disable what was never built. Same class as R-05's phase gate: a control living in prose only. **INTERIM BY DESIGN, and the module says so:** the documented end-state is an audio-driven clock (rf-audio's cpal ring + dynamic rate control resampling ±0.5% against buffer fill, W2-05, behind W2-01a → W2-01b), which is right because the audio device's consumption rate cannot drift against itself and is what FM-02's underrun mitigation assumes. W2-05 subsumes this; shipping it now beat leaving release 18.8× too fast for three tickets. **Deadline-based, not sleep-per-frame**, because the two failure modes pull opposite ways: a fixed sleep accumulates every scheduler overshoot forever (drift), while a pure deadline owes hundreds of frames after a stall and then free-runs repaying them (death spiral, seen by the user as unwanted fast-forward). Solved with an absolute deadline advanced by exactly one period, plus a 34 ms `MAX_LAG` past which the debt is dropped. The pacer holds **no clock of its own** — every method takes `now` — so all 8 tests use synthetic timestamps and none sleeps. **DETERMINISM IS UNAFFECTED, structurally:** pacing gates *when* `tick_running_with_input` is called, never what it is called with; `validate-arch`'s FR-CORE-003 wall-clock lint greps only the core crates and this is the app shell; and the release 10k-frame double-run pair was re-run after the change (2 passed, unchanged) — that suite drives `EmuStepper` directly and never constructs a pacer. MUTATION: deleting the `MAX_LAG` resync branch fails the one-second-stall test, which asserts the next frame is paced normally rather than being one of ~60 catch-up frames. **FOUR TESTS WERE NAIVE, NOT THE CODE** — same trap as W2-15's, recurring within a day: the first drafts called `next_delay` exactly *at* the deadline and expected a full period, but the return is time-*until-due*, so arriving on time correctly means "run now, wait zero". Rewritten to model the real loop, reasoning kept in the test doc. Gate: all seven exit 0, **495 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored** (was 487); `validate-evidence` green with no regeneration, itself confirming nothing under `crates/rf-nes` was touched.

2026-08-06 W3-05a done — **sprite-limit bypass overlay: the first enhancement feature.** An opt-in, **off-by-default** (law 6) overlay draws the sprites the 8-per-scanline limit dropped, so a crowded scanline stops flickering. The accuracy path still drops the 9th sprite exactly as hardware does — the overlay is a *parallel record* of what was dropped, composited over the finished frame. Requested by Brad ("no flashing"), split out of W3-05 whose full closure is 29 pts including authoring a cc65 platformer as the oracle. **THE SUBTLE CORRECTNESS POINT, and the best thing in this ticket:** overlay pattern fetches go through a new `Ppu::chr_peek` rather than `mem_read`, because `mem_read` unconditionally calls `observe_ppu_bus_address` — the A12 rising-edge filter **MMC3's scanline IRQ counter depends on** (W2-03). Had the overlay used `mem_read`, merely *enabling* the enhancement would have clocked MMC3's IRQ counter and desynchronised every MMC3 game's raster effects: an enhancement silently corrupting the simulation, exactly what law 6 exists to prevent, and **invisible to any test that only compares pixels**. `mmc3_test_2` 6/6 is a real canary here, not decoration; the conductor re-ran that mutation independently (reverting `chr_peek`→`mem_read` fails the A12 test). **THE IMPLEMENTING AGENT CORRECTED THE CONDUCTOR'S BRIEF FOR THE SECOND TIME THIS SESSION** (W2-03 was the first) and was right again: the brief specified `EmuStepper::state_hash()` compared frame-by-frame as the mode-invariant proof, but that check is **insufficient** — `state_hash` covers CPU regs/WRAM/OAM/PRG-RAM/cycles and **excludes PPU internals by design**, a limitation the conductor documented in W1-07 and then wrote a brief that inherited. Verified independently: injecting a status-flag perturbation fails the agent's own field-by-field PPU comparison ("status ($2002) must be identical") while the `state_hash` test the brief asked for keeps passing. The agent added the stronger check unprompted and kept the weaker one as the requested formality, labelling which is which. *Conductor note on method:* the first two attempts at that mutation were no-ops — one OR-ed a flag the >8-sprite fixture already sets legitimately, the other used a regex that didn't match the real signature — and a less careful check would have wrongly concluded the tests were vacuous. **Verify a mutation actually applied before trusting its result.** **AN ORDERING BUG WAS FOUND AND FIXED, not shipped as a known gap:** reading the evaluation-time overlay buffer (dot 65) instead of a dot-257 latched copy tears mid-scanline, because evaluation runs partway through the very scanline being emitted — x<64 would show the previous line's overlay, x≥64 the current one. The accuracy path never has this problem because its latch already happens at dots 257–320. Fixed with `overlay_active_sprites` mirroring `active_sprites`, covered by a dot-driven regression test verified to fail pre-fix. **CONTRACT CHANGE, minimal and backward-compatible:** `CoreSink` gains `overlay_scanline` as a **default no-op**, so `PpuPixel`/`video_scanline` stay untouched and the sink remains accuracy-exact per Brad's 2026-08-03 ruling; its doc warns wrapping sinks must forward it, and the conductor verified `stepper.rs`'s `CountingSink` does. **NOT VERIFIED AND NOT CLAIMED:** intentional-blink preservation — without RF-Scroller's engineered scenes there is no automated check that deliberate flicker (invincibility frames, low-health warnings) survives the bypass; that stays a human check until W3-05 + W2-10. Gate: all seven exit 0, **505 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored** (was 495); canaries hold with the PPU's sprite code edited — sprite_hit 11/11, both golden frames, nestest 8991/8991 byte-exact, nes6502 2,560,000/2,560,000, ppu_vbl_nmi 9/10, mmc3_test_2 6/6.

2026-08-06 W3-01 BLOCKED after two stalls — wgpu original pipeline. Both subagent attempts hit the 600 s watchdog and **left the working tree completely clean**: zero files between them, the second despite an explicit instruction to land work incrementally. Per PLAYBOOK's block-note discipline ("a ticket blocked twice parks for a human — no third unattended attempt") it is parked, but with the cause narrowed rather than merely reported. **THE ENVIRONMENT IS NOT THE BLOCKER**, established by compile-probe rather than inferred: a scratch binary requesting a headless wgpu adapter and device succeeds here — 1 adapter, **Metal / Apple M5 Max**, `request_adapter` and `request_device` both `Ok`, no hang. So a stall is a *blocking call*, not a missing GPU — and attempt 1's own last words point straight at it: it had the palette pass working "end-to-end on real Metal hardware" and stalled immediately on *"verify the device-loss callback mechanism"*, i.e. almost certainly waiting on a device-lost callback that never fires, or a `map_async`/`device.poll` readback deadlock. **Milestone 4 is the hazard; milestones 1–3 are not.** **WHAT ELSE THE INVESTIGATION FOUND, and why it likely cost both agents their time:** wgpu 29's API differs from every pre-29 idiom — and therefore from training data — in three ways, each a *compile error* rather than a loud failure: `InstanceDescriptor` has **no `Default` impl** (use `new_without_display_handle()`, which is exactly the headless constructor this ticket needs); `Instance::new` takes the descriptor **by value**; and `enumerate_adapters` returns an **`impl Future`**, not a `Vec`. The conductor hit all three in sequence while probing, each costing a build cycle. All three plus a **verified-working headless skeleton** are now recorded in `docs/TECH_STACK.md`'s new "wgpu 29 API traps" section, so a third attempt starts from working code rather than rediscovering them — CLAUDE.md law 2 paying off exactly as intended, just paid by the conductor after the fact instead of by the implementer up front. Already committed and reusable: the pinned `wgpu = "29.0.4"` dep with the one-wgpu invariant verified empirically (`grep -c '^name = "wgpu"$' Cargo.lock` == 1, resolving to 29.0.4 — a direct pin unifies with egui-wgpu's requirement and does **not** reinstate the two-version trap) and a warm build cache (cold build measured at 26.8 s, ruling out build time as the stall cause). Gate green at park: fmt 0 · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0 · evidence 0 · 505 passing.

2026-08-07 W3-01 attempt 3 — milestones 1, 2, 4 landed; milestone 3 not attempted, reported not silently skipped. Third attempt, human-authorized (PLAYBOOK's two-attempt rule) after the device-loss hazard that killed attempts 1-2 was split out to **W3-01a**, out of this ticket's scope. **Milestone 1 (palette pass + golden hash):** `crates/rf-renderer/src/gpu.rs` (headless adapter/device acquisition, `GpuContext::request_headless() -> Result<_, GpuUnavailable>`) + `src/original_pipeline.rs` (`IndexedFrame`, `PalettePass`: R8Uint texture upload → fullscreen-triangle render pipeline, `textureLoad` against a 64-entry storage-buffer LUT → RGBA8 target → bounded-timeout readback) + `src/shaders/palette.wgsl`. `crates/rf-harness/tests/golden_frame_gpu.rs` exercises the real pipeline against a synthetic 256×240 diagonal-stripe frame (covers all 64 palette entries) with two independent checks: every readback pixel re-verified against the CPU oracle (`palette_index_to_rgb`, never touches the GPU shader) every run, plus a frozen SHA-256 literal of the RGBA8 bytes (measured once on Metal/Apple M5 Max) as the actual golden-regression gate — the CPU-oracle check alone cannot catch a corrupted LUT (both sides move together), only the frozen literal can, which is why both exist. **MUTATION VERIFIED**: swapping `NES_PALETTE[$00]`/`[$01]` in `crates/rf-renderer/src/palette.rs` flips the golden-hash assertion from pass to fail (`7a6a00b6...` → `d1b71489...`), restored and confirmed byte-identical by SHA-256 of the source file before/after. **Milestone 2 (palette to data):** the `NES_PALETTE` literal moved to a checked-in `crates/rf-renderer/assets/nes.pal` (192 bytes, unchanged values) decoded at compile time via a `const fn` (`decode_pal`) — zero runtime cost, single source of truth. `src/bin/gen-pal` is the generator (extracts the 64-entry no-emphasis slice from an upstream `.pal`), round-trip verified byte-identical against the checked-in asset. **Re-ran the milestone-1 golden hash unchanged after the move — proof the move was lossless**, exactly as intended. **Milestone 4 (CI/doc alignment + mechanical guard):** RENDERER.md §7 corrected — CI's `WGPU_BACKEND=gl` is OpenGL/**llvmpipe**, not Vulkan/Lavapipe as an earlier draft implied; kept `gl` (decided on its merits: the palette pass is a plain render pipeline with no compute-shader requirement, and llvmpipe ships in `ubuntu-latest`'s stock Mesa with no extra `apt` packages) and documented why. The golden-hash's cross-backend claim is stated as *derived from* RENDERER.md §7's bit-exactness argument, not yet independently observed past Metal — CI's first green run on llvmpipe is what confirms it; a red run there is flagged as a cross-backend question first, not automatically a regression. `scripts/validate-arch.sh` gained rule 5, the one-wgpu invariant made mechanical (`grep -c '^name = "wgpu"$' Cargo.lock` == 1) rather than an honour-system sentence in HANDOFF.md. `docs/TECH_STACK.md`'s GPU row amended per the W3-01 ruling (direct pin in `rf-renderer`, lockstep with egui, purpose preserved) and its "wgpu 29 API traps" table extended with the four more traps found completing the readback path the original skeleton stopped short of: `PollType` replacing `Maintain` (bounded `timeout: Some(_)` — the anti-hang mechanism itself), the `TexelCopy*` renames, `PipelineLayoutDescriptor::bind_group_layouts` becoming `&[Option<&BindGroupLayout>]` with `immediate_size` replacing `push_constant_ranges`, and `RenderPassColorAttachment`'s new `depth_slice` field. **Milestone 3 (scale pass) deliberately not attempted**: RENDERER.md §7 is explicit that anything past the 1× buffer is not CI-hashable (shader/scale output differs per driver) — verifying a scale pass needs a reference-image-with-tolerance mechanism this ticket never scoped, and landing an unverified scale pass would convert a clean "not attempted" into "attempted, correctness unknown," which is worse. Left for a follow-up ticket alongside W3-01a. **W3-05a's overlay compositing untouched and still verified**: `frame.rs` (the CPU blit path `overlay_scanline`/`FrameBuffer` this ticket did not touch) — all 6 of its tests still pass. Gate, all re-run independently this session: `cargo fmt --all --check` 0 · `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` 0 · `cargo test --workspace` 0 (**507 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored**, +2 over the pre-ticket 505: `gpu::tests::request_headless_does_not_panic_either_way` + `palette_pass_golden_hash`) · `scripts/validate-arch.sh` 0 (`arch OK`, including the new rule 5) · `node scripts/validate-plan.mjs` 0 · `node scripts/validate-traceability.mjs` 0 (101/101, zero dangling) · `node scripts/validate-evidence.mjs` 0, **green with no regeneration** (confirms nothing under `crates/rf-nes` was touched) · `cargo deny check licenses` 0 ("licenses ok", covers the new direct `pollster` dep). `grep -c '^name = "wgpu"$' Cargo.lock` == 1, held throughout. Not committed and ticket not closed — per this attempt's brief, the conductor owns status mutation and re-runs the gate independently.

2026-08-07 W3-01 done (attempt 3, after two stalls) — **wgpu original pipeline + headless golden frames.** The GPU palette pass lands: indexed frame → R8Uint upload → LUT palette pass → RGBA8 → bounded readback → SHA-256, with the palette moved out of a Rust literal into a checked-in `.pal` asset plus a `gen-pal` generator, so palette research never touches shaders. **THE STALLS' ACTUAL ROOT CAUSE, found and fixed at the source:** wgpu 29 replaced `Maintain` with `PollType`, and `Device::poll` must be given a **bounded** `timeout: Some(_)` — `timeout: None` blocks forever. That is the readback deadlock that killed attempts 1 and 2, and it sits exactly where attempt 1 reported freezing ("verify the device-loss callback mechanism"). The conductor's TECH_STACK skeleton got instance/adapter/device right but stopped short of the readback — precisely the gap the earlier attempts fell into. Attempt 3 verified **four more** breaking changes against the vendored source and recorded them all: `Maintain`→`PollType` with bounded timeout, `ImageCopy*`→`TexelCopy*`, `PipelineLayoutDescriptor::bind_group_layouts` now `&[Option<&BindGroupLayout>]` with `immediate_size` replacing `push_constant_ranges`, and `RenderPassColorAttachment` gaining a required `depth_slice`. **Seven verified wgpu-29 API breaks now sit in `docs/TECH_STACK.md` where the next renderer ticket will find them** — CLAUDE.md law 2 turned into durable artefact rather than a lesson relearned. **THE ONE-WGPU INVARIANT IS NOW MECHANICAL:** `scripts/validate-arch.sh` gained rule 5 (`grep -c '^name = "wgpu"$' Cargo.lock` == 1), replacing an honour-system sentence in HANDOFF — which stopped being reliable the moment `rf-renderer` became the second wgpu-pulling crate. Conductor mutation-verified it: appending a fake second wgpu entry fails the gate. CI/doc conflict resolved deliberately rather than by matching strings: kept `WGPU_BACKEND=gl` (llvmpipe ships in stock `ubuntu-latest` Mesa; the palette pass needs no compute shader) and corrected RENDERER.md's "Lavapipe" wording, since Lavapipe is the **Vulkan** rasteriser — reasoning recorded in the doc. **Conductor verification:** the golden test genuinely *runs* here (Metal adapter present) and its skip path prints a distinguishable `SKIP` line — the exact failure mode the Phase 1 challenger caught for real. MUTATION: corrupting the `.pal` **data asset** (two swapped bytes, not the Rust literal) flips the golden hash pass→FAIL, so the asset is load-bearing rather than decorative. **GOLDEN-HASH HONESTY, flagged by the agent rather than glossed:** the frozen SHA-256 was measured once on Metal and is documented as *derived from* the bit-exactness argument (integer LUT lookups, no filtering, no sRGB math on the 1× buffer), **not** as cross-backend-observed; CI's first llvmpipe run confirms or flags it, and an independent CPU-oracle pixel check that never touches the GPU shader makes a backend disagreement distinguishable from a logic bug. **SCALE PASS SPLIT TO W3-01b** — the agent declined to build it and said why: RENDERER.md §7 makes post-1× output non-hashable across backends, so no mechanism in this repo can gate a scaled frame, and "landing an unverified scale pass would have converted a clean not-attempted into attempted-correctness-unknown, which is worse". W3-01b is scoped as scale pass **plus** the reference-image tolerance harness that can check it. This is W3-01's second split (W3-01a took device-loss) and is flagged as such. Gate: all seven exit 0, **507 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored** (was 505), `cargo deny` "licenses ok", evidence green with no regeneration.

2026-08-07 W3-03 attempt 1 — BG/sprite layer extraction + debug view, landed but not closed (agent brief: conductor owns status mutation). `crates/rf-renderer/src/layers.rs` adds `LayeredFrame`, a `CoreSink` mirroring `FrameBuffer`'s shape that buckets every `PpuPixel` into two independently renderable RGBA buffers using `PixelLayer` alone — `bg_rgba` (`Backdrop` + `Background`, any plane index) and `sprite_rgba` (`Sprite`) — deliberately **not** `RENDERER.md` §3's `SceneGraph`/`ExtractedBg`/`SpriteSet` (that's `rf-enhance`'s `SceneComposer` output, still a stub with no second consumer to inform its shape — the ticket's own scope fence). Each buffer is explicitly transparent wherever the *other* layer drew that scanline, not merely left stale, so isolation holds pixel-for-pixel. `crates/retroforge/src/core_thread.rs` adds a private `DualSink` fanout (forwards `video_scanline` to both `FrameBuffer` and `LayeredFrame`; `overlay_scanline` forwards **only** to `FrameBuffer` — W3-05a's de-flicker overlay stays exactly where it is, migrating it is W3-05's job) wired at all three `EmuStepper` call sites, plus two new `FrameMsg` fields populated every frame. `crates/retroforge/src/app.rs` adds a "Layers (debug)" checkbox + `egui::Window` showing both layers as separate egui textures side by side — acceptance criterion 2. `rf-core-api` **not touched** — `PixelLayer`/`PpuPixel` already carried everything needed. **MUTATION VERIFIED**: routing sprite pixels into the BG buffer instead of the sprite buffer flips 2 of 8 `layers` tests from pass to FAIL; restored and confirmed byte-identical by SHA-256 before/after. W3-01's golden hash (real GPU run on this Metal host, not a skip) and all 6 of `frame.rs`'s W3-05a overlay tests plus all 3 `sprite_overlay_mode_invariant.rs` tests re-ran unchanged and pass — neither file touched. Gate, all re-run this session: `cargo fmt --all --check` 0 · `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` 0 · `cargo test --workspace` 0 (**516 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored**, +9 over the pre-ticket 507) · `scripts/validate-arch.sh` 0 (arch OK, wgpu count still 1) · `node scripts/validate-plan.mjs` 0 · `node scripts/validate-traceability.mjs` 0 (101/101, zero dangling) · `node scripts/validate-evidence.mjs` 0, green with no regeneration. No new dependency added (`Cargo.lock` zero diff), so `cargo deny` not run. **Self-review fix before reporting:** the `core_thread.rs` wiring test was first written vacuous (length-only assertion, which `LayeredFrame::new()`'s pre-allocation passes even with the `layers` forward silently dropped) — fixed to assert real opaque-pixel content on the second `StepFrame` (the first is the pre-render-scanline boot artifact, per `stepper.rs`'s own documented trap) and mutation-verified at that seam too (commenting out the forward makes it FAIL). Also added an explicit "deliberate limitation" paragraph to `layers.rs`: `CoreSink::video_scanline` is already occlusion-resolved (one `PpuPixel` per x), so `bg_rgba` has sprite-shaped holes and `sprite_rgba` never shows a sprite drawn behind an opaque BG pixel — real information loss versus RENDERER.md §3's eventual atlas-based extraction, not a bug here. **Honestly flagged, not silently passed over:** acceptance criterion 2 (debug view) has zero automated coverage — `app.rs`'s checkbox/window/texture code is compile-verified only, never exercised in a running window this session (PLAYBOOK's manual-checklist allowance for UI tickets applies); `pump_core_events` now does two unconditional 240 KB buffer clones + two texture uploads per frame regardless of whether the debug window is shown (unquantified cost, a possible follow-up); `Background(n)` planes all collapse into one bg bucket (no loss for NES, would be lossy if reused for a future multi-plane SNES core). Gate re-ran clean after the fixes, counts unchanged (516/0/3 — the fix renamed/strengthened one test rather than adding one). Not committed; ticket left `in_progress` per this attempt's brief.

2026-08-07 W3-03 done — **layer/sprite extraction**, second piece of the enhancement spine. `LayeredFrame` (a `CoreSink` mirroring `FrameBuffer`'s shape) buckets every `PpuPixel` into independently renderable BG and sprite RGBA buffers using only `rf_core_api::PixelLayer`. The property that makes it real is **per-pixel exclusivity**: a pixel routed to one layer is written opaque there *and* explicitly transparent in the other at the same offset, so a debug view of either buffer alone shows a genuinely isolated layer rather than stale content from a previous frame's occupant. **`rf-core-api` was NOT touched** — `PixelLayer`/`PpuPixel` already carried everything, so the public contract took no new commitment. **THE SCOPE FENCE HELD:** no `SceneGraph`/`ExtractedBg`/`SpriteSet` was built. Those are ENHANCEMENT_RUNTIME §7's, produced by rf-enhance's still-stubbed `SceneComposer` and arriving via W4-01's event bus; building them speculatively would have been the same "unvalidated scaffolding" W1-02 declined for the `Mapper` trait, and for the same reason — no second consumer yet to inform the shape. MUTATION re-run independently: routing sprite pixels into `bg_rgba` fails `sprite_pixel_appears_in_sprite_layer_and_not_in_bg_layer` and `mixed_row_splits_bg_and_sprite_pixels_independently` — names that matter, since they assert exclusivity rather than the near-vacuous "both layers non-empty" the brief warned against. *Conductor method note, second occurrence this session:* the first mutation attempt didn't match the real code and silently changed nothing, reporting a false pass — **always confirm a mutation applied before trusting its result.** **THE AGENT'S OWN SELF-REVIEW CAUGHT A VACUOUS TEST:** its first `core_thread` wiring test asserted only buffer *length*, which passes even if `DualSink` silently drops the layer sink; it found that itself, rewrote it to assert real opaque-pixel content on the **second** `StepFrame` (the first being the documented pre-render boot artifact — the same trap W2-15 and W1-06 both hit), and verified the fanout removal makes it fail. **PERF MEASURED, NOT SPECULATED:** the agent flagged that `DualSink` and the two extra `FrameMsg` buffers are unconditional — every frame pays the split plus two ~240 KB clones whether or not the window is open. Measured through the real core thread on Alter Ego: **60.0 fps debug (0.998× NTSC), 60.2 release (1.003×)** — fully absorbed inside W2-18's 16.64 ms budget even in the tightest build, so not a regression, but headroom a slower host or heavier scene would want. Filed as **W3-03a** rather than conductor-fixed: the conductor-fix precedent covers *defects*, and this is an optimisation of correct, tested, measured code. **KNOWN GAP:** criterion 2 (debug view) is **compile-verified only** — there is now a "Layers (debug)" checkbox opening a side-by-side BG/sprite window, but nobody has looked at it; same manual-checklist situation as W1-06. Criterion 1 is fully covered by 8 isolation tests. Also documented as a real limitation of *this* approach rather than a bug: `video_scanline` is already occlusion-resolved (one `PpuPixel` per x), so `bg_rgba` carries sprite-shaped holes and `sprite_rgba` cannot show a sprite hidden behind opaque background — recovering that needs the atlas-based extraction RENDERER §3 describes. Gate: all seven exit 0, **516 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored** (was 507).

2026-08-07 W4-00 done — **rf-nes emits CoreEvents**, the producer half of a contract that had none. W0-04 shipped `CoreEvent` + `EventMask` as a versioned rf-core-api contract; nothing ever emitted them, and three tickets were queued behind it (W4-01's bus, W4-03a's stitcher, W4-06a's event viewer) — a gap found while pre-flighting W4-01, which would otherwise have built a bus with neither producer nor consumer. Now emitting: `FrameStart`, `FrameEnd`, `VblankStart`, `Scanline`, `ScrollWrite` (loopy `t`/`x` decoded to pixel coords), `OamRewrite`, `MapperIrq`, `DmaStart` — every one `EventMask`-gated. **`rf-core-api` untouched, no new dependency** (`Cargo.lock` zero diff): the contract was already complete. **DESIGN CALL WORTH KEEPING:** a *single* shared event FIFO that `NesBus` also pushes into, rather than two queues drained separately — that preserves cross-source ordering (`MapperIrq` vs `ScrollWrite` on the same scanline), which is exactly what W4-03a needs to attribute a mid-frame split. Wrong here would have been invisible now and expensive later. **THE HAZARD WAS NAVIGATED, re-verified independently:** every emission reads only already-in-hand register state and none touches `mem_read`/`chr_peek` or any bus-observing accessor. Conductor mutation: injecting a side-effecting `read_untimed($2007)` at the `MapperIrq` emission fails the non-perturbation test — the W3-05a hazard class (an enhancement silently clocking MMC3's A12 counter, invisible to pixel comparison) now mechanically guarded rather than merely avoided. Non-perturbation uses the **strong** check as required, not `state_hash`: field-by-field comparison of `status`/`v`/`t`/`x`/`w`/scanline/dot/frame counters/line buffer **plus the A12 trio**, and at bus level `master_cycle`/RAM/open bus/PRG-RAM/DMA stall/NMI latch/IRQ line/OAM — with a dedicated test proving the workload actually reaches all eight variants, so a perturbation test cannot pass by never exercising what it guards. **TWO SELF-CAUGHT ERRORS ARE THE BEST PART:** the agent's first bench was invalid (never drained the event queue, so unbounded per-iteration `Vec` growth dominated — CI ±75%, sign-flipped means); it noticed, fixed it, and re-ran a proper A/B (git worktree at `b30584a`, shared target dir, criterion baselines), reporting both arms as-is — scroll writes `[-0.97%, -0.15%, +0.67%] p=0.73` "no change detected", frame tick within noise. And one of its own mutations was **vacuous** — a call injected inside a `for _ in 0..take_a12_edges()` body does nothing since the range is evaluated once — which it found, stated, and replaced with one that genuinely fails. That is the third self-caught vacuous test this session. Documented gaps rather than silent omissions: `$2006`-driven mid-frame splits and individual `$2004` writes are not emitted (handed to W4-03a, the ticket that will discover whether they matter); `MemWatch` is debugger-configured, not hardware-produced. **CONDUCTOR ERROR WORTH RECORDING:** the closing `plan.json` edit put seven conductor notes into `acceptance` instead of `notes` (`rindex` matched the wrong array terminator) and was committed before `validate-plan` was re-run; the first repair guessed at boundaries and produced invalid JSON. Restored from git, redone against printed line indices with per-comma assertions — one of which fired on a wrong assumption and aborted before writing. Lesson: plan.json's 1-space indent and `\uXXXX` escaping rule out a JSON round-trip, so structural edits need exact indices confirmed by inspection, never pattern matching. Gate: all seven exit 0, **526 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored** (was 516); evidence regenerated on a clean tree; canaries green including **mmc3_test_2 6/6**.

2026-08-07 W4-01 attempt 1 — event bus + FrameBundle + subscriptions, landed but not closed (agent brief: conductor owns status mutation). **`FrameBundle` lands in `rf-core-api`** per the claim note's placement ruling: `frame_count`/`width`/`height`/`video: Vec<PpuPixel>`/`events: Vec<CoreEvent>` only — `audio` and `state_snapshot_refs` (both named in ARCHITECTURE §6's sketch) deliberately omitted as speculative (no consumer for either exists: `rf-audio` is an empty stub, and `StateView` is borrowed/`<'_>` so it cannot live in an owned triple-buffered value until `rf-nes` implements `EmulatorCore::state_view()`, itself out of scope). `FrameBundleBuilder` (a `CoreSink` accumulator, deliberately no-ops `overlay_scanline` so `FrameBundle::video` stays accuracy-exact) and `TripleBufferWriter`/`TripleBufferReader` (hand-rolled, `Arc`-swap-per-slot, `Mutex`-guarded only for the O(1) swap itself — chosen over a crate dependency) round out `rf-core-api`'s addition; `CoreEvent::mask_bit()` added to `event.rs` for subscription filtering. **`rf-enhance` gets its first real content**: `bus::FrameSubscriber` (`EventMask`-filtered view over a cloned `TripleBufferReader<FrameBundle>`) — deliberately stops short of `SpriteHistorian`/`ScrollTracker`/`EnhancementRuntime`, same scope fence W3-03 drew around `SceneGraph` (no second consumer yet). **`core_thread.rs`**: `DualSink` renamed `FanoutSink` (third leg added), `bundle_builder`/`bundle_writer` wired at all three `EmuStepper` call sites, `CoreHandle.frame_bundle: TripleBufferReader<FrameBundle>` exposed for render/debug threads to clone independently. **PERF MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED** (W3-03a's own precedent): through the real core thread on a sprite-rendering fixture, **60.11 fps debug, 60.08 fps release** — fully absorbed inside W2-18's 16.64 ms budget, no regression. **Mode-invariant harness** (`retroforge::mode_invariant`, criteria 3+4): corpus is exactly one entry (W3-05a's overlay) per the claim note's honesty constraint — the mechanism (N-ROMs-x-M-frames runner keyed by a `CorpusEntry::enable_enhancement` hook called every frame, matching ARCHITECTURE §8's per-frame `on_frame`) is real and built for W3-05/W4-03a to add entries without touching `check`. **Strong check**: `state_hash` plus a SHA-256 over the full indexed `Vec<PpuPixel>` stream (`FrameBundleBuilder`'s own accumulator, reused rather than a second bespoke sink) — chosen over hashing resolved RGBA specifically because RGBA collapses `sprite_id`/`priority`/`layer` into color, proven directly (`hash_indexed_video_is_more_precise_than_a_resolved_rgb_hash_would_be`: two pixels sharing a `palette_index` but differing in those fields hash differently). **HONEST LIMIT, found empirically and reported rather than argued past** (advisor caught the first, weaker draft before it shipped): a live demonstration of "video_hash catches a divergence `state_hash` structurally cannot" was attempted via an out-of-band `$2001` (PPUMASK) bus poke and produced the *opposite* of the intended shape — `state_hash` diverged (from the poke's own cycle cost: `NesBus::write`'s source, read not modified, ticks `master_cycle` on every write except `$4014`) while `video_hash` did not (the divergence surfaced before any rendering-visible frame). Read directly: **no externally-reachable perturbation can be constructed that `state_hash` misses**, because the only available primitive (`EmuStepper::poke_bus`, routed through already-public `rf_nes::CpuBus::write`) always taints `master_cycle`. The sharper claim stays proven only by W4-00's own in-`rf-nes` test (a zero-cost direct field write), out of this ticket's scope to duplicate. Mutation-test #1 was redone with a legible WRAM poke (`$0000`, a byte the fixture ROM never touches) instead, correctly caught by `state_hash` and documented as such — no false claim shipped. **R-F4**: `dump_divergence_evidence` reuses the existing `.rfreplay` format (`rf_input::ReplayRecorder`) for the replay slice rather than inventing one; wired into the real CI-gating test (not just the harness self-tests) so an actual regression dumps evidence before panicking; `.github/workflows/ci.yml` gained an `if: failure()` `actions/upload-artifact` step reading `target/tmp/mode-invariant-evidence/` (`$CARGO_TARGET_TMPDIR`, verified empirically to resolve workspace-wide, not per-binary). Verified end-to-end by temporarily forcing the real corpus entry to diverge (not just the harness self-tests): evidence dumped to disk, files inspected, mutation reverted, `sha256` confirmed byte-identical restoration. **MUTATION #2 (triple buffer) VERIFIED**: hardcoding `TripleBufferReader::latest()` to slot 0 made `each_publish_creates_a_genuinely_new_buffer_not_a_mutated_alias` fail with the exact expected mismatch; reverted, `sha256` byte-identical. **MUTATION on `core_thread.rs` wiring VERIFIED**: commenting out `bundle_writer.publish(...)` made the new `step_frame_command_also_publishes_a_matching_frame_bundle` test fail (`frame_count` stuck at 0); reverted, `sha256` byte-identical. Criterion 1 (EventMask ~zero-cost) **not re-benched** — already delivered by W4-00 on the emission side (`p=0.73`, no change detected); this ticket's own subscription-side cost is structurally bounded by that same emission-side mask (an empty `events: Vec` costs nothing to filter), reasoned through rather than re-measured, per the claim note's own instruction to only bench if the bus itself adds per-event cost. `rf-nes` **not touched** (`docs/evidence/local-gate.json` diff-clean, `validate-evidence` green with no regeneration, `retroforge_commit` unchanged). Gate, all re-run this session: `cargo fmt --all --check` 0 · `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` 0 · `cargo test --workspace` 0 (**552 passed / 0 failed / 4 ignored**, +26 over the pre-ticket 526) · `scripts/validate-arch.sh` 0 · `node scripts/validate-plan.mjs` 0 · `node scripts/validate-traceability.mjs` 0 (101/101, zero dangling) · `node scripts/validate-evidence.mjs` 0, green with no regeneration · `cargo deny check licenses` 0 (`licenses ok`; only new dependency is the internal `rf-enhance -> rf-core-api` path dep, `Cargo.lock` diff is 3 lines). 2026-08-07 W4-01 done — **event bus + FrameBundle + subscriptions. THE ENHANCEMENT SPINE IS COMPLETE**: W3-01 (GPU palette pipeline) + W3-03 (layer extraction) + W4-00 (core event emission) + W4-01 (bus, triple-buffered `FrameBundle`, mode-invariant harness). `FrameBundle` lands in `rf-core-api` (not rf-enhance) because RENDERER and FRONTEND_UI both consume it and neither may depend on the enhancement side; triple buffering is a hand-rolled `Arc`-swap-per-slot design with no new dependency. `dump_divergence_evidence` reuses the `.rfreplay` format and CI gained an `if: failure()` artifact upload (R-F4). **THE IMPLEMENTING AGENT CORRECTED THE CONDUCTOR'S BRIEF FOR THE THIRD TIME THIS SESSION** (after W2-03 and W3-05a), and the correction is sharper than the instruction it replaced. The brief said "use the strong PPU-internal check, not `state_hash`", generalising W3-05a's finding. The agent tried to *demonstrate* that, got the opposite result, and reasoned about why rather than fudging: **from outside `rf-nes` the only perturbation tool is `CpuBus::write`, and every write ticks `master_cycle` — which `state_hash` covers — so no externally-reachable perturbation can evade it.** Conductor verified independently from source: `NesBus::write` has two paths (`$4014` DMA and normal) and **both** call `tick_master(1)`. So W3-05a's lesson is real but **scoped**: `state_hash`'s PPU blind spot is only reachable by code *inside* `rf-nes` writing PPU state directly — exactly what W3-05a's overlay and W4-00's emission do, and exactly why both were tested in-crate with field-by-field comparisons. The conductor had over-generalised that lesson across three briefs. **CONDUCTOR MUTATIONS, one informative by failing to fail:** pinning the triple buffer's **reader** to slot 0 fails three tests, so the primitive is genuinely guarded; pinning the **writer** fails nothing — and that is *not* a test gap, because correctness comes from replacing the whole value behind a fresh `Arc` under a mutex, so a reader gets the old `Arc` or the new one and never a torn read. The three slots reduce *contention*, a performance property. Recorded so nobody later reads that no-op as a hole. **SCOPE DISCIPLINE HELD:** `rf-core-api` gained `FrameBundle`, `TripleBuffer` and `CoreEvent::mask_bit` and nothing speculative — the agent declined the `audio` and `state_snapshot_refs` fields ARCHITECTURE §6 sketches (rf-audio has no consumers; `StateView` is borrowed and can't live in an owned bundle until W2-04 lands `state_view`). Same restraint as W1-02 on the Mapper trait and W3-03 on the SceneGraph. **HONEST GAPS:** the mode-invariant corpus has exactly **one** entry (W3-05a's overlay) because that is the only enhancement that exists — the deliverable is the *mechanism*, structured for W3-05/W4-03a to add entries without touching the checker; rf-renderer and UI panels do not yet consume `FrameBundle`. Perf measured through the real core thread: **60.11 fps debug, 60.08 release**, inside W2-18's budget. **CONDUCTOR PROCESS FIX:** this close corrupted `plan.json` a *third* time (notes landed in `stories`, because a bare `]` search matched that array's terminator while `notes` ends `],`). Restored and replaced the technique — `scratchpad/append_notes.py` locates a named ticket's notes array by walking **bracket depth** and refuses to write anything that doesn't re-parse. All three corruptions this session came from guessing where an array ends. Gate: all seven exit 0, **552 passed / 0 failed / 4 ignored** (was 526), evidence green with no regeneration.

2026-08-07 W4-03a done — **scroll tracker + stitcher core**; the level now accumulates as you play (wideNES technique). **THE `$2006` QUESTION W4-00 HANDED FORWARD IS ANSWERED, and structurally rather than by trial:** `background.rs`'s `process_render_dot` calls `copy_horizontal` at dot 257 of **every** scanline but `copy_vertical` only on the pre-render line at dots 280–304 — so the vertical half of `v` is never refreshed from `t` during the visible picture by any hardware path, and the only mid-picture route is a direct `v` write, which is exactly `$2006`'s second write. nesdev's PPU_scrolling says the same in words: *"Without the second write to $2006, only the horizontal portion of v will be reloaded from t."* A mid-frame **vertical** split is therefore unreachable through `$2005` alone, and a stitcher blind to `$2006` could never exclude a HUD band. That note W4-00 wrote did its job one ticket later, which is the point of writing them. **THE EMPIRICAL HALF WAS HONEST ABOUT ITS LIMITS:** the agent drove Alter Ego and Marble Madness 4000 frames each and never coaxed either into real scrolling gameplay, so a genuine split was never *directly observed* — reported, not glossed. The measurement still earned its keep negatively: all **394** observed active-picture `$2006` writes had rendering **disabled** (ordinary blanked-screen VRAM setup) against 12,054/22,412 vblank writes, which is what justifies the gate `rendering_enabled() && scanline < 240` producing zero false positives on the fixture set. **CONDUCTOR FIX — THE NEW EMISSION SHIPPED WITH NO TEST, AND THAT WAS MY FAULT:** rf-nes's count was unchanged at 224 after a second `ScrollWrite` site was added, because the write_scope I wrote granted `ppu/scroll.rs` but **not** `ppu/tests/**` — the **14th** self-blocking scope on this board and an exact repeat of the W2-02 mistake (`system/mod.rs` without `system/tests/`). Scope widened; four tests added, one per axis of a two-part gate; **mutation-verified in both directions** — dropping `rendering_enabled()` fails only the rendering test, dropping `scanline < 240` fails only the vblank test, so each half is independently guarded rather than jointly covered. **THE AGENT CAUGHT A CIRCULAR TEST OF ITS OWN** (third self-caught vacuity this session): an earlier determinism test compared canvas *content* at a predicted world coordinate and kept passing under the wraparound mutation, because overlapping 224-row bands meant earlier correct frames coincidentally satisfied later frames' wrong predictions; it refactored to assert `ScrollTracker`'s own returned world positions. It also found a **real bug in its own heuristic** — anchoring world accumulation to the first non-HUD band wrongly picked the HUD on frame 0, corrupting every later wraparound delta; fixed by selecting the largest band as primary. Canvas is a flat buffer, not a `HashMap`, because unstable iteration order would poison the byte-identical determinism check. Pipeline costs **0.19 ms/frame release, 2.85 ms debug** — far inside the 16.64 ms budget, though *not* measured through the real core thread since nothing wires the tracker in yet. Scope fence held: no persistence, no scene identity, no camera (W4-03b/W4-08). Gate: all seven exit 0, **577 passed / 0 failed / 4 ignored** (was 552), `licenses ok`.

2026-08-07 W4-08 done — **rf-cache is a real store**, and W4-03b (the persisted whole-level / ultrawide view) is now claimable: this was its last `depends_on`. Public surface `Cache::open(root, cap_bytes)/get/put/contains/total_bytes/cap_bytes` over **opaque bytes**; `CanvasChunk` untouched, since W4-03b owns both scene identity and canvas serialization. **DESIGN CALLS WORTH KEEPING:** entry filename = SHA-256 of the *length-prefixed* 4-tuple `(rom_sha256, asset_hash, producer, settings_hash)` — `producer` is the only free-text component and therefore the only path-traversal vector, so hashing removes it by construction and collapses acceptance 1 and 4 into one decision; length-prefixing (not concatenation) is what stops field-boundary shifting from colliding two different tuples. The cost is paid deliberately: a filename no longer reconstructs its key, so each entry carries its own key header — which is exactly what makes `Index::rebuild_from_entries` possible, and hence the index a *rebuildable local artifact* rather than a public contract (no versioned-TLV envelope, but it must survive being absent or corrupt — two tests prove it does). LRU clock is a **monotonic access counter**, never `SystemTime`/mtime/atime; **note for future tickets: `validate-arch.sh` rule 4's wall-clock ban lists only rf-core-api/rf-nes/rf-snes/rf-cart, so rf-cache is EXEMPT and the linter would not have caught a `SystemTime` here** — enforced by review, not by a gate. `zstd` deliberately **not** added despite being pre-authorized (no criterion needs compression); `Cargo.lock` grows by exactly two lines. **NFR-010's pattern is set here** — the first realpath containment in the repo: root canonicalized *once* at `open`, candidate validated via its deepest *existing* ancestor, because `canonicalize` fails on paths that don't exist yet and a cache exists to create such paths; all comparisons canonical-to-canonical, since on macOS `temp_dir` sits under `/var` → `/private/var` and an uncanonicalized root makes a *correct* implementation look broken. **CONDUCTOR FINDING — A BRANCH WAS REASONED-FOR BUT UNTESTED:** `ensure_contained`'s leaf-symlink check documents its justification as the **dangling**-symlink case (`Path::exists()` follows links and reports false, so the ancestor walk pops past it), but the existing leaf test pointed at an **existing** file, which the ancestor walk refuses on its own — so disabling the leaf branch alone left **all 20 tests green**. The agent's report claimed "two independent branches, both tested"; the ancestor branch genuinely was, the leaf branch was not. Added `refuses_a_dangling_leaf_symlink_pointing_outside`, which fails **alone** under that mutation. Nothing can escape today only because every write goes through `atomic_write` and `rename(2)` replaces a symlink rather than following it — the check is defense in depth against a future caller reaching for `File::create`. **Generalizable lesson: a test that *exercises* a branch is not a test that *discriminates* it; when two checks guard overlapping cases, mutate each one alone.** Two further conductor mutations run independently of the agent's: inverting victim selection (`min_by`→`max_by`) failed exactly the two LRU-order tests and nothing else — `eviction_never_exceeds_cap_after_multiple_puts` correctly stayed green, since it asserts only that the cap holds, not which entry goes. The agent also self-caught one vacuity before reporting (**fourth this session**): its first persistence test read key A in-session before triggering eviction, manufacturing the ordering it was meant to prove came from disk. Gate: all seven exit 0, **596 passed / 0 failed / 4 ignored** (was 577; rf-cache's own count 2 → 21), `licenses ok`. Evidence **not** regenerated and none due — `coveredPaths` are all `crates/rf-nes/*`, untouched here.

2026-08-07 W4-03b done — **the whole-level view has its data layer**, and one criterion is honestly short. Shipped: `SceneGraph`/`Camera`/`SceneLayer` per ENHANCEMENT_RUNTIME §7 (only `StitchedCanvas` has a producer; the other six variants are shape-only and *documented* as such rather than stubbed with fake producers), `FogMask` derived strictly from `Option<PpuPixel>::is_none`, canvas persistence through rf-cache, and the FM-13 zoom-fallback policy. `CanvasChunk` — shaped by W4-03a, constructed by nobody — is finally constructed. GPU compositing is W4-03c. **CRITERION 1 IS PARTIALLY MET, recorded against the agent's own closing line** ("no criterion is partially met", in the same report that documented 36 distinct scene ids across a scrolling session — both cannot be true). Scene identity is stable for room/static scenes, under sprite+HUD noise, and under per-call 1–4px jitter; it **fragments under sustained scroll**. **A CONDUCTOR PROBE REFUTED THE OBVIOUS FIX BEFORE IT COULD BE ATTEMPTED:** a perceptual hash is normally compared by Hamming *distance*, not equality, so the natural theory is that exact-key comparison was the bug — measured distance between consecutive frames' edge signatures under scroll is **34–44 of 112 buckets** (~30–40%), 106/112 against the first frame. The signature *decorrelates*, it does not drift, and no threshold repairs it without merging genuinely different scenes. Root cause is screen-space grid anchoring — and world-anchoring alone is not a fix either, since a scrolling viewport genuinely shows different world content, which is why §3's formulation works for room-based games (Zelda screens, Metroid rooms) and cannot work for scrollers by construction. **Filed rather than guessed:** W4-03d captures the continuity-based design (scene continuity as a function of ScrollTracker continuity + mapper bank state, perceptual hash used only to disambiguate after a discontinuity), depends on W2-10 for a real scrolling fixture, and is wired as a `depends_on` of W5-03 — where a fragmenting id would surface as a visibly broken level rather than a test failure. Magnitudes are **fixture-measured**: the fixture has structure at exactly the 32px block period, which is why distance oscillates (0, 34, 6, 34, 4, 44) rather than climbing; "36 ids per 150px" must not be carried forward as a property of real games. **TEST RENAMED AT CLOSE** — `..._never_exceed_one_distinct_scene_id` → `..._over_an_unchanged_background_stay_one_scene_id`: FM-11's detection column is "canvas count per scene watermark", so a green test under that label would tell a future reader canvas explosion is guarded when only the static-background half is. **Same failure class as the `rf_cache::fsutil` finding one ticket earlier — a test that *exercises* a branch is not a test that *discriminates* it, and an overstating name is how that survives review.** zstd earned its place on **measurement**, not reflex: a realistic 4-screen chunk with a 25% unvisited frontier compressed 1,351,691 → 135,171 bytes (**10.0×**); an earlier fixture reported 0.02% because its art was too periodic, and the agent noticed the flattering number, discarded the fixture and re-measured. No new crate entered the licence graph. Design calls kept: FM-13's policy is an **integer divisor**, not a float ratio (unambiguous "smallest divisor that fits", no float-determinism exposure); `CanvasChunk` serializes through a `WirePixel`/`WireChunk` bincode mirror because the orphan rule blocks deriving on `PpuPixel`, with a capped decompress mirroring `rf_state::container`'s rather than inventing a second policy. One brief prediction was **wrong and the agent said so** rather than force-matching it: an always-1 FM-13 divisor fails the "target genuinely fits" assertion itself, not merely the "largest zoom" one. Honest limit unchanged from W4-03a: **no fixture ROM has reached real scrolling gameplay**, so camera and fog are exercised only over synthesised canvases — W2-10 is what changes that, it is claimable now, and it also blocks W3-05, W5-01 and W4-03d. Gate: all seven exit 0, **620 passed / 0 failed / 4 ignored** (was 596), `licenses ok`.

2026-08-07 W2-11 done — **Alter Ego's 5-minute replay regression is real**, and the fixture is **not** Public Domain. Two tests: a cheap always-on one driving ~330 scripted frames (player X reaches 216 from a spawn of 184), and the full FR-CORE-026 regression recording an 18,000-frame log through a genuine `.rfreplay` round trip via rf-input, then replaying it through a **second, independent** `Cpu`/`NesBus` and checking every periodic hash, the final hash and 4 golden frames agree across both runs and against frozen constants (`#[ignore]`d, >120s debug / ~18s release). **The input log was derived EMPIRICALLY against the real fetched ROM** — a probe found the title-skip timing, located the player-X byte by WRAM diffing cross-checked against the OAM shadow, and rendered checkpoint frames to PNG. **That last step caught what no hash could:** the first script (hold Right to the wall, then idle) looked healthy by WRAM diffs while the player was actually dying repeatedly and had looped back to the title screen by frame 9000. Reworked to idle short of the wall and re-verified live across all 18,000 frames, so the goldens are genuinely distinct moving-enemy frames rather than a frozen menu. Mutation-verified both directions: all-zeros log fails 0 vs 216 (never leaves credits); mash-only log that reaches gameplay but presses no direction fails 184 vs 216. **That is the W1-07 vacuity class — a replay whose log never actually drives the game — closed properly.** `zip 8.6.0` added to rf-harness dev-deps (byte-identical pin to `retroforge`); TECH_STACK's Archive row amended and `fetch.rs`'s module doc updated, since it cited "would need a new zip crate dependency (a whole new TECH_STACK decision)" as its reason for having no unpack step and W2-13 resolved that. TESTING §4's Alter Ego row went from a bare "A" to "A — RUNS LOCALLY ONLY", per that section's own rule that a tier label overstating coverage is worse than an honest gap. **ESCALATED, NOT DECIDED — THE PD CLAIM IS WRONG:** `notes.txt`, shipped *inside* `alter_ego.zip`, reads "the game is released as freeware, not Public Domain… rights to other components (game concept, characters, title, music) are reserved". Only Shiru's own code/SFX/graphics carry do-what-you-want terms. Conductor verified against the primary source directly, not the agent's summary. RISKS R-16 already tracked this with the mitigation "keep the source-zip PD notice in the manifest" — **and that notice did not exist until this ticket**; it is now in verbatim with citation. `license_status` deliberately unchanged: none of the schema's three values encodes "freeware, mixed reservations", D-001 (Brad's, vetoable) calls the fixture PD, and the conservative reclassification to `no-license-grant-fetch-only` — which *tightens* handling to never vendor/mirror/re-host — is still a human call. Filed **W2-11a**. Gate: all seven exit 0, **621 passed / 0 failed / 5 ignored**, `licenses ok`.

2026-08-07 W4-01a done — **the triple buffer could hand a reader an older frame after a newer one.** Found while conductor-verifying W2-11, whose agent saw one failure, re-ran clean and reported "confirmed flaky". It is not flaky: reproduced at **3 failures in 60 runs** of `cargo test -p rf-core-api -- --test-threads=16` (a 40-run pass at default parallelism showed nothing, which is why it read as flakiness). **ROOT CAUSE, established by construction before touching code:** `publish` locked the slot, wrote the `Arc`, **released the lock**, then stored `latest`; `latest()` loads the index and only then locks that slot. A reader already blocked on the slot the writer is about to release takes it the instant it frees and reads the new frame while `latest` still names the *previous* slot — then its next call resolves that stale index to an older frame. With `latest=0` and slots holding (99, 97, 98): reader loads index 0 and is preempted; writer publishes 100→slot1, 101→slot2, 102→slot0; the waiting reader takes slot0 on release and sees 102 while `latest` is still 2; the next call loads 2 and reads 101. **Two alternatives refuted rather than assumed away:** shared test state (`last_seen` is a per-thread local) and weak memory ordering (Release/Acquire is the correct pairing — the defect is that the slot write and index store aren't atomic *as a pair*). **The invariant is right and the implementation was wrong, so the test was not weakened** — ARCHITECTURE §6 lets workers *lag*, i.e. miss intermediate frames; handing a render thread 102 then 101 is *rollback*, and would surface as visible stutter in the enhanced pipeline. Fix: store `latest` inside the slot's critical section, so the only way to observe a slot's new value is after that section ends, by which point `latest` already names it or newer. **THE SECOND HALF IS THE REAL LESSON:** the pre-existing test was a **weak guard**, and that is how this shipped past W4-01's own gate — 2 readers × 500 publishes caught the defect only ~1–5 runs in 60, which on a single CI run is indistinguishable from no guard. Resized to 6 readers × 4000 publishes and **measured**: 30/30 detection against the reverted fix, 0/60 failures with it. **Third instance of the same class in three tickets** — `rf_cache::fsutil`'s leaf-symlink branch exercised but not discriminated, rf-enhance's FM-11 watermark named for coverage it lacked, and now a concurrency test that detected its own race 5% of the time. All three passed their original gate. **For a concurrency test, "it passed" and "it would have caught the bug" are different claims, and only the second is worth anything.** Gate: all seven exit 0, **621 passed / 0 failed / 5 ignored**, `licenses ok`.

2026-08-07 W2-10 done — **RF-Scroller exists: an in-repo NES platformer built from source**, and the toolchain gap that stalled two W3-01 attempts was closed by probing before briefing. Multi-screen horizontal scroll with genuine nametable wraparound, a static HUD band split from the scrolling playfield, a documented column-RLE metatile level format (FORMAT.md), CC0 assets, deterministic build, and a Tier-A replay regression in the harness W2-11 built. Build is one command — `cl65 -t nes -o build/rf-scroller.nes --mapfile build/rf-scroller.map src/chr.s src/level_data.c src/main.c` — ROM `sha256=aa3b08c2…`, reproduced byte-identically from a fresh directory copy; the binary is never committed, only the hash (D-001). **DEVIATION FROM D-001's WORDING, flagged:** cc65's own `-t nes` target is used rather than neslib — they are different toolchains (neslib ships its own `crt0.s`/config and expects you *not* to use cc65's nes target), only the first was probe-verified, and it avoids vendoring third-party C into `fixtures/` entirely, which would need a licence review `cargo deny` structurally cannot perform. **Anti-vacuity:** 900 frames of held Right assert `player_x==752`, `camera_x==512`, `columns_streamed==95` — that last is load-bearing, since per FORMAT.md it can only exceed 63 once a physical nametable has genuinely been reused, so it witnesses *wraparound* rather than mere scrolling; a constant-zero log leaves `player_x` at its spawn 16. **The real engineering was a cycle-budget failure:** the original decode-and-blit-a-whole-column-per-frame design overran vblank by ~20,000 cycles, root-caused from actual PPU event timestamps after two single-cause theories were tested and *disproven*; fixed by `stream_chunk()`, a resumable RLE cursor doing 2 metatile rows/frame with movement paced so supply (2 rows/frame) exceeds demand (1.75). **TWO CONDUCTOR CORRECTIONS, BOTH FROM MEASUREMENT.** (1) **A documented defect that does not exist** — report and FORMAT.md both described the HUD split landing at scanline ~32–37 instead of 14–18 on streaming frames. Measured over the same 900 held-Right frames via `CoreEvent::Scanline` + `ScrollWrite` through rf-nes's ordered FIFO: **1682 mid-frame scroll writes, 100% inside 14–18** (916 at 16, 766 at 17), **zero later than 18**; the only split-free frames are 0–58 (warm-up). The earlier figures predate `SPLIT_DELAY`'s final calibration and were never re-measured. FORMAT.md corrected in place. **This direction of error is unusual but not harmless — a fixture exists to be a trustworthy regression target, so a defect it does NOT have misleads exactly as much as one it does;** W4-03d, W3-05 and W5-01 would have designed around a phantom. (2) **The "unexplained" PPUSTATUS bit-6 discrepancy is not an rf-nes bug** — the PPU clears sprite-0 hit at **dot 1 of the pre-render line**, which is *after* vblank ends, so during the vblank window this code runs in bit 6 is still set from the *previous* frame's hit and a bare "wait until set" exits instantly on the stale flag. Verified in `ppu/mod.rs`'s `PRERENDER_SCANLINE` arm, consistent with nesdev and the `sprite_hit_tests` 11/11 canary. SMB1 uses a two-phase wait for exactly this reason — **and that fix was implemented and measured rather than assumed:** it tightens the split (1631/1678 at scanline 15) but costs enough throughput that 900 frames reach only `player_x==528`, failing the anti-vacuity test, because streaming runs first so execution arrives *after* the hit and phase 1 blocks until the next pre-render. Reverted. **CRITERION 2 IS PARTIALLY MET:** sprite 0 is present and collides every frame and the mid-frame `$2005` rewrite is real (the first positive exercise of W4-03a's emission), but the split's *trigger* is a calibrated delay. **Fragility W2-10a must not inherit blindly:** the split lands correctly because preceding streaming work already carries execution to scanline ~16; `SPLIT_DELAY=12` is a trim, not the mechanism — so adding scenes changes the timing and W2-10a must re-measure. CI's cc65 step is **unverified from this environment** (apt package name, and whether Ubuntu's cc65 emits identical bytes) — first real CI run must confirm both. Gate: all seven exit 0, **622 passed / 0 failed / 6 ignored**, `licenses ok`.

2026-08-07 W4-03d done — **continuity-based scene identity, and a live crash in shipped W4-03a code.** The crash justifies the whole decision to block this ticket on a real fixture: driving `ScrollTracker::observe_frame` from an **actual running core** panicked at frame 711 of a 900-frame RF-Scroller session — `attempt to subtract with overflow` in `compute_bands`. **Conductor verified independently** — reverting the fix reproduces it at `scroll_tracker.rs:312` against the real fixture. Mechanism is named in `stepper.rs::run_until_next_frame`'s own doc: a single `Cpu::step` can legitimately drain up to ~4.5 scanlines belonging to the **next** frame when an OAM-DMA-triggering instruction straddles a frame boundary; `compute_bands` has no cross-call memory to notice a `Scanline` value regressed, so it closed the in-progress band with `end < start`. **Every prior test fed it a hand-authored, always-monotonic event log, so nothing could ever have caught it** — a live crash reachable by any real deployment, invisible to synthetic testing by construction. Fixed with `saturating_sub`, and that is right rather than a band-aid (checked, not accepted): the malformed band is leaked next-frame data, a 0-width band can never win the `max_by_key` primary-band selection, and Rust's `Range` semantics already make `start..end` empty when `start >= end`, so nothing downstream paints or positions from it. **`SceneTracker`** makes identity stateful — while ScrollTracker reports continuity (world delta ≤ 64px) and bank state is unchanged, the pinned id is reused untouched and `compute_scene_id` runs only at a discontinuity; 900 real frames → **1 scene id**, max per-frame delta 8px, matching RF-Scroller's real 8px/8-frame budget. **THE MEASURED BASELINE INVERTS W4-03b's NUMBER, and that is the finding.** Pre-ticket `compute_scene_id` over the same 900 real frames also gives **1** distinct id — not 36 per 150px. **Both figures were measuring the FIXTURE, not the algorithm:** W4-03b's synthetic texture had artificially high contrast (values in 0/96/176 thirds) so the signature was hyperactive, while real NES content produces block-average deltas never exceeding ~10, far under `EDGE_THRESHOLD`'s 48, leaving the signature all-flat for an entire level. The pre-flight instruction to *measure the real baseline before designing* is what surfaced this — designing against the synthetic 36 would have solved a problem that does not exist. **So "1 id" is VACUOUSLY good, and the agent proved it rather than reporting a win:** RF-Scroller and Alter Ego — unrelated games — hash to the *identical* `SceneId(7309415231882407843)`. No single threshold has a working operating point, since catching real ~10-unit content deltas would break the ~17–34-unit sprite/HUD-noise perturbation the existing watermark test depends on. That is a design gap in W4-03b's algorithm, not a tuning miss, and it was correctly **not** patched here. **ACCEPTANCE 2 IS BLOCKED AT THE FIXTURE LEVEL**, reported rather than force-fit — both available real ROMs are NROM with no bank state and both produce the degenerate signature; its intent is proven at unit level with legitimately-varying synthetic content, mutation-verified. **Severity checked, not assumed:** `canvas_cache_key` includes `rom_sha256` alongside the scene id, so a cross-ROM collision cannot load one game's canvas into another. **Proven: the continuity mechanism. Not proven: the disambiguation half, which is not merely unexercised but known-degenerate on real low-contrast content** — though a level transition would still yield a new scene via the scroll-discontinuity path, so the feature is not dead; and RF-Scroller being NROM with constant bank state leaves that half unvalidated too. Open follow-up: `compute_scene_id` needs discrimination that works on real NES palette-index ranges (a relative threshold scaled to the frame's own dynamic range, or nametable/attribute content rather than rendered indices) — and W2-10a's added scenes will widen RF-Scroller's contrast range, so re-measure after it lands. Mutations: forcing `discontinuous=true` killed both stability tests (1→48 and 1→28 ids); forcing ids equal killed the scroll-jump and bank-change tests; reverting `saturating_sub` reproduced the frame-711 panic. Gate: all seven exit 0, **631 passed / 0 failed / 6 ignored** (was 622), `licenses ok`.

2026-08-07 W4-03c done — **the enhanced pipeline renders arbitrary/ultrawide targets**, and the stitching arc is now complete except for being visible. `EnhancedCompositor` composites shell-resolved RGBA layers back-to-front into an arbitrarily sized target, clamped to the real GPU limit *before* any texture is created; `clamp_target_size` is a pure function unit-testable with a mocked limit and no GPU, and `TargetReduction` reports what was reduced so the shell can surface FM-13's toast. **THE CONDUCTOR BRIEF WAS WRONG AND THE AGENT CAUGHT IT** (fourth subagent correction this session). The brief said to read `adapter.limits().max_texture_dimension_2d` as if sufficient — it is not: wgpu's `Device::limits()` returns the limits **granted at device creation**, i.e. equal to the `required_limits` passed in, and `request_headless()` was passing `DeviceDescriptor::default()`, requesting the conservative 8192 regardless of hardware. So the adapter reported 16384 while the device enforced 8192, and clamping against the adapter alone **reproduced empirically the exact failure FM-13 exists to prevent**: "Dimension X value 16384 exceeds the limit of 8192". Fixed at both ends — `request_headless` now requests `required_limits: adapter_limits.clone()`, and `composite()` additionally defends with `adapter_limits.min(device.limits())` rather than trusting the two to stay in sync. **Conductor verified independently:** reverting only the first half fails the FM-13 test, and it fails via the defensive `min()` rather than a validation panic, which is the defence-in-depth working. Real observed `max_texture_dimension_2d` is **16384** (Apple M5 Max, Metal), not the 8192 this ticket's own pre-flight guessed as typical — exactly why the brief insisted on reading it rather than hardcoding. Shell-mediated boundary held: no rf-enhance dependency, layers arrive as plain `CompositeLayer` RGBA rather than a `SceneGraph`, and no handle registry was invented inside rf-renderer. Mutations, each reverted and reconfirmed: reversing draw order killed the overlap-ordering and alpha-blend tests **while both arbitrary-size tests still passed** (confirming it is order-specific, not a blanket break — which is what makes it a real discrimination test); removing the clamp killed the FM-13 test via a genuine wgpu validation panic at 20480 > 16384, the literal device-loss-shaped failure; forcing a fixed 256×240 target killed all four size-sensitive tests.
