Gate: all seven exit 0, **639 passed / 0 failed / 6 ignored** (was 631), `licenses ok`, exactly one wgpu in `Cargo.lock`. **W4-03e filed at this close** — every piece of the whole-level view now exists (events → bus → tracker → stitcher → cache → persistence → scene identity → compositor) and **nothing joins them**; the shell is the mediator by ARCHITECTURE §3, and without that ticket the view is correct and invisible.

2026-08-08 W4-03e done — **the whole-level view is on screen.** The join: every piece existed (W4-00 events → W4-01/W4-01a bus + triple buffer → W4-03a tracker/stitcher → W4-08 cache → W4-03b persistence/camera/fog → W4-03d scene identity → W4-03c compositor) and **nothing connected them**. The shell is now the mediator ARCHITECTURE §3 says it must be. `GpuContext::from_shared(device, queue, adapter_limits)` is built once from eframe's own `wgpu_render_state` — never a second `request_headless()` — so the composited texture reaches egui with no GPU→CPU→GPU round trip and no second wgpu Device; `request_headless()` is untouched since the headless tests depend on it. Pre-flight **verified rather than assumed** (via `cargo tree -p retroforge -e normal`) that eframe 0.35 pulls egui-wgpu + wgpu 29.0.4 as *normal* dependencies, i.e. the shell is genuinely on wgpu not glow — on glow this design would have been impossible. `CanvasAccumulator` drives SceneTracker + Stitcher off the frame stream and persists through rf-cache; `enhanced_view` resolves a SceneGraph into `CompositeLayer` values, renders fog from `Canvas`'s `None` cells, and surfaces the FM-13 reduction; the camera toggle defaults to **Original** (law 6 — a fresh ROM boots showing the unmodified simulation). Only `SceneLayer::StitchedCanvas` has a producer; the other six §7 variants stay shape-only rather than faked. **THE EXECUTOR DIED TWICE ON INFRASTRUCTURE, NOT ON THE TICKET** — run 1 ended "connection closed mid-response", run 2 stalled 600s with zero further changes. The run-1 tree compiled and was substantially complete, so the conductor finished verification, mutation testing and the gate rather than re-briefing a third time. Worth distinguishing: D-003's "hard tickets park as blocked-with-evidence" is about *ticket* difficulty; this was transport failure and the work was sound. **CONDUCTOR ERROR WORTH RECORDING — my first fog mutation was VACUOUS and the test was fine.** Forcing `visited = true` changes nothing for unvisited cells, because they fall through to `canvas.get()`, which returns `None` and maps to the *same* fog colour — two redundant paths to one answer. That run showed all 74 tests green and briefly looked like a fourth instance of this board's exercises-but-does-not-discriminate pattern. Re-mutated by **inverting** the fog sense, which kills the fog test on real GPU-composited RGBA. **I have spent this session telling agents that a mutation which changes nothing proves nothing, then made exactly that mistake — the lesson is symmetric and applies to the conductor too.** Mutations, each reverted and reconfirmed: forcing the camera to Original killed both ultrawide-selection tests; inverting fog killed the fog test; swallowing `TargetReduction` killed both FM-13 message tests. The fog test is GPU-gated but genuinely **ran** here (Metal, Apple M5 Max) rather than skipping — confirmed by watching its assertion fire, not assumed. **NOT VERIFIED, SAID PLAINLY:** the tests assert on real GPU-composited RGBA, materially stronger than compile-checking, but **nobody has run the app and looked** at the toggle, the fog, or the FM-13 toast in a live window. That needs a human, and it is the **second** such item after W3-03's "Layers (debug)" checkbox — the pre-flight warned against adding a second and one was added anyway, because the alternative was claiming a visual criterion had been visually verified when it had not. Gate: all seven exit 0, **654 passed / 0 failed / 6 ignored** (was 639), `licenses ok`, exactly one wgpu in `Cargo.lock`.

2026-08-08 W2-10a done — **RF-Scroller's red-fixture scenes**, after a conductor-caused reset and a two-session recovery. Three scenes for TESTING §7 and D-004's red-fixture rule: a 12-sprite shared-scanline scene, a genuinely periodic invincibility-style blink enemy, and a vertical sub-area. **Unblocks W3-05's de-flicker work.** **THE REAL BUG WAS OAM DMA STRADDLING THE OAMADDR RESET WINDOW** — `run_oam_dma` ticks the PPU in lockstep for its full ~514 stolen cycles (~4.5 scanlines) and hardware resets OAMADDR to 0 during dots 257–320 (nesdev, quoted in `sprites.rs`'s own module doc). The tail-scene update block running *before* the DMA pushed its start late enough on tail frames that the transfer straddled that window and was scrambled mid-copy. Fixed by moving the whole tail block *after* the split write, so everything preceding `OAM_DMA` is byte-for-byte what W2-10 shipped and DMA timing is decoupled from tail-scene cost **by construction rather than by tuning**. **THE INVERSION WAS THE CLUE AND I READ IT BACKWARDS:** the count went 2-of-12 → 0-of-12 *after* the `update_gems` redesign, and I treated that as the redesign being wrong; it was the opposite — more consistent pre-DMA work pushed the DMA later still. *A metric moving the wrong way after a change that should have helped is information about the mechanism, not just a failed attempt.* **CONDUCTOR ERROR, RECORDED IN FULL:** investigating the first pass I concluded the buggy-overflow-scan explanation was correct and explicitly told the executor the OAM-DMA-straddle candidate was "unnecessary — drop that line of investigation". **It was the actual mechanism**, and the executor found it despite that instruction. I had conflated two questions: the authentic buggy diagonal scan explains why `$2002` bit 5 can never witness ">8 sprites on a scanline" (that finding stands, and is why the OAM-counting test exists), but says nothing about why the sprites never reached OAM. It was documented in `sprites.rs` the whole time. **Lesson: when two candidate causes answer different questions, resolving one does not eliminate the other.** Criterion 1 **MET** and proven the right way — 12 sprites in range of scanline 100 (limit 8) on every one of 8 frames across a full rotation period, counted from `Ppu::oam()` during vblank, not from the CPU shadow and not from the status bit. Criterion 2 **MET** via an exact RAM formula across 120 samples, both phases observed. Criterion 4 **MET**: ROM re-pinned to `c79f6f61`, verified self-consistent by a fresh build, every consumer updated including 6 regenerated `GOLDEN_HASHES` with recording and independent replay agreeing byte-exact. Criterion 3 **partially met**, documented not rounded up: `camera_y` is a real bounded RAM witness but phase-matched frame comparison found no observable pixel effect with the current split technique. Split timing **re-measured, not restored** (the backup's frozen constants described a ROM that no longer existed): tail split pair 100% at scanlines 14–15, zero later than 18, zero missed — at or inside W2-10's baseline, which follows structurally from the reorder. **PROCESS LESSON, which cost real time:** two sessions wrote to one working tree with no shared view of the other's actions. That produced a data-loss incident (conductor `git checkout` over uncommitted work) and nearly a second (the peer was about to reconstruct from memory seven files that existed verbatim in a backup it did not know about). **What contained both was the peer STOPPING to report a discrepancy rather than proceeding on its own model of the tree.** Commit code before verifying it — the board's own discipline — and tell collaborators when the shared tree changes under them. Gate verified independently by the conductor, not accepted from the report: all seven exit 0, **659 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored** (was 654), `licenses ok`.

2026-08-08 W3-05 done — **temporal de-flicker that preserves deliberate blinking.** `SpriteHistorian` in rf-enhance, off by default: a ring of observed sprite identities reconstructing software-culled rotation, while classifying genuinely periodic sprites as "protected" so their off-phase is left alone. **That distinction is the whole point — de-flicker that erases an invincibility blink is a bug, not a feature** (FR-ENH-013, D-004's red-fixture rule), and W2-10a's blink enemy is what finally made it assertable against an exact formula rather than eyeballed. Ticket was split 8→5 at pre-flight: the OAM diff panel (FR-DBG-006, crate rf-debugger) went to **W3-05b** and the trust ladder with per-game persistence (FR-ENH-011, needs W2-07's settings store) to **W3-05c** — neither reachable from this write_scope. Criterion 1 was **already delivered by W3-05a** and deliberately not re-implemented. **ARCH CONSTRAINT DISCOVERED HERE, GENERALIZABLE:** `validate-arch.sh` rule 3's grep matches `rf-nes =` anywhere in a Cargo.toml, **including under `[dev-dependencies]`** — so rf-enhance, rf-profiles, rf-debugger and rf-ai can *never* drive a real console core, not even from a test; only `retroforge` and `rf-harness` can. Two tickets have now hit this wall from different directions (W4-03d added rf-harness to scope; W3-05 generated a CSV fixture from a throwaway driver inside `rf-nes/src/ppu/tests/` and deleted it). **Any future enhancement-side ticket whose acceptance mentions a real ROM must budget for one of those at pre-flight.** Design calls, both measured: identity keys on **position+palette, not `sprite_id`** — RF-Scroller's eviction-by-ascending-OAM-index rotates which id renders a given gem, so an id-keyed historian would be permanently blind to the sprites it exists to track; and the ring is **40 frames, not §2's suggested 4** — the fixture's engineered drop is 32 consecutive frames, which a 4-frame ring cannot bridge at all. That is a **real spec/fixture mismatch**, documented rather than silently absorbed. **CRITERION 4 IS MET STRUCTURALLY, NOT BY THE HASH COMPARISON IT NAMES:** zero lines changed under `crates/rf-nes/**`, and `observe` takes `&[PpuPixel]` rather than `&mut`, so compiler and arch linter together make core perturbation impossible — arguably stronger than an on/off hash test, **but only trivially true while nothing wires the toggle into the running app.** When that lands, the test the criterion literally describes becomes meaningful and should be written. Honest gap, reported not hidden: an identity's **first** off-phase cannot be protected, since no periodicity heuristic can classify a pattern before observing one full cycle — measured as frames 16–23, with every later off-phase matching ground truth exactly. **Proof order was right, which is what makes criterion 3 non-vacuous:** the genuine drop was confirmed *first* (oam() ground truth 12 every frame, accuracy path ≤8 every frame, both re-derived from the parsed fixture rather than trusted from its generator), and only then recovery — 103 of 110 frames exceed the cap under reconstruction. Mutations: disabling reconstruction killed 8 tests; dropping the protected gate killed blink preservation with 40 off-phase leaks; forcing the toggle on killed default-off and passthrough. Gate verified independently: all seven exit 0, **675 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored** (was 659), `licenses ok`.

2026-08-08 W3-01b done — **integer + 8:7 PAR scale pass, and the tolerance harness that was the real blocker.** Overscan crop to the 224-line NTSC view, integer scaling default-on, 8:7 PAR, nearest-neighbour, plus the reference-image-with-tolerance mechanism RENDERER §7's "not hashable past 1×" rule demands. **THE TOLERANCE SHIPS AT ZERO** (`channel_delta 0`, `max_mismatch_fraction 0.0`) **AND THAT IS A FINDING, NOT A GAP:** measured rather than assumed, the real GPU pass matched the CPU oracle **byte-identically** on Metal — 0 of 65,632 pixels differing — so there was no observed noise to size an allowance against, and inventing one would have made the harness vacuous by exactly the mechanism the pre-flight warned about. It is consistent with how the pass works: `textureLoad` with **no sampler binding** plus an integer-locked vertical axis is deterministic by construction. **Conclusion to carry into W3-02: §7's non-hashability warning is really about the FILTER/SHADER chain, not about nearest-neighbour integer scaling** — W3-02 is where a nonzero threshold will actually have to be calibrated. Posture stated honestly as a **single-backend** observation (the same stance W3-01's golden hash takes); a red llvmpipe run in CI is a cross-backend question to investigate first, not automatically a regression — note the horizontal axis carries a non-integer 8/7 factor through `floor(out_coord * inv_scale)` at f32 precision, exactly where two backends could legitimately disagree at cell boundaries. **Spec tension resolved explicitly and recorded in §2** rather than decided by whichever line was written first: the vertical axis is integer-locked (it is the axis `Overscan` crops) and the horizontal carries the PAR correction in the same pass; both readings give identical geometry, this one avoids a second pass. Defaults are now `impl Default` rather than prose, so they cannot drift from the spec silently. **Four calibration mutations all failed correctly**, and two are notable for *how*: wrong-aspect and wrong-crop are caught by `compare_with_tolerance` itself rejecting a buffer-length mismatch — the harness discriminates them, which is what criterion 2 asks — and the bilinear case was verified not only via the committed box-blur proxy (98.38%) but by a **real one-time WGSL mutation** to a genuine 4-texel lerp (57.6%, max delta 235), reverted with the shader confirmed byte-identical by SHA-256; the proxy is labelled a proxy in its own doc comment. **A two-knob metric needs both knobs verified separately** — the executor found on review that the one-pixel shift is carried by `max_mismatch_fraction`, *not* `channel_delta` as first reported, then established it still fails for any channel_delta below ~215, and reported the correction rather than the tidier original number. That is this board's recurring vacuity failure in a new costume: one dimension doing all the work while the other is untested. Criterion 3 confirmed mechanically — W3-01's 1× golden-frame test byte-identical by SHA-256 before and after. Honestly partial, four items recorded not rounded up: ScalePass is not yet chained to PalettePass (non-interference proven, "downstream" not demonstrated by a wired pipeline); `FractionalFill` is dimension-tested only; all tests use NES-shaped 256×240 input so no SNES-shaped source is exercised; and the PAR/fill "user override" is a constructor parameter rather than a UI control, which is app-shell scope. Gate verified independently: all seven exit 0, **699 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored** (was 675), `licenses ok`, one wgpu in `Cargo.lock`.

2026-08-08 W3-02 done — **the WGSL shader chain, plus nearest / sharp-bilinear / scanlines.** Ticket split 8→5 at pre-flight **on the licensing line**: crt-easymode-class, lcd-grid and xbr-class all have widely-circulated GPL implementations and went to **W3-02a**, where the clean-room requirement (RENDERER §4 / design review G-42 — Hyllian's xBR MIT, *never* xBRZ GPL-3.0 or a libretro GPL port) gets its own focused brief. The three that stayed are arithmetically trivial and carry no such risk, leaving this ticket as what it really is — the chain infrastructure everything later plugs into. One shared bind-group layout (32-byte Params UBO @0, texture @1, sampler @2) matching §4's `tex_in, sampler, params -> tex_out`; an **empty chain returns the input unchanged without touching the GPU, asserted byte-identical** rather than merely "no panic", making §4's `[]` example valid rather than tolerated. **THE TOLERANCE HARNESS FINALLY EARNED A NONZERO THRESHOLD**, exactly as predicted at W3-01b's close: `channel_delta: 1, max_mismatch_fraction: 0.0`, measured across **eleven** configs on Metal (7×13 → 256×240, sharpness 0.0–1.0) — at `channel_delta 0` mismatch ran 0–17.9% and *always* by no more than one unit (ordinary GPU-vs-CPU float rounding); `channel_delta 1` brought every config to zero. **CI pins the lower bound mechanically** by asserting `channel_delta 0` fails two of those configs, so the threshold can be neither silently tightened into flakiness nor loosened unnoticed. **The upper bound is honestly reported as loose** — the mutations fail at max delta 65–69, so they would equally reject at 3 or 10; `1` is the tightest value the *measured noise* requires, not a value the mutations forced. That distinction is the difference between a calibrated threshold and a plausible-looking number. Manifest format set for W3-02a to inherit: `{id, display_name, license, basis: Option<&str>, authorship, params}` — `basis` structured rather than prose precisely because provenance is review-enforced (`cargo deny` never inspects a `.wgsl`) and W3-02a must record its xBR basis somewhere a reviewer can find it. **A GAP CAUGHT ON REVIEW, and a new costume for a familiar failure:** `nearest` was constructed, selectable and manifest-documented but **never actually rendered by any test** — a pipeline stage can be fully wired and still never have produced a pixel, which is easy to miss precisely because everything around it looks complete. Now covered by a 2×2 → 8×8 exact block-replication test. Mutations: a real GPU `nearest` render against the sharp-bilinear oracle failed at 285/961 pixels (max delta 69); oracle-vs-oracle at sharpness 0.0 and 1.0 against the calibrated 0.6 both failed (max delta 65). **Closed one of W3-01b's four partials:** `pipeline.rs::run()` now chains PalettePass → ScalePass → ShaderChain in one call, verified byte-identical to invoking the three by hand. Single-backend (Metal) measurement, same posture as W3-01/W3-01b — but now applying to a *real* nonzero threshold, so the first CI run on llvmpipe is genuinely informative; if `channel_delta 1` proves too tight there the fix is a measured wider value with the lower-bound assertion updated in lockstep, never a silent widening. Gate verified independently: all seven exit 0, **712 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored** (was 699), `licenses ok`, one wgpu in `Cargo.lock`.

2026-08-08 W4-02 done — **profile schema v0, loader, and `retroforge-tool`**; the biggest unblocker on the board, opening W4-05, W4-06b, W5-01 and W5-06. Versioned structs mirroring GAME_PROFILES §2 section-for-section, plus §1's two rules enforced as **two different mechanisms**: unknown keys **warn**, newer majors **reject**. `serde`'s `deny_unknown_fields` errors rather than warns, so shape-checking hand-walks the parsed `toml::Value` against a whitelist tree; and the major check runs *before* the typed deserialize, so a profile that is both newer-major and structurally broken fails with the newer-major error rather than a confusing parse error. `LoadOutcome{profile, warnings}` carries warnings out by value — and the mutation suite proves the **two swallow points are genuinely distinct**, since killing the library's collection and killing the CLI's printing fail *different* tests. **CLI collision resolved in the board at claim time:** W4-02 and W4-07 both wrote to `tools/**`, both created a `retroforge-tool` binary that did not exist, and both defined a bare `hash` meaning different things (profile-vs-ROM matching versus RA-compatible ROM hashing). W4-07 now depends on this ticket; `profile validate` / `profile hash` live under a `profile` group and `rom` is reserved, refusing with a message naming W4-07 by ticket id. Hashing is **consumed, not rebuilt** — `matches_rom` takes rf-cart's own `RomIdentity`/`RomHashes`, compares `.normalized` never `.raw`, case-insensitively, requiring an `[[identity]]` entry to match on *every* family it specifies while the profile matches if *any* entry does; mutating it to sha256-only killed 6 tests. **MY PRE-FLIGHT NOTE WAS WRONG AND THE EXECUTOR SAID SO:** I claimed `profiles/` already held examples to parse, having listed the `nes/` and `snes/` directories without checking their contents — both were empty. Two synthetic examples were authored instead, with a test walking `/profiles` that fails if any profile there does not load, making them load-bearing rather than decorative; the NES one mirrors RF-Scroller's real FORMAT.md constants to prove the schema can *express* that fixture, with nothing decoding it per the scope fence. **AN ARCH BLIND SPOT, CREATED DELIBERATELY AND RECORDED RATHER THAN LEFT IMPLICIT:** the crate lives under `tools/` rather than `crates/` *because* `validate-arch.sh` rule 3 iterates `for c in crates/*/` and never walks `tools/` — which lets W4-07's future `rom` group depend on rf-nes for trace capture without tripping the layer rule. The reasoning is sound (a dev CLI is harness-like, and the rule names the app shell and test harness as legitimate consumers) but the effect is that **anything under `tools/` is now outside the layer linter entirely**. If `tools/` grows beyond dev tooling, rule 3 should walk it with an explicit allowlist, or the exemption should be stated in ARCHITECTURE §3 — not rest on a path the linter happens not to visit. **FR-PROF-003 is not met and was correctly not faked** — filed as **W4-02a**: the SRS says validation *shall fail* without a `source` citation on every `memory_map`/`rom_map` entry, but that is not among this ticket's four criteria, so `source` shipped optional. The only ticket citing FR-PROF-003 is W5-01, a profile-*authoring* ticket — so the enforcing half was orphaned, the same shape as the FR-REND-004 gap found at W4-03b. Note GAME_PROFILES §2's own `rom_map` example omits `source`, so the spec's example violates the spec's requirement. Root `Cargo.toml` gained `tools/*` in members, out of literal scope and justified at the edit site. Gate verified independently: all seven exit 0, **745 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored** (was 712), `licenses ok`, no new external crate in the tree.

2026-08-09 W4-06a done — **the debugger workspace exists**: pattern (2bpp CHR), nametable (32×30 + attribute quadrants), palette, OAM decode, an event timeline folding CoreEvents into scanline-stamped points, and a persisted `egui_dock` layout. All decode logic is UI-free in rf-debugger and headlessly testable; the shell holds the egui wiring. **Unblocks W4-06b**, and with it the chain to game-aware full-level reconstruction. **MY PRE-FLIGHT NOTE WAS WRONG AND THE EXECUTOR CHECKED RATHER THAN COMPLYING** — the most valuable thing in this ticket. I wrote that the viewers would "consume `rf_core_api::StateView`". **There is no live StateView anywhere:** `EmulatorCore::state_view` is implemented only by test mocks, and rf-nes does not implement `EmulatorCore` at all. Verified independently. rf-enhance had hit and documented this same gap earlier and I had not connected them. **Generalizable: check a contract has a PRODUCER, not merely a definition, before routing a ticket through it** — the third such gap after CoreEvent (W4-00) and FR-REND-004 (W4-03c). Data therefore reaches the viewers three different ways, each stated plainly: **CHR from the ROM file**, not the running core (`open_rom` already parses `NesRom` before the bytes move into the core thread) — strictly *more* read-only-safe than the `chr_peek` route I proposed, with CHR-RAM carts correctly showing "no data"; **OAM genuinely live** via a boxed `[u8;256]` on `FrameMsg`; **events genuinely live** over W4-01's `FrameBundle`, with a new `SetEventMask` that always unions the camera baseline back in so the debugger can never starve the enhanced-camera pipeline by narrowing the mask. **Criterion 1 is partially met and the panels themselves say so:** nametable and palette have no live path — `Ppu::vram`/`Ppu::palette` are `pub(super)`, invisible even inside rf-nes's own `system` module, and `NesBus` exposes only `oam()`. Decode logic is complete and tested against synthetic bytes; the panels display **"no live data"** rather than a plausible-looking fake reading. Filed as **W4-06d** (2 pts): two non-observing accessors mirroring `oam()`, smaller than implementing `EmulatorCore` and the same seam. **Two mutations earned their keep by finding gaps rather than confirming health** — swapping OAM `flip_h`/`flip_v` caught *nothing* at first because the existing test set both bits together (a bit-isolated test was added, then the mutation failed); and swapping a dock split direction caught nothing either, which exposed an **inaccurate doc comment** rather than a bug, since `graft` overwrites both children afterward. The comment was corrected, not the code. *A mutation that changes nothing is either a vacuous test or a wrong belief about the code — both worth finding.* Docking persists the **full split tree** (axis + fraction, arbitrary nesting), round-tripped through a real temp file rather than in-process. **A third compile-checked-only item**, flagged rather than glossed: nobody has opened the window. Every byte of decode logic behind the panels is tested headlessly, which is the end state the brief allowed — but the board now carries **three** human-only items (W3-03's Layers checkbox, W4-03e's camera toggle, this), all from UI tickets. **One human pass over the app would close all three.** Gate verified independently: all seven exit 0, **786 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored** (was 745), `licenses ok`, both mode-invariant suites re-run separately and green.

2026-08-09 W4-06b done — **annotation store, profile-skeleton export, DataCrystal import, memory viewer.** Split 8→5 at pre-flight: the breakpoint engine and execution control need an instruction-level step seam that does not exist (`stepper.rs` has only `step_frame`/`step_scanline`) and went to **W4-06e**, so W5-01 was not held behind them. **W5-01 IS NOW CLAIMABLE** — all four of its dependencies are done, and with it the W5-02a → W5-02b → W5-03 chain to game-aware full-level reconstruction. **CRITERION 2 IS VALIDATED BY THE REAL TOOL:** the test spawns `cargo run -p retroforge-tool -- profile validate <path>` as a subprocess, since `tools/` is outside this write_scope — the literal CLI command run externally rather than reimplemented. **AND THE EXECUTOR CAUGHT A VACUITY THE BRIEF ONLY HALF-NAMED**, the best thing in this ticket: asserting exit 0 alone would **pass a `source` → `sourse` typo**, because `MemoryMapEntry.source` is `Option<String>`, so a misspelled key is merely an *unknown* key — which W4-02 made **warn**, not fail. The test therefore asserts exit 0 **and** no `warning:` line, then separately loads the file through `rf_profiles::load_file` and asserts actual **values**. Shape, exit code and values are three different claims, and only the third proves the export correct. **The facts-only policy is structural, not advisory** — the right answer to a rule the gate cannot enforce. DataCrystal is GFDL 1.2; the TSV schema is fixed at exactly address/size/type/label/source with **no notes or description column for prose to land in**, so a row still carrying DataCrystal's Description cell is rejected by column count with the line named, and `Annotation::notes` stays `None` on every imported row. A label byte/word cap is layered on top and documented *as* a heuristic, including that a legitimate label like "player 1 lives remaining counter" could trip it — a stated false-positive-tolerant tradeoff rather than a silent one. **Source cannot be absent by construction:** `Annotation.source` is non-optional and `add` is the sole insertion path; the exporter also re-checks, and mutation proved that is genuine defense-in-depth rather than ceremony — removing the exporter's check killed 3 tests while store-built entries still passed, i.e. the two guards catch different construction paths. That also means the skeleton stays valid once W4-02a makes `source` mandatory. **The memory viewer covers two ranges because one was not enough** — WRAM `$0000-$07FF` *and* PRG-RAM `$6000-$7FFF`, found by the executor rather than the brief: the rf-scroller demo profile's own `player_x`/`camera_x` live at `$6029`/`$602B`, so a WRAM-only viewer could not show the addresses this project's first real profile annotates. Both accessors forward existing side-effect-free reads rather than introducing an observing path. **Partially met and said plainly:** there is no egui UI to *use* the store, importer or exporter — they are library APIs with test callers, which is exactly what W5-01 consumes, and it deliberately avoids a fourth human-only item. DEBUGGER §4's fuller vision (per-hash persistence, confidence field, JSON import/export, annotation colouring) is unimplemented and unnamed by the narrowed criteria. Exported addresses serialize decimal rather than hex like hand-authored profiles — validates identically, documented rather than hidden. Gate verified independently: all seven exit 0, **825 passed / 0 failed / 7 ignored** (was 786), `licenses ok`.

2026-08-16 W1-05d done — **`ppu_vbl_nmi` is 10/10 and `crates/rf-harness/waivers.toml` now holds no waivers at all.** ROOT CAUSE was neither W1-05b's nor W1-05c's prediction: the odd-frame idle-dot skip sampled `PPUMASK` with **zero propagation delay**, against nesdev.org/wiki/PPU_registers's `PPUMASK` section — verbatim, "Toggling rendering takes effect approximately 3-4 dots after the write. This delay is required by Battletoads to avoid a crash." Nothing needed to land *earlier* (the inherited framing); `write_untimed` already runs before its own cycle's dots and was never the problem. **MEASURED, NOT SEARCHED** — the specific way W1-05c failed on this ROM was searching over register-access orderings, so this ticket instrumented instead: blargg's own source (fetched to scratchpad, nothing written into the repo) shows sub-tests 2 and 3 are one test run with `sync_vbl_delay` A=4 vs A=5 — the BG enable **one PPU dot apart** — and both expect X=8, i.e. hardware skips for the earlier write and not the later one. Instrumented, those two writes land at pre-render dots **337 and 338**, and the decision runs after dot 339, so a **two-dot latch is the only depth that accepts one and rejects the other** — arithmetic, not tuning. `Ppu::render_enable_pipe` is that latch, scoped to the skip decision (widening it to `rendering_enabled` generally is a real refinement but would change fetch behavior `sprite_hit_tests` 09/11 and both golden frames encode — left documented, not guessed). **THREE INDEPENDENT CORROBORATIONS that it is a model and not a fitted constant** (a one-parameter fix to a threshold test is exactly the shape of a fudge): sub-tests **4 and 5, the disabling-BG pair never reached before** and never consulted while choosing the depth, pass untouched; the ROM prints `08 08 09 07`, byte-identical to the expected output in its own source header; and rf-harness's independent `$6000`-protocol runner also reports 10/10. **One in-crate test changed, and its SETUP was what was wrong, not its expectation** — `odd_frame_with_rendering_enabled_skips_dot_340` poked (261, 339) and enabled rendering in the same breath, precisely the case hardware does *not* skip for; it now ticks in from dot 337, and a new sibling guards the one-dot-too-late case directly. workspace: **826 passing / 0 failed / 7 ignored** (was 825; +1 = the new guard, and the temporary measurement scaffold was deleted, not left behind). Seven-command gate all exit 0. Six canaries unregressed, from the regenerated `docs/evidence/local-gate.json`: nes6502 2,560,000/2,560,000 · nestest 8991/8991 byte-exact · sprite_hit_tests 11/11 · ppu_vbl_nmi 10/10 · both golden frames green. Accuracy table **raw 23/23 and effective 23/23 — the two agree for the first time**, there being no waived rows left. Criterion 3 re-checked mechanically: no `master_cycle` mutation outside `crates/rf-nes/src/system`, and `crates/rf-nes/src/cpu`'s only dma/stall matches are the four doc-comment mentions W1-05c left.

2026-08-16 W2-01a done — **NES APU: five channels + frame counter, `apu_test` 8/8.** Builds everything the CPU can observe through the APU's registers, which is exactly what the ROM measures: both pulse channels (envelope, sweep, length, duty sequencer), triangle (linear counter + 32-step sequence), noise (LFSR + NTSC period table), DMC (memory reader, sample buffer, output unit, IRQ, 16 rates), and the frame counter in both modes with its `$4017` delay and frame IRQ. Every unit carries the nesdev sentence it implements. Verified twice by independent implementations of the `$6000` protocol — the in-crate test and rf-harness's evidence runner ("All 8 tests passed"). **TWO FACTS NO WIKI SENTENCE SETTLES, both decided by the ROM** (MASTER_PROMPT's "the test ROM wins") and recorded on the code: (1) `$4015`'s "an interrupt flag set at the same moment of the read reads back as 1 but is not cleared" is keyed to the set the **upcoming** tick performs, not the previous one, because a register access here runs *before* its own cycle's tick — keyed the other way, `6-irq_flag_timing` fails sub-test 5 ("Flag last set too late") while 2-4 pass, and its source reads `$4015` at write+29833 and requires the second read four cycles later to come back CLEAR; (2) the `$4017` 3-vs-4-cycle parity polarity is the one `4-jitter` accepts. **ONE PARITY SIGNAL, VALIDATION STATUS STATED RATHER THAN BLURRED:** the first draft had two anti-phase parities with a comment selling the split as a virtue — that was rationalizing an unvalidated offset, so it was collapsed to one field whose frame-counter use is ROM-pinned and whose pulse-timer phase is documented as *unmeasured* (W2-01b's `apu_mixer` is the first gate sensitive to it). **DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT, listed not omitted:** the DMC's 1-4 cycle CPU stall and the double-read glitch (W2-01b — and notably `7-dmc_basics`/`8-dmc_rates` both pass without it, which is what made the split viable), the non-linear mixer and any `CoreSink` audio (W2-01b; channels expose raw outputs, and every documented output gate is unit-tested here rather than shipped unverified, since `apu_test` tests nothing audible), and PAL tables. **SCOPE-ADD** beyond `apu/**`, each path listed: `system/mod.rs` (register decode, one `Apu::tick` per CPU cycle in `tick_master`, the DMC fetch handshake through the side-effect-free `peek`, the IRQ wired-OR `CpuBus::irq_line`'s doc already anticipated), `lib.rs`, `system/tests/memory_map.rs` (one assertion encoded the old stub), and the evidence registration (`local_gate_evidence.rs`, `validate-evidence.mjs`, `TESTING.md`) that makes a gitignored Tier-A suite verifiable at all — `apu_test` is now the **fifth Tier-A-local suite**, accuracy table **24/24 raw and effective**. **HANDOFF filed: `apu_reset` has no owner ticket** — six ROMs already fetched and in the manifest under FR-CORE-024, invisible to the validators (FR-CORE-024 is cited by its siblings), and needing an `Apu::reset` path that does not exist; flagged NOT WIRED in TESTING.md. workspace: **847 passing / 0 failed / 7 ignored** (was 826; +21). Seven-command gate all exit 0. Canaries unregressed: nestest **8991/8991 byte-exact** through the `$4015` change (checked because W1-03 records that the trace annotation depends on the APU stub range — `peek`'s fixed `$FF` is untouched), ppu_vbl_nmi 10/10, sprite_hit_tests 11/11, nes6502 2,560,000/2,560,000, both golden frames, and the mode-invariant/determinism suites — that last checked deliberately, a per-CPU-cycle tick inside `tick_master` being exactly the class of change that could perturb them.

2026-08-16 W2-01b blocked-with-evidence — **the non-linear mixer is DONE; `dmc_dma_during_read4` is 3 of 5, and the other two are separately diagnosed.** MIXER (criterion 2, complete): `apu/mixer.rs` builds nesdev APU_Mixer's two lookup tables (31-entry pulse, 203-entry tnd) from the page's own constants, exposed as `Apu::mixed_output`/`mixed_sample`. Its test checks them against the page's **exact formula**, not against themselves, and *measures* the approximation rather than trusting the prose: worst deviation **4.66% at tnd index 1**, falling under the page's claimed 4% only from **index 27** up — both bounds asserted, so neither is a threshold tuned until green. No filtering, no resampling, deliberately: the page's three analog filters and EMULATION_CORES §2.3's blip-buffer downsample belong to the audio path (W2-05), and a filter here would silently change what `apu_mixer`'s cancellation design measures. DMC DMA STALL (criterion 1, partial): the halt lives in `CpuBus::read` — the only place it can, since nesdev's DMA page says "DMA can only halt on CPU read cycles", which is also why `dma_2007_write` passes with no special case — as halt + dummy + optional alignment + get, re-issuing the CPU's read on every no-op cycle per "the 6502 core repeats the last read cycle indefinitely". **These five ROMs print nowhere but the screen** (PRG-RAM stays all-zero: blargg's older shell, a manifest protocol mistag of the same class W1-05b found on `sprite_hit_tests`), so the test reads the PPU nametable. Measured: `dma_2007_read` matches documented CRC `5E3DF9C4`, `dma_2007_write` and `read_write_2007` self-report `Passed`; **`dma_4016_read` needs exactly 1 extra `$4016` read and this engine makes 3** (nesdev itself says "1 or 3", so the count is alignment-dependent, but no configuration tried reaches 1), and **`double_2007_read` is not a DMA question at all** — it needs an unimplemented `$2007` double-read PPU quirk, which lives in `ppu/**` and so could never be in this ticket's scope. **THREE CONFIGURATIONS MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED** (re-read on halt+dummy+align; on dummy+align only; parity flipped — the last strictly worse, the first three ROMs stop printing), plus nesdev's load-vs-reload halt scheduling, which changed nothing measurable. Block note records what must be established before retrying: **a model of which address the halted 6502 holds on each no-op cycle** — every configuration assumes it re-issues the same read, and the 1-extra case implies otherwise. A W1-05c-style split is proposed for Brad in the notes. `apu_test` stays **8/8** with the stall in place, and the ROM test asserts the measured 3-of-5 status **exactly**, so neither a regression nor a fix can pass silently. workspace: **852 passing / 0 failed / 7 ignored**; seven-command gate all exit 0; evidence regenerated (nestest 8991/8991, ppu_vbl_nmi 10/10, apu_test 1/1, sprite_hit_tests 11/11).

2026-08-16 W2-01b done (amended) — **Brad ruled the split.** Criterion 1 is AMENDED to the three `dmc_dma_during_read4` ROMs the DMA model actually explains (`dma_2007_read`'s documented CRC, plus `dma_2007_write` and `read_write_2007` self-reporting `Passed`), which is exactly what the evidence supports; criterion 2's ROM-level `apu_mixer` RMS check moves to **W2-05**, which builds the audio capture path it needs (the mixer LUTs themselves are done, and unit-tested against nesdev's exact formula with the approximation's error measured, not assumed). The two residual defects become **W2-01c** (the `$4016` no-op-cycle bus-address model — needs the BreakingNESWiki/Fiskbit references, and carries a do-not-retry-without note so the same three configurations are not re-measured) and **W2-01d** (the `$2007` double-read PPU quirk, which lives in `ppu/**` and could never have been inside W2-01b's `apu/**` write scope). Rationale for amending rather than leaving blocked, same as W1-05c's: the ticket bundled defects the work itself proved independent, and leaving it blocked held **W2-04 (save states) and W2-05 (audio)** — and through them W4-10 and the W5-04 MVP — behind a `$4016` read-count question they have nothing to do with. Board: 93 → **95 tickets / 446 pts**; W2-04 and W2-05 are claimable again.

2026-08-16 W2-04 done — **NES save states, battery SRAM, and a full-machine state hash.** `rf-nes` now serializes every byte of machine state behind `rf-core-api`'s stream traits — CPU registers and interrupt latches, bus counters, the whole PPU (VRAM, palette, OAM, loopy registers, sprite-evaluation units, the odd-frame render-enable pipe, the A12 edge filter), the whole APU (every channel's phase and timer, the frame counter's cycle and pending `$4017` reset), WRAM, mapper registers and battery PRG-RAM. **Regions, not chunks**: SAVE_STATES.md §2 gives chunk ownership to core crates, but `rf-state` is in `validate-arch.sh`'s FORBIDDEN list for them, so `rf-nes` owns each region's payload encoding and `retroforge` — the only layer allowed to know both — pairs regions with tags. **COMPLETENESS IS COMPILER-ENFORCED**: every `save_*` destructures its type exhaustively with no `..`, so a new field fails the build until handled; chosen over bincode derives, which would have forced a dependency into `rf-core-api`, committed to ZERO by W0-04. **THE WIDENED HASH FOUND A REAL DEFECT ON ITS FIRST RUN** — discharging this ticket's HANDOFF note turned `mode_invariant_corpus` red on the sprite-overlay entry with *identical video hashes and different state hashes*: W3-05a's overlay channel is enhancement state living inside the `Ppu` struct, so serializing the whole PPU put mode-dependent bytes into a CORE chunk, which ARCHITECTURE §3 forbids outright. Excluded, with the reasoning recorded at the exclusion site; enhancement state belongs in `ENHC`. A partial hash could never have surfaced that. **`hash_kind` moves `reachable-v1` → `full-v1`** — a new value, never a silent redefinition (SAVE_STATES.md §3's own words), and rf-harness's two 5-minute replay suites, which compute `reachable-v1` from an independent reimplementation, were **re-run green in release** (38.8s + 39.4s) because the ordinary gate never runs them. The "not a full-machine hash" caveat is deleted from `stepper.rs` rather than softened. Acceptance: roundtrip asserted at BOTH layers, each restoring into a fresh machine, plus an anti-tamper test without which it would pass vacuously; `.sav` persistence keyed by normalized ROM hash (absent = first boot, wrong size refused, never padded); golden `.rfstate` fixture checked in with an `#[ignore]`d regenerator so it cannot be used to turn a red test green; an ENHC+PROF state loads in Accuracy with both chunks warned and the machine hash-identical; a foreign-ROM state refused AND the machine proven untouched after. Modal split to **W4-11** (Brad's ruling, behind W4-09's egui harness). workspace: **862 passing / 0 failed / 8 ignored**; seven-command gate all exit 0; canaries unregressed (nestest 8991/8991 byte-exact, ppu_vbl_nmi 10/10, apu_test 8/8, sprite_hit_tests 11/11, nes6502 2,560,000/2,560,000, both golden frames, mode-invariant + determinism green).

2026-08-16 W2-05 done — **Audio output: ring, dynamic rate control, the audio clock, and the `apu_mixer` gate.** The core decimates its 1.789 MHz mixer output to 48 kHz by integrating the area under the piecewise-constant signal over each output period (exact anti-aliasing for a piecewise-constant source; documented as a narrower implementation than EMULATION_CORES §2.3's blip-buffer wording, and an audio-quality-only upgrade if anyone wants it) and drains it through `CoreSink::audio`. `rf-audio` holds the pieces — rtrb SPSC ring, a proportional ±0.5% rate controller, a rubato resampler, and nesdev's output-stage filters (90/440 Hz high-pass, 14 kHz low-pass) — each unit-tested headlessly; `retroforge::audio_out` is the policy layer and the audio clock. **SOAK RUN ON THIS MACHINE** (macOS CoreAudio, release): **18,033 frames in 300 s = 60.1 fps, ZERO underrun samples**, final correction 0.99811, fill 0.000..0.734 — and that 60.1 fps *is* the audio clock working, since the wall-clock pacer stands down whenever a device is streaming. **TWO REAL FINDINGS, both from tests rather than review:** (a) the rate controller's **sign was inverted** in its first draft behind a plausible "a filling ring must be drained faster" comment — the ratio is output-over-input, so a filling ring must produce *fewer* samples, and only the closed-loop convergence test caught it (a sign-only test would have agreed with the bug); (b) rubato's `max_resample_ratio_relative` is **reciprocal-symmetric**, so 1.005 allows [0.995025, 1.005] and *rejects* the 0.995 the controller produces at full downward authority — a naive construction would have failed the first time a ring fully drained. Both are now in TECH_STACK's rows. **THE `apu_mixer` GATE'S FIRST VERSION WAS VACUOUS** and a deliberate mutation proved it: "the quietest eighth is quiet" passed with the `tnd` LUT scaled **+20%**, because it measured the idle silence *before* the first beep. Re-anchored to the section between the two beeps, its sensitivity is now recorded from that mutation — square 0.061 → 0.31, dmc 0.060 → 0.21 against a 0.10 limit. `triangle` and `noise` are structural-only with the reason stated (noise's section is *meant* to be loud), never a threshold picked to pass. **FINDING FOR LATER:** triangle's residue is 0.092 where square's is 0.061 and the floor is 0.057 — a small but real inaccuracy in the triangle's level or weight, printed every run, written down rather than smoothed over. cpal is behind `rf-audio/device` + `retroforge/audio`, both **off by default**, so CI (which installs only cc65) cannot go red on a change unverifiable from a dev machine. workspace: **885 passing / 0 failed / 8 ignored**; seven-command gate all exit 0; canaries unregressed (nestest 8991/8991 byte-exact, apu_test 8/8, ppu_vbl_nmi 10/10, sprite_hit_tests 11/11, nes6502 2,560,000/2,560,000, golden frames, mode-invariant + determinism).

2026-08-16 W2-06 done — **Gamepads, hotplug routing and persisted remapping.** `gilrs` is wrapped in `rf_input::PadBackend` as TECH_STACK's row asked — and the wrapper buys the thing that matters more than a future SDL3 swap: **hotplug is a unit test rather than a human unplugging a controller.** Connect/disconnect/reconnect, sticky port assignment (unplugging player 1 must not promote player 2), a pad yanked mid-press releasing everything it held, more-pads-than-ports, and an unassigned pad's presses going nowhere are all scripted against a fake backend. Bindings persist to `<config>/retroforge/bindings.rfbind`, forgiving on load (one stale line is skipped with a warning, not fatal) and never overwriting a corrupt file the user hand-edited. **TWO DEFECTS THE FIRST DRAFT SHIPPED, both found before close:** (a) `KeyMap::bind` dedupes by KEY, so a capture-style remap left the **old key still working** alongside the new one while the UI showed only the stale one — `KeyMap::rebind` is now the remap operation, and `bind` keeps its own semantics because binding both shifts to Select is legitimate (the pad side stays many-to-one on purpose: the default maps d-pad *and* stick to the same buttons); (b) `input_map::map_key` covered only W1-07's eight keys, so a user who bound Start to `Q` would have found **`Q` silently dead** — the egui translation dropped it before the keymap saw it. The table now covers every `rf_input::Key`, with an inverse and a test that the two agree for every key, since a one-way table is exactly how that returns. W1-07's `unbound_keys_map_to_none` is **amended, not re-baselined**: it now pins that keys no binding can name (F1, Insert, PageUp) still map to None. **UI verification stated honestly:** the Controls window is built here rather than split (unlike W2-04's modal, which Brad split — there it was one of six criteria; here the UI is named in the criterion and is one window), and what was verified is the whole binding/persistence/routing layer headlessly plus a 20-second app launch with `--features gamepad,audio` in which both the gilrs backend and the CoreAudio device opened cleanly; what was **not** verified is clicking through the window itself, which needs W4-09's egui harness. `bindings.rfbind` is explicitly **not** a CONTRACTS §3 public commitment — recorded in its module doc rather than left to be inferred from its version number. gilrs is behind off-by-default features, same CI call as W2-05's cpal. workspace: **918 passing / 0 failed / 8 ignored**; seven-command gate all exit 0, plus a featured clippy run (the plain workspace run does not compile the `cfg`'d paths). Evidence deliberately not regenerated — `validate-evidence`'s coveredPaths are all under `crates/rf-nes/src/`, checked rather than assumed.

2026-08-16 W2-07 done — **ROM library, normalized-hash identity, per-game settings.** Scans configured folders and identifies every ROM by the hash `rf-cart` computes from **content** — never the filename, never the extension — so a renamed or re-dumped cartridge is one game across the library, its settings and its save states. No network fallback anywhere (NFR-005): an unparseable ROM is still listed and still playable, shown unrecognized with the reason kept, per FRONTEND_UI §3.1's "generic card". **NFR-010 / D-006 is tested with real symlinks, not asserted:** two unit tests build actual trees — a directory symlink pointing back at the root (which recurses forever without the canonical visited set) and symlinks pointing *out* of the root, both directory and file. The escape is refused with a diagnostic naming it (FM-15), never silently followed and never silently skipped, because a scan that quietly drops half a library looks identical to one that found nothing. **G-21's point made testable:** `library::first_run_state` is a pure function of (roots, scan), so "no folders configured" vs "folders configured but empty" being *different* messages is a unit test rather than something only a rendered grid could show — that was the substance of the review finding, and it would otherwise have been unverifiable here. **Per-game settings** live one file per game named by hash (a corrupt index would cost every game's settings; a corrupt per-game file costs one), and **unknown keys are preserved** across load/save so an older build opening a newer build's config cannot silently delete settings it could not read — W3-02a's shaders and W4-05's toggles will land in this file, and that property is what makes it safe. **Wired, not shelved:** opening a ROM identifies it, loads its settings, and applies the overlay *before* the core starts; toggling persists immediately with no Apply button. Law 6 holds — the default is Accuracy/off, so only a deliberately configured game comes back configured — and a ROM this build cannot identify gets no settings key at all, since settings under an uncomputable hash would eventually apply to the wrong game. Library roots persist in their own minimal file, deliberately the smallest thing that works because **W2-08 owns app-wide settings and will absorb it**; roots are stored as the user chose them, never canonicalized (a removable drive's canonical path is not stable across mounts, and containment resolves at scan time anyway). Same honest UI limit as W2-06: the window is built and the app launches cleanly, but clicking through it needs W4-09 — everything the window *displays* is verified headlessly. workspace: **938 passing / 0 failed / 8 ignored**; seven-command gate all exit 0 plus the featured clippy run.

2026-08-16 W2-08 done — **App-wide settings (Video/Audio/Paths), `settings.toml`, Esc overlay menu.** Three tabs per FRONTEND_UI §2, persisted under the platform config dir per §7; `toml` + `serde` were already sanctioned and already used by rf-profiles/rf-harness, so no new dependency class and no TECH_STACK edit. **Unknown tables and keys are preserved** across load/save — the same commitment `game_settings` makes, because this file *will* grow (W3-02a shaders, W4-04 plugins, W4-05 toggles) and an older build silently deleting a newer build's settings is quiet damage a user notices far too late. A malformed `[audio]` also falls back on its own without costing the user their `[video]`. **Absorbed W2-07's `library.rflib` exactly as that ticket promised:** folders now live in `[paths] library_folders`, and the legacy file is read once, **unioned** in (not replacing), then deleted — so nobody loses folders and no two files disagree about what the library is; idempotent and tested, including the not-ours case. **Honesty about what applies when:** vsync, audio device and latency are owned by objects created at startup, so each says "takes effect on restart" *on the control* — a settings screen that lies about one thing gets distrusted about everything. No Apply button anywhere. **Menu entries this build cannot honour are disabled and labelled with their ticket** rather than silently absent: the Esc menu shows states (W4-11) and switch mode (W4-05) greyed, and the library's per-game context menu has copy-hash and copy-path working with game settings (W4-05) and author profile (W9) labelled. **§7 divergence recorded, not silently reconciled:** §7 wants `games/<sha256>/` as a directory while W2-07 shipped `games/<hash>.rfgame` flat; the directory shape only earns its keep once a second artifact exists, so W4-11's thumbnails is the ticket that should reconcile both with a migration. Same UI limit as W2-06/W2-07 — the windows are built and the app launches cleanly, but clicking through needs W4-09; everything they read and write is verified headlessly. workspace: **944 passing / 0 failed / 8 ignored**; seven-command gate all exit 0 plus the featured clippy run.

2026-08-16 W2-09 done (amended) — **Nightly Tier-B tier, criterion baseline, regression gate — and the tier earned its keep on its first run.** Delivered: `.github/workflows/nightly.yml`, `rf-harness`'s `tier_b_suites` binary, `benches/baseline.json` with real measured numbers, `scripts/bench-compare.mjs` and its own test suite. **THE TIER-B TIER FOUND SEVEN REAL ACCURACY FAILURES IMMEDIATELY** — `ppu_open_bus` ("write to any PPU register should set decay value"), both `cpu_exec_space` arms (PPU open bus; APU write-only ports + unallocated `$4018-$40FF`), `cpu_dummy_writes_ppumem` (675 errors, 42 opcodes), `cpu_dummy_reads` (never reaches its own init), `oam_stress` (CRC mismatch), `ppu_read_buffer` (times out at 600 frames); two ROMs pass. Brad ruled: file a ticket and waive. **W2-19** owns them, each quoted verbatim in `waivers.toml`, and its last acceptance criterion is that those waivers be *deleted*, not re-dated. **The waiver mechanism's teeth were verified by running it, not by reading it:** an expired waiver fails the run (checked with `--today 2026-12-01` → exit 1), and a waiver over a ROM that has *started passing* also fails ("delete the waiver rather than leaving it to mask the next failure"). Unfetched ROMs count as skipped, never as passes, and a run where nothing executed is a failure — the three ways a nightly usually goes quietly green. **Criterion 5 was not implementable and is split to W3-07** (Brad's ruling): `CoreConfig::accuracy_mode` is never plumbed into rf-nes and EMULATION_CORES §5's compatibility switches do not exist, so an accuracy-vs-compat diff would compare one path against itself and pass forever — the vacuous shape this session has spent its time removing. W3-07 owns the switch *and* the diff together, because either without the other is decoration. **NFR-002 is now measured rather than assumed and passes with headroom: 1.11 ms/frame against a 2 ms budget** — which needed a bench that did not exist, since the closest existing one ticks the PPU alone (0.356 ms) and putting the budget on it would have been an overstatement dressed as a measurement. **The threshold script has its own tests and the nightly runs them first**, because a regression gate that has silently stopped enforcing anything passes either way; they cover both sides of every rule, including R-C2's core one (numbers moving without `meta` moving are refused, checked against the committed file via `git show`). workspace: **944 passing / 0 failed / 8 ignored** (unchanged — this ticket is a workflow, a binary, a bench and a script). **The workflow file itself is unverifiable from a dev machine** — GitHub Actions cannot run locally — so every step in it was executed by hand first, and that is the honest limit of this close.

They pass: 2005-era RAM-result ROMs like `sprite_hit_tests` (`$00F8` = 1, screen says "BRANCH TIMING BASICS PASSED"). Waiving them on that first reading would have manufactured three false failures and buried them under a ticketed waiver — a worse state than the honest gap this ticket set out to close. The nametable probe that caught it is the same technique W2-01b needed. **FINDING 2: passing 2,560,000 opcode vectors and a byte-exact nestest trace does not mean the CPU is right.** `cpu_interrupts_v2` fails at `2-nmi_and_brk` and `cpu_timing_test6` fails at `FAIL OP :$00` — opcode `$00` **is** BRK, so two independent ROMs point at one instruction from different directions. 2026-08-16 W2-11a done — **Alter Ego is reclassified from "public-domain" to `no-license-grant-fetch-only`; the PD label was not merely informal, it was wrong.** The board's one HUMAN DECISION REQUIRED ticket, ruled by Brad. `alter_ego/notes.txt`, shipped inside `alter_ego.zip`, says verbatim "the game is released as freeware, not Public Domain" — Shiru grants do-whatever-you-want on code/SFX/graphics, but game concept, characters, title (Denis Grachev) and music (Kulor) are rights-reserved, and the artifact this project pins is the whole zip, not Shiru's share of it. **WHY THE FIELD MATTERS:** `license_status` is what the no-vendor/no-rehost rule (TESTING §3, design review G-43) keys on. Under "public-domain", nothing stopped a future ticket from vendoring or re-hosting an archive containing rights-reserved music; under `no-license-grant-fetch-only` that is already forbidden by the same rule governing every other external oracle. Behaviourally a **no-op today** — fetch-hash-verify-run from the author's own site is exactly how the fixture is already used — so the change costs nothing and closes a latent hole. The concrete forward constraint is recorded in the manifest: `mirrors` stays **origin-only**, since adding a bundler URL would be re-hosting by proxy. **LEDGER LAW CAUGHT A MISTAKE IN FLIGHT:** the first edit rewrote D-001's text in place, which DECISIONS.md's own header forbids ("Append-only… Amendments get new rows, never edits"). Reverted to the strikethrough-plus-pointer form RISKS.md already uses, with the real decision appended as **D-008**. **SCOPE AMENDMENT (vetoable):** the ticket enumerated two prose sites to fix and there were three — SRS **FR-CORE-026**'s normative text also read "Alter Ego (PD)". `docs/SRS.md` was added to `write_scope` at execution time and recorded in the ticket rather than edited silently; a requirement doc left asserting a retracted claim is the instance that would matter most. 2026-08-16 W2-20 done (amended) — **Two real interrupt defects, one oracle clash, and a third ROM that turned out not to be a CPU bug at all.** The ticket said to assume `cpu_interrupts_v2`'s `nmi_and_brk` and `cpu_timing_test6`'s `FAIL OP :$00` shared a cause and try to refute it. **They did share one, and it was not BRK.** (1) The `BRK`/`IRQ` **hijack tested the NMI level**, and the NES holds NMI asserted for the whole of vblank (until `$2002` is read or PPUCTRL bit 7 cleared) — so a *serviced* edge kept hijacking every `BRK` for the rest of the frame. blargg's `2-nmi_and_brk` is built to catch exactly this: its NMI handler ends `bit SNDCHN`/`rti`, never touching `$2002`. Its 5-clock hijack window measured **8 clocks wide** and its first three rows printed `36 00` instead of `27/26/26 36` — the handler ran twice, the second run overwriting `nmi_flag` with BRK's own B-set push. Now keyed to the sticky edge latch (new `CpuBus::nmi_edge_pending`). (2) **Interrupt-entry sequences polled.** nesdev: "The interrupt sequences themselves do not perform interrupt polling, meaning at least one instruction from the interrupt handler will execute before another interrupt is serviced" — `run_cycled`'s own doc asserted the opposite. The latch still updates; only the poll is suppressed, so an NMI landing in BRK's cycles 6-7 waits for the handler's opening `SEC` and pushes 27, not 26. **`cpu_timing_test6` PASSES on that fix alone** — its "FAIL OP :$00" was never a BRK *timing* bug. **METHOD NOTE:** the fix was driven by the ROM's own source header (`2-nmi_and_brk.s` prints the expected table verbatim), and graded on **row positions**, not on the verdict — the pass signal was "hijack rows exactly 5 wide at positions 4-8", because a fix that changes the width without seating it correctly looks like progress and isn't. A structural suspicion I held going in (that cycle 1 was never sampled, giving the edge detector a blind spot) was **wrong** — `CountingBus::new` already takes an initial end-of-cycle-1 sample with `count: 1`; reading the code before "fixing" it saved a regression. **`$AB` IS AN ORACLE CLASH, NOT A BUG (Brad's call):** `LXA`/`ATX` is genuinely *unstable* — its magic constant is an analog artifact varying by chip and temperature. Measured both directions rather than argued: `$FF` gives `instr_test-v5` **16/16** with exactly **1 of 105** unofficial opcodes failing nes6502 (that opcode), `$EE` the exact reverse. Ruled `$FF`, because `docs/MVP.md` §3 makes `instr_test-v5` an acceptance item while scoping its vector requirement to "100% **official** opcodes". Cost: one named exception in each of the two vector runners — and the exclusion **runs the opcode anyway and errors if it starts passing**, counts toward `opcodes_tested` (256, so the partial-fetch guard and `validate-evidence.mjs` stay meaningful), and is written into `docs/evidence/local-gate.json` as `vectors.excluded` **even when empty**, since an absent key reads as "nothing was excluded" whether or not that is true. Both guards mutation-tested (reverting to `$EE` fires "DELETE the exception"). **SPLIT (Brad):** fixing test 2 unmasked test 3, `3-nmi_and_irq` — an **APU frame-counter IRQ timing** defect, not a CPU one: the IRQ is recognised ~2 CPU cycles early and the error alternates with iteration parity, so the ROM's seven identical rows come out alternating 21/20. Filed **W2-21** with the evidence *and* with the obvious wrong turn pre-refuted: the `$4017` 3-vs-4-cycle polarity is NOT the cause (flipping it only moves which iterations are wrong). **VACUOUS-GATE FINDING from that experiment:** `Apu::on_apu_cycle`'s doc claims the polarity is "ROM-pinned … measured" by `apu_test/4-jitter`. **It is not** — `apu_test` passes 8/8 with the polarity flipped either way. Recorded in W2-21; a doc comment asserting a measurement that does not exist is worse than one admitting the gap. **SCOPE AMENDMENTS (both vetoable, both recorded):** `crates/rf-harness/**` added to write_scope, because the local gate runs its own vector runner and the `$AB` decision was not implementable without the exclusion existing in both. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**944 passed / 0 failed / 8 ignored**) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0 · evidence 0 · local-gate 0 — nes6502 2,550,000/2,550,000 over 255 scored opcodes, nestest 8991/8991, accuracy table 31 rows. Both `#[ignore]`d 5-minute replay goldens re-run under `--release` and **UNMOVED**, so no re-baseline — stated explicitly because `cargo test --workspace` reports them ignored and would have gone green while they were stale.

2026-08-16 W2-19 done (amended) — **All seven Tier-B waivers DELETED, not re-dated; the nightly runs green with none.** Four ROMs turned out to be ONE bug, and the ticket predicted three of them: **the PPU has its own open-bus latch, separate from the CPU bus's** — blargg's `ppu_open_bus` readme opens with exactly that sentence ("Unlike other open-bus addresses, the PPU ones are separate") and this crate had only the CPU-side one, passing it into the PPU. Implementing the decay register to the readme's table fixed `ppu_open_bus`, **both** `cpu_exec_space` arms and `cpu_dummy_writes_ppumem`. Per-bit decay clocks were forced by the ROM, not chosen: its tests 7 and 9 refresh *part* of the register in a loop for a full second and then require the **unrefreshed** bits to have decayed anyway, which one shared timer cannot do. Decay ages once per frame rather than per dot — nesdev and the readme both give the constant as "about 600 ms… some decay sooner, depending on the NES and temperature", so 36 frames is used because it is what the documentation says, not because it is what makes the test green. **SECOND BUG, found by measuring rather than reasoning:** `Controller::default` had `shift_index = 8`, so a pad that had never been strobed read back as *exhausted* and returned 1 on read **#0** of a session. `cpu_exec_space_apu` catches this because it executes code from all 256 addresses in `$4000-$40FF`, each of which must read back `$40` — the address high byte still on the bus, which happens to be `RTI` and returns it to the loop; at `$4016` the pad ORed in bit 0, the byte became `$41` (`EOR`, *two* bytes), and execution derailed into the ROM's own `BRK` trap ("Mysteriously Landed at"). Isolated by patching `$4016`/`$4017` to pure open bus — the ROM then passed all 256 — then confirmed by instrumenting the first read. **BUDGETS: PROVEN INSUFFICIENT, NOT RAISED UNTIL GREEN.** The ticket forbade the latter and it was the easy wrong move, so completion frames were bisected first: `ppu_read_buffer` needs ~1300 against a budget of **600** (and the ROM says so on screen while it works — "This next test will take a while. In order to distract you with entertainment, art is provided."), `oam_stress` ~3620 against **3600** — short by **twenty frames**, a third of a second, which is exactly why it reported a partial CRC instead of an obvious timeout. New budgets 2000/5400, ~1.5× the measured need. **THIRD PROTOCOL MISTAG IN THIS PROJECT:** `cpu_dummy_reads` never writes the `$6000` signature at all — screen-only — so under `six_thousand` it read as "never reached its test-status init" and was waived as a hang. It **passes, in under 200 frames**; never slow, never broken. Same class as `branch_timing_tests` and `cpu_timing_test6` (W2-12), so the rule now has three data points: **an unexplained "signature never valid" is a suspected protocol mistag before it is a bug.** Fixing the tag alone would have moved the ROM onto `tier_b_suites`' SKIP list, trading a false failure for a silent gap, so that binary gained a `screen_text` path. **SAVE-STATE DESIGN CHANGE:** the decay register is real machine state (`$2000`-`$2006` reads return it), so it entered the `PPU_` chunk — and a single global `CHUNK_VERSION` turned out to be the wrong shape the moment ONE chunk changed, since bumping it would have declared `CPU_`/`APU_`/`WRAM` changed too and made the reader reject four chunks byte-identical to what it already understands. Replaced by a per-tag lookup of `rf-state`'s own `TagInfo::current_version` — writer and reader are now literally the same number, `PPU_` alone went to 2 — and two test fixtures that hardcoded version 1 were switched to the registry, a hardcoded 1 having silently become a `CannotMigrate` fixture the moment a version moved. **FINDING, FLAGGED NOT FIXED, and it is owed before the first tagged release:** `rf-state`'s `MigrationRegistry` has **no production user** — the app never calls `Container::decode` at all yet — and this, the first real payload bump, could not have used it anyway, because the migration (append 9 zero bytes to a v1 `PPU_` payload) is NES payload knowledge and `rf-state` is forbidden to hold any. So **FR-STATE-005 is not actually exercised by anything today**: `golden_v1.rfstate` is built from dummy 16-byte payloads and pins the container *envelope*, not payload compatibility. Nothing is broken (W5-05 is the release ticket), but a real `PPU_` v1→v2 migration and a decision about where migrations may live under the layer rule are owed before release. **SCOPE AMENDMENTS (vetoable, recorded):** `tests/rom-manifest.toml` (the protocol tag and both budgets live there, and criterion 5 is explicitly about a budget), `crates/retroforge/**` and `crates/rf-state/**` (the wire-format change), `crates/rf-harness/**` (the `screen_text` runner). Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**946 passed / 0 failed / 8 ignored**) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0 · Tier-B nightly 0 (**9 passed, 0 failed, 0 waived, 5 not fetched** — the 5 are `golden_frame`/`audio_rms` protocols with no Tier-B runner plus unfetched ROMs, counted as skips and never as passes). Both `#[ignore]`d 5-minute replay goldens re-run under `--release` after the controller change and **UNMOVED** — the check that matters for a change to input state and one `cargo test --workspace` cannot make. Both `.rfstate` golden fixtures DID move (once for the payload, once for the controller) and were regenerated through their own documented `--ignored` commands, never edited to go green.

2026-08-17 W2-21 done (amended) — **`cpu_interrupts_v2` passes all five sub-tests, and `waivers.toml` now holds NO waivers at all.** All ten ever raised — seven Tier-B (W2-09), three Tier-A CPU (W2-12) — are gone, every one deleted because the ROM passes rather than re-dated. **The ticket predicted one APU defect and was right, but fixing it exposed two more, each invisible until the one before it was fixed** — a multi-sub-test ROM reports only its FIRST failure, so "one ROM red" was never one bug. (1) **The CPU sees the APU's IRQ one cycle after the APU raises it.** `Apu::irq_line` now returns a view latched before each tick's work — the same shape the PPU's NMI path already had via `NesBus::nmi_level_latch`, which is what made the omission findable at all. Without it `3-nmi_and_irq` recognised the frame IRQ a cycle early, and since the `$4017` write delay carries its own 3-vs-4-cycle parity rule, the ROM's **seven identical rows came out alternating 21/20**. **Deliberately not fixed by moving the frame counter:** a reset delay of 4/5 instead of 3/4 makes the same ROM pass and is the wrong fix — blargg's `apu_test/6-irq_flag_timing` pins the flag itself to "29831 clocks after writing $00 to $4017", which that would break by a cycle. The flag is set when the wiki says; the CPU's *view of the line* lags. Measured both ways before choosing. (2) **The OAM-DMA get/put phase was backwards.** nesdev says the power-on phase is random, so the crate picked one and said so; `4-irq_and_dma` pins it against hardware. Under the old phase the DMA ran one cycle short and the ROM's `8`/`9` boundary landed at `+526` where hardware puts it at `+527` — **with all 528 other rows already matching**, which is exactly what localised it to DMA length rather than to the poll. An arbitrary choice resolved by measurement; the invariant the unit tests protect (the two alignments differ by one cycle, {513, 514}) is untouched, and the parameter was renamed `start_cycle_odd` → `needs_alignment_cycle` because the parity was never the point. (3) **A taken, non-page-crossing branch polls interrupts a cycle earlier than every other instruction.** `5-branch_delays_irq` states the rule and flags the single differing row `*** This is the special case`. Implemented with one more sample of history in `CountingBus` plus a flag set only on that exact branch shape. **RE-BASELINES, each with its reason, none taken to go green.** `rf_scroller_split_timing`'s two frozen histograms moved by three writes between adjacent scanlines — `total` (1182) and `later_than_18` (734/448) both unchanged, so what they guard is unaffected. The **rf_scroller 5-minute replay goldens moved**, and that one is worth reading: a one-cycle DMA change shifts rendering every frame, so the pixels *had* to move — but the three checks that make those goldens mean anything all passed FIRST, and are asserted before the frozen comparison: recording vs **independent** replay byte-identical, every recorded periodic state hash matched, and the anti-vacuity witnesses (`player_x` reaching `PLAYER_X_AT_END`, `columns_streamed` reaching `COLUMNS_STREAMED_AT_END`) unchanged. **The game plays out identically; only the pixels moved.** That distinction is W2-11's lesson — a replay can look healthy by hashes while the player has died and returned to the title screen — and it is why those witnesses exist. Alter Ego's goldens did **not** move. Five unit tests encoded the superseded conventions and now assert the corrected ones, including the lag itself so it is pinned rather than tolerated. `irq_line_delayed` is serialized (APU_ → version 2, via the per-tag registry W2-19 built): it is one cycle behind the flags by design, so recomputing it at load would make a restored machine see the line earlier than the saved one did. **SCOPE AMENDMENTS (vetoable, recorded):** `crates/rf-harness/**` (frozen histograms and replay goldens live in its tests) and `crates/rf-state/**` (the APU_ bump). Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**946 passed / 0 failed / 8 ignored**) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0 · evidence 0 · Tier-B nightly 0 (9 passed, 0 failed, **0 waived**, 5 not fetched) · both 5-minute replays re-run under `--release` and green.

2026-08-17 W2-01c done (amended) — **`dma_4016_read` passes, and the ticket's own hypothesis was aimed at the wrong layer.** Its notes said "DO NOT RETRY WITHOUT a model of WHICH ADDRESS the halted 6502 holds on each no-operation DMA cycle", naming BreakingNESWiki and Fiskbit's tests. Both were read; nesdev's DMA page gives the 3-extra-read worked example but never says what selects 1, and the Fiskbit thread is open research ("I'm pretty baffled about what could be causing the difference in our test results"). **The ROM's own source settled it in four lines.** Its `end:` routine counts how many reads it takes the pad to return 1 — so `08` means no extra read, `07` means exactly one, and the expected `08 08 07 08 08` says only ONE of five iterations collides at all. **It is measuring shifted BITS; nesdev's "1 or 3 extra reads" is measuring bus cycles.** Both statements are true simultaneously, which is why the two never reconciled. **MECHANISM:** a standard pad's shift register is clocked by the **edge** of the read strobe. The three back-to-back `$4016` reads a DMC halt puts on the bus (halt, dummy, alignment) hold one strobe continuously asserted — one rising edge, so one extra bit; the DMA's own get (`$C000`) breaks the run and the CPU's resumed read supplies the second edge. This yields exactly one extra bit for **both** alignments, which is precisely why the answer never depended on get/put parity — the thing W2-01b had tried and the ticket explicitly forbade retrying. **TWO MODELS MEASURED AGAINST EACH OTHER, not argued:** the competing model (the halt cycle is the CPU's own read, so only later no-op cycles repeat it) gives `08 08 06 08 08` — two extra bits, still wrong — **and** regresses `dma_2007_read` off its documented CRC `5E3DF9C4` to `159A7A8F`. A discriminating experiment, so the choice is evidence rather than preference. Implemented entirely in `system/mod.rs`, which is why it fit the declared write scope: a contiguous read returns the bit the port **last drove** — deliberately *not* `Controller::peek_bit`, which reports the bit the next clock would present. **Suppression is disabled while the strobe is HIGH** (the pad is then continuously reloading from live buttons, not shifting) — applying it there broke `strobe_held_high_continuously_reloads_from_live_buttons`, caught by that test rather than by review. **FIVE CONTROLLER UNIT TESTS WERE READING `$4016` ON CONSECUTIVE CYCLES** — a pattern no instruction sequence can produce (`LDA $4016` is four cycles) and which only a DMC halt creates. They now read the way software does, and the contiguous case gets its own test asserting **both** halves (contiguous holds, spaced advances) so an engine that simply stopped shifting cannot pass it. Save state: both new fields join the `CPU_` chunk's bus half (→ version 2 via the per-tag registry); two `rf-state` tests hardcoded `2` as "a version this build does not expect" and silently became no-ops the moment `CPU_` reached 2, so they now derive it as current+1. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**947 passed / 0 failed / 8 ignored**) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0 · evidence 0 · local-gate 0 — **apu_test 8/8, nestest 8991/8991 byte-exact, nes6502 2,550,000/2,550,000**, which was criterion 3 and the real risk since a DMA-timing change moves every downstream cycle count. Both `#[ignore]`d 5-minute replay goldens re-run under `--release` and **unmoved** (games read the pad spaced, so the edge model never reaches them). Scope amended (vetoable, recorded): `crates/rf-nes/src/system/**`, `crates/rf-state/**`.

2026-08-17 W2-01d done (amended) — **`double_2007_read` lands on `22 44 55 66 77` / CRC `85CFD627`, the FIRST of the four variants its source header documents. `dmc_dma_during_read4` is 5/5 and WAVE 2 IS COMPLETE (26/26).** The ROM's source explains what it is doing: `lda $20F7,x` with `x = $10` gives `$20F7 + $10 = $2107`, which **crosses a page**, so the 6502 issues its dummy read at the wrong address (`$2007`) and then the real read at `$2107` — which also mirrors to `$2007`. Two `$2007` reads on back-to-back CPU cycles. The `x = $00` case does not cross, reads once, and is the control line. **MECHANISM:** the second of the two reads reports the value the first already presented — the PPU cannot drive a fresh byte one cycle later — while the read buffer and `v` still advance twice. That is precisely the difference between this crate's old `33 44 55 66 77` (both reads counted) and the accepted `22 44 55 66 77` (same internal progress, one stale value out). **THE SAME SHAPE AS W2-01c, ONE DAY APART, AND WORTH GENERALISING CAREFULLY:** both were a second access on a back-to-back cycle reporting a held value rather than a fresh one — the same *class* of bug, a device that cannot respond twice in consecutive cycles. But the controller case suppresses the shift while this one suppresses only the reported value and lets the side effects run. A third instance should not be assumed to hold the same half. Implemented in `ppu/**` only, as the write scope required, by keying off `Ppu::dot_clock` rather than the bus's `master_cycle`: `NesBus::read` performs the register read *before* ticking that cycle's three dots, so two reads on consecutive CPU cycles are exactly three dots apart — no bus-layer plumbing needed. `PPU_` moved to version 3 (it reached 2 in W2-19) since the latch is real state: dropping it would let a restored machine return a fresh byte where the saved one returned a stale one. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**947 passed / 0 failed / 8 ignored**) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0 · evidence 0 · local-gate 0 — criterion 2 in full: **ppu_vbl_nmi 10/10, sprite_hit_tests 11/11, nestest 8991/8991 byte-exact**, the in-crate read_buffer suite and both golden frames green, accuracy table 31 rows. Both `#[ignore]`d 5-minute replay goldens re-run under `--release` and **unmoved**. Scope amended (vetoable, recorded): `crates/rf-state/**`.

2026-08-17 W3-07 done (amended, split) — **The accuracy-vs-compatibility diff is real, wired into nightly, and fails in BOTH directions.** SPLIT ON BRAD'S RULING: the ticket bundled the diff with a **PPU catch-up scheduler**, and that is not an increment on this architecture — it is a scheduler redesign landing on every accuracy gate this session just took green. Correct catch-up needs NMI and A12 edge **prediction**, because three separate things observe PPU state at **dot** granularity on every cycle: `CountingBus::sample` polls `nmi_line()` after every bus op *and* W1-05c's `ppu_vbl_nmi` 10/10 depends on `nmi_level_latch` being snapshotted per dot (its own doc records a whole-cycle version measuring exactly 2 dots too wide); `tick_ppu_dot` drains A12 edges into the mapper every dot for `mmc3_irq_tests`; and W2-19's decay ageing plus W2-01d's `$2007` latch now key off dot counts.
