Filed **W3-07b** carrying that analysis so nobody rediscovers it. **THE SECOND PATH IS §5's "Open-bus modeling: full | simplified"**, which is the one §5 switch this crate can offer *both* sides of — W2-19 built the full model (the PPU's decay register with per-bit ageing) and "simplified" is what this crate did before it. The obvious alternative, "OAM decay: emulated | off", is unavailable: this crate does not emulate OAM decay at all, so only one side exists and a diff would compare a path against itself — the exact vacuity the ticket's own note warned about. **THE ACCEPTANCE INVERTS THE USUAL INSTINCT, deliberately.** §5: *"divergences must be test-suite-visible, or the switch doesn't exist."* So `mode_diff` fails on an **undeclared** divergence *and* on a **declared divergence that stops happening** — because a run where both configs agree everywhere means the switch has become a no-op, and green would be indistinguishable from correct. That is the direct analog of `tier_b_suites`' stale-waiver rule. **NON-VACUITY PROVEN IN BOTH DIRECTIONS** against the real suite, the way W2-05's `apu_mixer` mutation was: (1) break the compat path somewhere undeclared → a second ROM diverges, `UNDECLARED DIVERGENCE`, **exit 1**; (2) make the switch a no-op → **0 of 52** diverge, `STALE DECLARATION`, **exit 1**; baseline → 1 divergence, **exit 0**. Both directions are also unit-tested with synthetic data so they run without any fetched ROM (CI has none, NFR-006). Comparison key is the verdict **and** its message: two runs that both fail but at *different* sub-tests have diverged, and folding them together would hide the very class of difference this exists to surface. `accuracy_mode` is deliberately not serialized into `PPU_`, for the same reason `event_mask` is not — it is caller configuration, and restoring it from a file would let a state saved in Compatibility drag a session out of Accuracy, which law 6 exists to prevent. Honest about what the switch buys: **simplicity, not measurable speed** (ageing eight bits once per frame is not a hot path); §5's column is "full | simplified", not fast/slow. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**952 passed / 0 failed / 8 ignored**) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0 · mode-diff 0 (52 compared, 1 declared divergence, 29 not runnable/fetched). Scope amended (vetoable, recorded): `.github/workflows/nightly.yml`.

2026-08-17 W3-01a done — **The criterion that stalled W3-01 twice at a 600s watchdog now passes in ~50-120 MICROSECONDS**, and it did not hang. Every GPU run was executed under an explicit external watchdog (background PID + bounded poll loop + kill) rather than trusting any built-in timeout, because that is exactly what the two previous stalls cost. **THE KEY FACT, and why the earlier attempts had nothing to branch on:** wgpu reports validation errors **out-of-band**. A shader that fails to compile does *not* return `Err` from `create_shader_module` — it reaches the device's uncaptured-error handler, whose default behaviour is to **panic the process**. So FR-REND-007 ("renderer failures shall fall back to the original pipeline, never abort emulation") is literally unimplementable until those are captured: `fallback::guarded` wraps GPU work in a `wgpu::ErrorScope` and turns a captured validation error into an `Err`. That seam *is* the ticket. **HANG AVOIDED BY VERIFICATION, NOT BY HOPE:** the module awaits exactly ONE future and it is already resolved when created — checked against the vendored wgpu-29.0.4 source rather than assumed (`backend/wgpu_core.rs`'s `pop_error_scope` ends `Box::pin(ready(scope.error))`, i.e. `std::future::ready`, `Poll::Ready` on first poll, needing no `device.poll` to progress; wgpu's own doc on `ErrorScopeGuard::pop` agrees in prose). **`Device::set_device_lost_callback` is deliberately not used anywhere in this crate** — it is the one API whose contract permits never firing, and it is precisely what the acceptance criterion names. **SCOPE WIDENED BEYOND A BOOLEAN, deliberately:** the first cut returned `Failed` (no pixels) on device loss, which satisfies "never abort emulation" and *fails* criterion 1's "falls back to the **original pipeline**". Device loss is recoverable on every backend this targets — driver reset, GPU switch, suspend — so `FallbackRenderer` **recreates** the device and retries once on the original pipeline, reporting `RecoveredOnOriginal`. "No picture, forever" would have met the letter of the requirement and none of its intent. Also added because a real caller needs it: a **sticky shader-disable** — a chain that failed to compile will not start compiling, and retrying it 60 times a second pays the failure cost forever. **THE TESTS INJECT REAL FAILURES** (criterion 2): device loss via `Device::destroy()`, shader failure via WGSL that genuinely does not compile — a naga *validation* error (calling a nonexistent function) rather than a parse error, since that is the likelier shape for a real third-party shader — fed through `try_enable_shader`, the production path a user-selected shader will arrive by when W3-02a lands. Nothing is compiled out, no GPU is stubbed, and each test **first asserts the enhanced path works on the same objects**, so a fallback that was always taken would fail rather than pass; the compile test also asserts a *valid* shader still compiles through the same guarded path, so it cannot pass by rejecting everything. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**957 passed / 0 failed / 8 ignored**) · arch 0 · plan 0.

2026-08-17 W3-02a done — **CRT-class, LCD-grid-style and xBR-class shaders shipped, and the licensing question was raised BEFORE writing a line rather than interpreted quietly.** G-42's clean-room test is that the WGSL author "must not have the GPL/unlicensed libretro sources open". That test was written for a human and does **not** mean the same thing for an LLM author which may have been *trained* on those sources — so it was put to Brad as a ruling, with three options, instead of being read favourably. **Brad ruled: write all three, document provenance in full.** So every WGSL file opens with a PROVENANCE block that **derives** each term from stated reasoning a reviewer can check line by line — gaussian beam profile `exp(-(d/sigma)^2)`, three-phase aperture mask, gamma decode/encode round-trip for the CRT; cell-edge distance with a smoothstep shoulder plus subpixel stripes keyed *within* the cell for the LCD; one diagonal-edge comparison with a Rec. 601 luma-weighted distance for the upscaler — rather than asserting an unverifiable "sources closed". Each manifest's `authorship` carries the caveat verbatim, so a human licence review knows exactly what to audit. **THE UPSCALER, the G-42-critical one:** `basis` names Hyllian's xBR (MIT) as the permitted family and **explicitly rules out xBRZ (Zenju, GPL-3.0) and libretro GPL ports**. It is also not a transcription of Hyllian's MIT xBR — MIT would have *permitted* a credited port, but a port is not what this is, and claiming one would misstate the provenance in the other direction. The implementation is deliberately **simpler** than real xBR (one 2×2 comparison versus a larger neighbourhood and a multi-level weighted rule table), so "-class" is accurate rather than modest. **TOLERANCE FINALLY EARNED A NONZERO THRESHOLD — and only where measurement justified it.** Calibration on Metal: crt max channel delta **1** (0.0145% mismatched at delta 0), lcd-grid max channel delta **1** (14.68% at delta 0), xbr max channel delta **0** (0.00%). So crt and lcd-grid ship `channel_delta: 1` — this crate's first nonzero threshold, exactly what the brief predicted — with `max_mismatch_fraction` still 0.0, because at delta 1 *zero* pixels mismatch and the threshold does all its work where the noise actually is (`pow` and `smoothstep` evaluating differently on GPU and CPU). **xBR keeps `channel_delta: 0` deliberately**: it came back **byte-exact**, its arithmetic being comparisons plus one `mix(_, _, 0.5)` with no transcendental, and giving it an unearned allowance is precisely the vacuity W3-01b refused when it shipped nearest at 0. **Mutation-verified through the real GPU pass**, per the brief's "a threshold that passes a visibly wrong render is worse than no test": each tolerance is checked against a *different* shader rendered in its place and rejects it with max channel deltas of **131, 64 and 252** against thresholds of 1, 1 and 0. What can be mechanised is (`every_shader_manifest_records_its_provenance` asserts licence, substantive authorship, the basis naming Hyllian/MIT *and* ruling out xBRZ/GPL/libretro, the "-class" label, and the LLM caveat on all three); the rest stays review-enforced, which is the honest status since `cargo deny` will never look inside a `.wgsl`. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**960 passed / 0 failed / 9 ignored**) · arch 0 · plan 0.

2026-08-17 W3-03a done — **A closed debug window no longer costs anything on the frame path.** `CoreCommand::SetLayerExtraction(bool)`, off by default; `FanoutSink.layers` is now `Option<&mut LayeredFrame>` so the BG/sprite split is not performed at all when off, and `FrameMsg`'s two ~240 KB clones are skipped with it. The ticket pointed at `SetSpriteOverlay` as the shape to copy; I copied **`SetEventMask`** instead, because it is the same *principle* one layer over rather than merely the same plumbing — DEBUGGER.md §6's "closed panels register no event subscriptions". **MEASURED THROUGH THE REAL CORE THREAD, and reported in full rather than cherry-picked.** Paced (existing `frame_bundle_perf` harness): **60.10 fps debug, 60.06 release** against NTSC's 60.0988 Hz — 1.000×, unchanged, which is the half that had to *not* move. Unpaced headroom needed a new measurement, because paced throughput is capped at 60 Hz by design and both configurations sit at the cap, so the saving is structurally invisible there; the new test drives `StepFrame`, which produces a frame on demand with no pacer wait and is therefore the real per-frame **work** cost. **All four runs: debug +7.4% (19.687 → 18.225 ms/frame, 1.462 ms saved); release −0.5%, +11.8%, +6.8%.** The first release run showed *no* saving, which is why it was re-run rather than reported — on a shared machine this carries real variance and one run is not a result. The saving is genuine (three of four positive, median ~7%) but noisy, and that is the honest characterisation rather than "measurably improves" full stop. **The assertion is deliberately the weak one** — extraction-off must not be more than 5% *slower* — precisely because of that variance; a strict "must be faster" would have failed the −0.5% run and been quietly loosened later, which is how a perf gate becomes decoration. The printed numbers are for a human; the assertion catches only the failure mode that matters (a toggle whose branch costs more than the work it removes). **Two tests that fail in opposite directions** make the gate non-vacuous: the pre-existing content test now sends `SetLayerExtraction(true)` first and proves ON still delivers real layer content; the new `layer_extraction_is_off_until_asked_for` proves OFF sends no layer bytes while the main frame still arrives. **Reload hazard handled**, with a documented precedent for exactly it: a fresh core thread starts off, so an already-open Layers window across a ROM reload would silently never extract — `app.rs` re-asserts on load, beside the sprite-overlay re-send and for the same reason `event_subscription_active` is reset there. W3-03's own reasoning was **reversed deliberately, with its comment updated rather than deleted**: keeping textures current regardless of the checkbox was cheap on the UI side and avoided a stale-image flash — both true — but left the core thread paying forever, and the flash is bounded at one frame (≤16.6 ms), under a human's flicker threshold. The UI now keys off whether layer bytes actually arrived rather than its own copy of `show_layers`, so the two cannot disagree during the one frame a command is in flight. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**961 passed / 0 failed / 10 ignored**) · arch 0 · plan 0.

2026-08-18 W3-04 done — **Side-by-side compare + screenshot, and the real design work was finding a pair of buffers that are genuinely the same frame.** The shell had no second same-geometry buffer: the ultrawide/enhanced camera is a *different size*, and scaling one into the other would have meant inventing a policy this ticket has no business guessing at. The pair that does exist: the **enhanced** half is what the shell already displays (`FrameBuffer::overlay_scanline` paints the enhancement over the accuracy frame), and the **original** half is `FrameBundle::video`, whose own doc says *"always the accuracy-exact stream: assembled from `video_scanline` only, never `overlay_scanline`"*. One frame, two renderings, identical 256×240 geometry, read inside a single `pump_core_events` call under the ordering comment that already establishes bundle-before-frame. **So FR-REND-005's "synced frame" is structural rather than maintained** — there is no code path that can pair an original from frame N with an enhanced from N-1. If the geometries ever disagree the pair is *dropped* rather than composed misaligned. **The split composes a buffer rather than clipping two draws**, which is what a UI toolkit would do: composing is what lets one function answer both acceptance criteria, so what is on screen and what a capture contains cannot disagree, and it makes the divider testable by reading pixels instead of driving a UI. **PNG WITHOUT A DEPENDENCY.** There is no image codec in this workspace and no TECH_STACK row for one, so adding `png`/`image` would have been a TECH_STACK decision with an NFR-011 licence review attached — a different ticket's work. Instead: zlib permits **stored** (uncompressed) deflate blocks (RFC 1951 §3.2.4), so a conformant PNG needs only CRC-32 and Adler-32, both implemented from their specifications and checked against their published test vectors (`0xCBF43926` for "123456789", `0x11E60398` for "Wikipedia"). **Honest trade, stated in the module doc and RENDERER §6:** files are roughly raw-RGBA sized (~245 KB for a 256×240 frame vs ~10-40 KB compressed) — fine for a deliberate screenshot, *wrong* for anything per-frame, so §6's Phase-8 video capture must not be built on it. **Verified with independent decoders**, not only its own round-trip: `file` reports "PNG image data, 64 x 40, 8-bit/color RGBA, non-interlaced", macOS `sips` decodes it, and Python's `zlib.decompress` inflates the IDAT to exactly the expected byte count with every chunk CRC validating; the in-crate test additionally hand-decodes the stored-block stream at a size that **spans more than one block** (>65535 raw bytes), the boundary a single-block implementation silently gets wrong. **Cost gated, following W3-03a from the day before:** buffers are cloned only while compare is on or for the single frame a screenshot is pending — re-adding an unconditional per-frame clone for an off-by-default view would have undone that ticket immediately, which is also why the screenshot defers to the next frame. **Two files, not one composed image**, because FR-FE-005 says "separately" and because a composed split is reproducible from the halves while neither half is recoverable from a split; the test asserts the two PNGs **differ**, since identical bytes would mean one buffer was written twice and a fixture using one buffer for both would have passed. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**972 passed / 0 failed / 10 ignored**) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0.

2026-08-18 W3-05c done — **The trust ladder is now a general mechanism, which is what the ticket asked for and what D-004 actually is.** `rf_enhance::trust` knows *nothing* about anti-flicker: a heuristic is a string id, so the stitcher's scene-cut detection and the profile decoders inherit the ladder for free when they arrive. **THE ONE RULE THAT CARRIES THE WEIGHT — advisory does not act.** FR-ENH-011 defines shadow as "detect + record, never act" and advisory as "badge suggests"; a suggestion is a UI event, not a change to the emulation. That is the distinction most likely to be quietly got wrong later, which is why `should_act` is a *single function every call site must go through* rather than a `match` each writes for itself, and why the test asserts all three rungs rather than the two obvious ones. **AUTO-REOPEN IS A QUERY, NOT A BACKGROUND JOB** (FR-ENH-012): a suppression records the scene context it was granted in, and `is_suppressed` compares against the scene being asked about — so it stops applying the moment the trigger fires elsewhere, with nothing to remember to run. The reasoning is in the module doc: a user silencing a warning on one screen has said something about *that screen*, and treating it as a statement about the whole game is how a safety heuristic becomes decorative. **FAILING TOWARD LESS TRUST, everywhere it could go either way:** an unknown trust level reads as Shadow rather than erroring (a file from a newer build must not stop a game loading), and a stored suppression with no justification is **dropped**, which reopens the heuristic — honouring a malformed file would be the unsafe direction. **PERSISTED THROUGH THE EXISTING PER-GAME STORE**, which is exactly why this depended on W2-07: `game_settings.rs` is already keyed by normalized ROM hash and already preserves keys it does not understand, so a second persistence path inside rf-enhance would have been the duplicate abstraction the ticket's own note warns against. An untouched game writes **no trust key at all** — "fresh install all-shadow" stays a guarantee of the code (`TrustState`'s `Default`) rather than something the file asserts, so it cannot rot as heuristics are added. **NO TELEMETRY, STRUCTURALLY** (NFR-005): the module has no I/O, no clock and no network dependency of any kind — the report card is an in-memory `Vec` it never flushes anywhere, and persistence is the caller's job. There is nowhere for it to leak to even by accident, which is a stronger statement than "we do not send it". Surfaced in a Heuristics menu (ladder radios persisted immediately, any suppression shown with its justification and scene, plus the report card); the heuristic *name list* lives in the shell, not the mechanism, which is what keeps the mechanism general. Out of scope and flagged rather than absorbed: FR-ENH-013's red-fixture-must-fire-in-CI is a separate FR verified by rf-harness. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**984 passed / 0 failed / 10 ignored**) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0.

2026-08-18 W3-06 done — **3-OS build matrix (ubuntu/macos/windows) with per-OS artifacts, plus the golden-frame GPU suite as its own named Linux step.** **What is verified locally and what is not, stated plainly, because this is a CI ticket and I cannot make GitHub run.** VERIFIED: `actionlint` passes on both workflow files — a real validator (action versions, expression syntax, matrix references, runner labels), not merely a YAML parse; and `cargo build --release -p retroforge --features audio,gamepad` compiles and links on macOS, landing at exactly the path the job uploads (13.8 MB, `target/release/retroforge`). **NOT VERIFIED: that the jobs are green** — the first CI run is what proves that, the same class of claim RENDERER §7 already records about llvmpipe ("CI's first green run is what turns 'should be identical' into 'is identical'"). **A separate job rather than a matrix over `gate`**, and the cost reasoning matters: fmt/clippy/arch/validators/licence/determinism are OS-*independent* — their answers cannot differ by platform — so matrixing them would triple CI time to re-derive the same result twice. What genuinely varies per OS is whether the thing **compiles and links**, which is what this job proves. `fail-fast` is off: "Windows is broken" and "Windows and macOS are broken" are different findings, and fail-fast reports them identically. **Builds with `--features audio,gamepad`, not the CI-default set, and that is the point** — those features are off by default so `cargo test --workspace` needs no ALSA headers, which also means cpal and gilrs, the two most platform-specific dependency trees in the workspace, are otherwise compiled on *no* platform in CI. A build artifact a human is meant to run had better have sound and controllers. **Criterion 3 is its own named step** rather than buried inside `cargo test --workspace`: it costs almost nothing (already compiled, cached) and buys a green/red check a human can point at, plus `--nocapture` so the log records *which* adapter answered — and it cannot degrade to a silent skip, because `gpu_or_skip` panics rather than skipping when `CI` is set. **DELIBERATE SCOPE LIMIT, FLAGGED NOT PAPERED OVER:** the build matrix does *not* run the workspace suite on macOS/Windows. `gpu_or_skip`'s CI-panic exists so a Linux run cannot silently lose GPU coverage, so running those tests there would gate this job on the mac/win runners exposing a usable adapter — which this ticket did not verify and a *build* job should not depend on. Letting them skip cleanly per-job needs a crate-side CI-strictness signal narrower than "is CI", outside this ticket's `.github/**` write scope. **Residual gap, real and worth a follow-up:** runtime behaviour on macOS/Windows (path handling, line endings) remains untested by CI. Criterion 1 says "build jobs green", and build is what this delivers. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (984 passing, unchanged — a `.github/**`-only ticket) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0 · actionlint 0.

2026-08-18 W4-02a done — **FR-PROF-003 is enforced; the orphaned requirement now has its enforcing half.** `ProfileError::MapEntryMissingSource { table, index, label }` fails any profile whose `memory_map`/`rom_map` entry lacks a `source`, checked in declaration order so the *first* offending row is reported — someone fixing a profile wants the top of the list, not an arbitrary member of it. **It names the label, not just the index**, and the test asserts both the error variant *and* the rendered `Display` string: an index alone is a poor thing to hand someone editing a forty-row memory map by hand, and an error type carrying the label is no use if `Display` drops it. **A blank source is not a citation** — `source = ""` and `source = "   "` both fail; without that the requirement would be satisfiable by an empty string and the provenance rule would be decorative. **CRITERION 2 PROVEN BY MUTATION, not by tests passing.** The shipped profiles already cited sources, so `example_profiles.rs` passed both *before* and *after* this change — which proves nothing on its own. Removing one `source` line from `profiles/nes/rf-scroller-demo/profile.toml` makes those existing tests fail with exactly `MapEntryMissingSource { table: "memory_map", index: 0, label: "player_x" }`, then restored byte-identical. That is what turns "exercised by real data rather than only by a negative test" into a claim rather than a hope. **The spec's own example failed the spec** (criterion 3): GAME_PROFILES.md §2's `rom_map` example omitted `source` entirely, so anyone implementing from it would have produced an invalid profile. Fixed, and the rule is now stated once beside the tables it governs rather than living only in the SRS — including *why* it exists: D-005's deny-by-default community intake requires provenance, and an unenforced `source` lets a profile assert a RAM map with no stated origin. The schema doc was corrected too: `source` stays `Option<String>`, but it is Option so the failure is the loader's own named error rather than serde's generic "missing field", which would point at a line number and leave the reader to work out which entry it meant. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**988 passed / 0 failed / 10 ignored**) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0.

2026-08-18 W4-04 done — **Lua script host, and the sandbox was built and tested FIRST because every other criterion is decorative if a script can reach `io.open`.** **PROBED, NOT ASSUMED — and the probe found two real holes.** With a minimal `StdLib` (TABLE|STRING|MATH|UTF8), `io`/`os`/`package`/`require`/`debug`/`loadstring`/`coroutine` are correctly **absent** — but **`dofile`, `loadfile` and `load` are still present**, because Lua's base library is always loaded and has no `StdLib` bit of its own. `dofile` and `loadfile` *read files*. All three are now removed explicitly, via a `pub` list an auditor can read rather than infer. **The test asserts absence, not refusal:** a binding that does not exist cannot be reached by a cleverer call — paired with an anti-vacuity test proving the sandbox is not simply empty (string/math/table/utf8/pcall/print remain and a real computation runs). **A denied capability means the function is ABSENT**, so a denied call is Lua's own nil-call error pointing at the author's line rather than a runtime refusal they could catch and ignore; both directions asserted, so "the gate works" and "nothing is ever bound" cannot look the same. **Error containment is in the signature** (FR-PLUG-004): `on_frame` returns unit, never `Result` — "script fault pauses script not emulator" means a fault is a *state change*, not an error a caller might propagate into the frame loop. **Throttling tested in both directions** (FR-PLUG-005) — engages over budget *and releases* when the script recovers; testing only the engage half would pass on a one-way punishment. My first assertion there was off by one and the **code was right** ("every 4th frame" is three skips then a call), so I corrected the test rather than the behaviour. **Path containment** (NFR-010/D-006) canonicalizes both sides then compares, the same shape `retroforge::library` uses; a not-yet-existing file resolves via its *parent*, so the capability stays usable while every symlink and `..` on the way there is still caught — tested with a real symlink escape, plain `..` traversal, the no-capability case, **and a baseline in-dir path that must succeed**, without which the test would pass on an implementation that refused everything. **The ledger is the gate, not a log beside one:** `record` returns whether the write may proceed, so check and record are the same call. **`write_memory` is declarable and ledgered but the write itself is refused** — PLUGINS.md §2 requires the ledger also be recorded in save states (PROF/ENHC chunks) and the `ENHC` chunk does not exist; shipping it half-wired would be worse than shipping it honest. **The shipped example is executed, not just written:** the test loads the real `plugin.toml`/`main.lua`, runs 601 frames, and asserts the script neither faults nor throttles *and* that its own `frame % 600` print branch was reached — otherwise it would only prove the file parses. Scope amended (vetoable, recorded): `crates/rf-debugger/**` for one additive `DebugTab::LuaConsole`; `LAYOUT_FORMAT_VERSION` deliberately does not move. **Build-time risk named:** mlua's `vendored` feature compiles Lua 5.4 from C, making a C toolchain a build-time dependency on all three platforms — W3-06's matrix (itself never yet run) is where that first gets exercised. Honest gaps are in PLUGIN_AUTHORING.md's own "Known gaps" section: `rf.mem.read_u8` is gated but returns 0, `rf.gui.*` validates but does not reach the overlay path, and `replace_layers`/`input_bindings`/WASM are not implemented — the *gates* are real and tested; the data behind two of them is not yet. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**1009 passed / 0 failed / 10 ignored**) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0 · licences ok.

2026-08-18 W4-05 done — **Five mode presets, per-game enhancement toggles, the honesty badge, and the Enhance workspace.** **FR-MODE-003 is a property of the DEFAULT, not a check:** `Mode::enhancement_active()` is asked once, in one place, rather than re-derived by every caller — and the test that carries the requirement asserts a settings file with *every* feature flag set still runs nothing in Accuracy. "Enhancement never on silently" has to survive a file that says otherwise, or it is a UI convention rather than a guarantee. An unrecognised mode name (older file, typo, newer build) resolves to Accuracy, so enhancement is never on because a name failed to parse. **Research/Debug deliberately does not enable enhancement** — §4 says "enhancement optional", meaning not on by itself; a debugging mode that quietly changed the picture would defeat its own purpose, and that is asserted rather than left implicit because "everything exposed" reads like it should include enhancements. **The badge counts EFFECTIVE features, not enabled ones.** A badge reading "Enhanced ⚡(4)" while two of those cannot run without a profile is exactly the dishonesty FRONTEND_UI.md §1's badge exists to prevent — so `effective() = enabled AND available`, asked once. Enhanced without a profile reads (2); Game-Aware with one reads (4); Game-Aware *without* one falls back to (2). **The two unavailable reasons are distinguishable**, which is why `Availability` is returned rather than a bool: "turn on Enhanced" and "load a profile" are different user actions, and someone told only "unavailable" cannot tell which they need. **Unavailable rows are shown disabled and explained, never hidden** — §3.3's honesty contract; hiding them is the same lie by omission the badge prevents, since a user who cannot see that full-level view *needs* a profile concludes the emulator lacks the feature. **Hold-to-peek is held, not toggled**, and is applied at the view-selection site rather than by mutating `self.camera`, so releasing restores whatever the user had chosen without this having to remember it. **The ladder chip shows suppression, not just the rung** — a heuristic nominally "active" but suppressed in the current scene is *not* acting (reuses W3-05c's `TrustLadder` rather than a second model). Empty states say so everywhere ("No enhancements active — this is the unmodified emulation", "no profile matched this ROM's normalized hash", "(none declared)"), because an empty panel reads as a broken control. The profile inspector shows unmet capabilities **unchecked rather than omitted** and the precedence chain in §4's order, with the order itself asserted. **`profile_matched` is currently always false** — no profile loader is wired into the shell yet — and the inspector *says* no profile matched rather than implying one did; that gap belongs to whichever ticket wires W4-02's loader in. **Unverified, stated plainly:** the visual layout is checked by nothing. Every string and every enable/disable decision comes from `crate::enhance_ui` and is tested headlessly (8 tests); the egui layer only paints them, so what is asserted is what the panel *says*, not how it looks. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**1020 passed / 0 failed / 10 ignored**) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0.

2026-08-18 W4-06c done — **OAM diff panel: what moved since last frame, and what the 8-per-scanline limit dropped.** **The dropped set is derived from `scanline_occupancy`, not re-scanned**, so the diff panel and W4-06a's occupancy bar can never disagree about which sprites were on a line — the dropped set is *by construction* the tail of the same list, in the same ascending-slot evaluation order hardware uses. Re-implementing the scan would have been the obvious way to introduce a second, subtly different answer. **Each field is detected separately** (y/x/tile/attrs) rather than a single "something changed" bool, and the attribute byte routes palette, priority *and* both flips into one flag — tested across all four bits, because a diff that missed the attribute byte would hide flip and palette changes entirely while looking like it worked. **The 8-limit boundary is asserted from both sides:** nine sprites on a line reports exactly slot 8 (the *ninth* in evaluation order, not an arbitrary one), eight reports none — so an off-by-one cannot pass; and an empty scanline drops nothing, the common case a buggy implementation would still look fine on during a busy frame. **READ-ONLY BY CONSTRUCTION, not by discipline** (criterion 2): both OAM snapshots arrive as copies the frame *already carried*, so the panel has no path to the core at all and cannot perturb state even by accident — W3-05a's named hazard class (an enhancement silently clocking MMC3's A12 counter, invisible to pixel comparison). Verified rather than asserted: `mode_invariant_corpus` 6/6 and `sprite_overlay_mode_invariant` 3/3 run explicitly and stay green. **Both snapshots are the PPU's own OAM, never the CPU-side shadow at `$0200`** — W2-10a's gem defect was exactly those two disagreeing when an OAM DMA straddled the dots-257-320 `OAMADDR` reset window, and a panel reading the shadow would have shown a picture the PPU never had. Recorded in the function doc so a future edit cannot quietly switch sources. **The 256-byte per-frame cost is deliberately NOT gated** behind the panel being open, unlike W3-03a's layer buffers — and the contrast is written down: 256 bytes is nothing next to the ~245 KB frame it arrives with, whereas two 240 KB clones were worth a `CoreCommand` round-trip. Gating on principle would have added a command path for a rounding error. **Honest limitation, in the panel text itself:** the drop analysis assumes 8×8 sprites, because this panel has no PPUCTRL access of its own (the point of being a read-only consumer) — so the UI says "(assumes 8x8)" rather than letting a reader assume otherwise. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**1025 passed / 0 failed / 10 ignored**) · arch 0 · plan 0.

2026-08-18 W4-06d done — **The nametable and palette viewers have live data; W4-06a's honest "no live data" placeholders are gone because the gap they described is closed.** `NesBus::vram()`/`palette()` mirror `NesBus::oam()` exactly — plain borrows, forwarded through `EmuStepper` and carried on `FrameMsg` like OAM. **Non-observing is STRUCTURAL, not discipline:** a borrow cannot have a side effect, so the guarantee cannot be lost by a future edit the way a hand-written peek path could. The distinction rf-nes already draws deliberately (`mem_read` observes and ticks the MMC3 A12 filter; `chr_peek` does not) is what makes this load-bearing rather than a nicety — a debug viewer repaints **continuously**, so an observing accessor would perturb IRQ timing on every frame a panel is open, invisibly to any pixel comparison. **Tested in both directions, not asserted.** Criterion 2: a test writes known bytes through the **real `$2006`/`$2007` register path** — the path a game uses, exercising the auto-increment on the way — and reads them back through the accessors, so an accessor wired to the wrong buffer fails; plus an anti-vacuity check that an unwritten address stays clear, so it cannot pass because everything happens to read back as the last value written. Criterion 3: 100 repeated accessor reads leave `master_cycle` and the open-bus latch (via `peek`, the side-effect-free trace-logger read) unchanged, and both mode-invariant suites stay green. **Palette is returned RAW, unmirrored** — a deliberate debugger decision, not an omission: the PPU applies `$3F10/$14/$18/$1C` → `$3F00/$04/$08/$0C` backdrop mirroring on read, but a difference between what is *stored* and what *renders* is exactly what a palette viewer exists to reveal. **Honest limitation kept visible:** the nametable viewer shows nametable 0 only; `$2400`/`$2800`/`$2C00` are mirrors depending on cartridge mirroring, which it does not resolve — stated in the panel rather than implied by showing one grid and calling it "the nametable". **The larger gap this ticket deliberately did not close, restated because it is still true:** `EmulatorCore::state_view` has **no production implementer** — rf-nes does not implement `EmulatorCore` at all, so `StateView` remains a complete contract with no producer. Two accessors were the right size for unblocking the viewers; implementing `EmulatorCore` for rf-nes is a much larger piece of work that deserves its own ticket informed by which consumers actually need it. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**1027 passed / 0 failed / 10 ignored**) · arch 0 · plan 0.

2026-08-18 W4-06e done — **Breakpoint engine + execution control, and the ticket's first job was choosing a seam.** **ROUTE (a) chosen deliberately:** the shell drives `cpu.step(bus)` per instruction and checks at boundaries. The deciding fact is what criterion 1 *actually lists* — "PC exec, value-conditional, scanline/dot position, IRQ/mapper events" — every one of which is answerable at an instruction boundary. A value-**conditional** breakpoint tests what memory *holds* when the check runs; it is not a write-watch. So route (b)'s only real advantage (seeing individual bus writes) buys nothing here, while its cost is permanent: debugger concerns compiled into the core, on the hot path of every instruction of every session. Route (a) also keeps `rf-debugger` core-free and UI-free. **What route (a) cannot do, stated up front rather than discovered later:** a true WATCHPOINT ("break when value V is *written* to address A") needs mid-instruction bus visibility, because a write overwritten within the same instruction is invisible at the boundary — and snapshot-diffing between steps would be O(memory) per instruction *and still miss that case*, so it is not a workaround. Write-watchpoints are not in criterion 1's list and are not built; `CoreEvent::WatchpointHit` remains a defined event with **no producer**, worth its own ticket. (W4-06b's *title* said "watchpoints" but its acceptance never did — which is why they were still unbuilt.) **A real bug the tests caught, and only a real machine would have:** `step_over` first captured the stack pointer *before* the `JSR`, but `RTS` restores `S` to exactly its pre-`JSR` value — so a predicate waiting for `S` to rise strictly *above* that never fires, and the test exhausted its instruction budget instead of landing after the call. Fixed by sampling the entry SP from *inside* the subroutine, which is also the conceptually right framing: stepping over a call **is** entering it and stepping out. Frame tracking is stack-pointer based rather than JSR/RTS counting, because a program manipulating the stack directly — or an interrupt mid-subroutine — would desynchronise a counter. **Breakpoints outrank the step request:** the shared loop checks them *before* the done-predicate, so a breakpoint inside a subroutine being stepped over fires rather than being silently skipped (tested directly — it is the behaviour a user would otherwise quietly lose). **"One-branch cost" MEASURED across three release runs** of 300,000 instructions: unarmed **103.6 / 108.6 / 104.6** ns per instruction, armed (with the most expensive condition kind, a never-matching memory compare) **100.5 / 107.9 / 104.4** — overhead −3.0%, −0.7%, −0.2%, i.e. the armed table measured *slightly faster every time*, meaning the check sits comfortably below measurement noise next to ~100 ns/instruction of emulation. Three runs rather than one, because a single −3% would be a flattering artefact; the honest claim is "below noise", not "free". The assertion is deliberately loose for the same reason W3-03a's is. **Mode invariant:** both suites green, plus a dedicated test asserting instruction stepping reaches a **byte-identical state hash** to frame stepping over the same span — a debugger that perturbs the machine is showing something the emulator never runs. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**1040 passed / 0 failed / 11 ignored**) · arch 0 · plan 0.
2026-08-18 W3-07b done — **The PPU catch-up scheduler is built, diverges nowhere, and is honest about buying much less than §1 predicts.** DESIGN, committed before writing code and on the strength of a measurement: skip provably-inert dot runs **inside** the PPU, position-accurate, rather than deferring dots and flushing on observation. The ticket's own note rules out accumulate-and-flush (`CountingBus` polls `nmi_line` after every bus op, so it would flush every cycle); skip-ahead leaves all three dot-granularity observers untouched **by construction**, which is what makes §5's "no divergence" contract reachable at all. **THE MEASUREMENT CAME FIRST AND IT CHANGED THE DESIGN.** Inert-dot fraction, instrumented on real workloads before any scheduler existed: `mmc3_test_2` 26-31%, `cpu_timing_test6` 13%, synthetic rendering-OFF 31.2%, synthetic rendering-**ON 8.0%**. Most of the blargg suite runs with rendering off, so measuring only the suite would have overstated what this buys a real game by four times — that spread is reported everywhere the number appears. **WHAT IT ACTUALLY BUYS, on `machine_frame`: rendering off 732 -> 693 us/frame (5.4% faster); rendering on 1.124 -> 1.116 ms (parity, inside noise).** §1's "~10-20% slower" for lock-step is not what this engine shows, because its idle dots were already nearly free. The accuracy path was measured before and after (interleaved, 3 pairs, ~3% run-to-run noise on this machine) and is **unchanged**; a first cut that put the mode check inside `tick_master`'s per-cycle loop measured a 3.9% accuracy tax and was rewritten to hoist it, so the accuracy arm is byte-for-byte the loop that preceded the ticket — acceptance says in as many words that the scheduler "may not be paid for out of the accuracy mode". **TWO REAL BUGS, BOTH CAUGHT BY THIS PROJECT'S OWN INSTRUMENTS RATHER THAN BY REVIEW.** (1) `mode_diff`'s first run reported UNDECLARED DIVERGENCE on three `mmc3_test_2` ROMs: skipping dots skips no PPU-side A12 edges, but a **CPU-side** edge (a `$2006`/`$2007` access, exactly how those ROMs clock MMC3 with rendering off) sits in `pending_a12_edges` until a dot drains it — the drain is now split out and called once per skipped run. (2) A naive `(240 - scanline) * 341` in the prediction **overflows u16** (81840); in release it wrapped silently and hung `mode_diff`. The prediction is now capped at one scanline, a lower bound that cannot overflow and still cuts the predicate from 89342 evaluations a frame to 262. **NON-VACUITY, INCLUDING WHERE IT FAILED.** Four mutations of the predicate: swallowing the vblank-set dot (241,1) fails 3 tests, swallowing the pre-render clear (261,1) fails 2, calling a rendering visible-line dot inert fails the predicate test only. The last is reported rather than papered over: that dot is behaviourally empty so the equivalence test cannot see it, and a dot mid-scanline is never even asked about because the prediction covers the whole line. **The equivalence test's workload was rewritten because of this exercise** — the first version polled `$2002` without branching on it, and the vblank mutation left every byte of state identical. A workload that never acts on what the PPU says cannot detect a PPU scheduling bug. Evidence: `mode_diff` 52 ROMs compared, **1 divergence, still exactly the declared open-bus one**; `ppu_vbl_nmi` **10/10 under both configs with byte-identical result text and identical frame counts** (including 05-08, the suite W1-05c's "2 dots too wide" landmine sits under, and 09/10, the odd-frame timing the `render_enable_pipe` cap protects); `mmc3_test_2` 6/6. `mmc3_irq_tests` is named in the acceptance but **has never executed in this repo** (protocol mismatch documented at `ppu/tests/blargg_roms.rs`) — said plainly rather than reported as green; `mmc3_test_2` covers the same ground and does run. Because CI has no ROMs (NFR-006), the equivalence property is also checked hermetically over every save-state region, and the predicate's structural rules exhaustively over all 262x341 positions. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**1054 passed / 0 failed**) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0 · mode-diff 0. Three new benches were added under `crates/rf-nes/benches/` (in scope); they are deliberately **not** added to `benches/baseline.json`, which is outside this ticket's write scope — `bench-compare.mjs` iterates the baseline, so unlisted benches are ignored rather than breaking the gate, and wiring them in is a one-line follow-up for whoever owns that file next.
2026-08-18 W4-09 done (acceptance corrected) — **The UI smoke flow is real, headless, and caught by mutation; the crate the ticket names could not have built it.** THE PREMISE WAS WRONG AND IS REPORTED, NOT WORKED AROUND: acceptance says "via egui 0.35 inspection protocol", and `docs/TECH_STACK.md`'s UI row says 0.35's "inspection protocol enables agent-driven UI tests". Checked against the crates rather than assumed, that conflates two things and **neither is a CI mechanism**: `egui::Context::inspection_ui` is a debug-info **panel** you draw into a `Ui`, and `egui_inspection` + `egui_mcp` is an MCP **server** driving a live app over a socket — an interactive agent tool. The headless harness is **`egui_kittest`**, which publishes 0.35.0 from emilk/egui itself, same repo and release train, so it satisfies TECH_STACK rule 2's lockstep requirement instead of dragging a second egui version in. Built against that; **recorded as a ruling for Brad to veto**, with the correction carried in the new TECH_STACK row (amending the wrong row is outside this write scope). Licence surface checked FIRST, because it was the cheapest thing that could kill the approach: `Cargo.lock` gains exactly two crates (`egui_kittest`, `kittest` — AccessKit was already in the tree) and `cargo deny check licenses` passes clean; the `wgpu` and `snapshot` features are deliberately off. **`eframe::CreationContext::_new_kittest` exists in eframe 0.35**, so the harness drives the REAL `RetroForgeApp` — no test-only shim, and the app-shell refactor this looked like it would need turned out to be unnecessary. **THE FLOW:** boot → assert the controls bar exists → assert the honesty badge reads `NES · Accuracy` (law 6, before any ROM) → open a synthetic in-test NROM (built, not fetched: `roms/` is gitignored and CI never has it, NFR-006) → click Run → poll to first frame → assert the transport now reads Pause and the badge STILL reads Accuracy → open and close all five panels (Layers, Controls, Library, Settings, Debug Viewers), each asserted closed before, open after, and closed again after → assert the core is still running and the video output survived. **TWO VACUITY TRAPS HIT WHILE WRITING IT, both recorded in the test's own module doc.** (1) At the harness's default window size **every panel window rendered zero accessible nodes** — the assertions would have been written against an empty tree and passed forever; the 1600x1000 harness size is load-bearing, not cosmetic. (2) The emulator frame is an `egui::Image` and contributes **no** labelled node, so no accessibility query can distinguish a running emulator from a permanently black window — hence `RetroForgeApp::has_presented_frame`, asserted directly. **NON-VACUITY PROVEN BY MUTATION against the real app:** the Library window silently not opening, the mode badge losing its mode text, and a checkbox wired to the wrong field each fail the test, at the assertion that should catch them. **NFR-004, split honestly.** "ROM load to first frame ≤500 ms" IS measured end to end through the shipped path — file read, identity hash, per-game settings, core-thread spawn, first completed frame, bundle hand-back, texture upload — and asserted: **46 ms against the 500 ms budget**. "Cold start to interactive ≤2 s" is **reported as a floor and deliberately NOT asserted** (39 ms): the harness constructs an `egui::Context` directly, so it excludes window creation, winit startup, wgpu device init, font atlas upload and the library disk scan — most of what a real cold start spends its time on. Asserting it would have manufactured a passing NFR-004 out of a measurement that omits the expensive half. A real cold-start number needs a windowed run, which is exactly what CI cannot do; that gap is stated rather than papered over. `RetroForgeApp::open_rom_path` is now `pub` because the native file dialog is the one step no headless harness can drive — the test calls what the dialog's callback calls, so everything after the picker is the shipped path. CI gets its own named step with `--nocapture`, same pattern as the golden-frame step, so the NFR-004 numbers land in the log. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**1055 passed / 0 failed**) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0 · licences 0.
2026-08-18 W4-10a done (split from W4-10) — **Per-chip trace rings, the trace viewer, lz4 trace-to-file, and a pay-for-use gate that can fail.** SPLIT FIRST, ON BRAD'S RULING: W4-10 bundled three self-contained criteria with a fourth that **could not be built inside its write scope at all** — `NesBus` exposes no APU accessor, so `Apu::channel_outputs` is unreachable from `retroforge`, and `crates/rf-nes/**` was not in scope. W4-10b carries that, with the finding that an accessor alone would not have sufficed either: `channel_outputs()` is an instantaneous getter and a scope needs a waveform, so sampling it per scanline would give ~262 points against ~800 audio samples per frame — 3x decimated, aliasing the high channels. **DEBUGGER.md §2 ASKS FOR SOMETHING rtrb CANNOT DO, and the resolution is written into the seam rather than fudged.** §2 wants both "never blocks the core thread" and "overflow drops oldest"; verified against the vendored rtrb 0.3 source, `Producer` has only `push`/`is_full`/`slots` and `pop` belongs to `Consumer`, so the core thread physically cannot evict the oldest entry. Two rings, each honouring the half it can: the **transport** drops the newest and counts it, the **scrollback** drops the oldest as it drains — which is the ring the user actually scrolls. `TraceStats` keeps the two losses apart on purpose, because transport loss means the trace has **holes** and scrollback loss means it merely **scrolled**, and only the first raises DEBUGGER.md's truncation flag. **THE TRUNCATION TRAP IS RUN, NOT ASSERTED:** a flag no test sets is indistinguishable from one that is broken, so the test overruns a 65 536-slot transport by 5 000 entries and checks the flag AND that delivered-plus-dropped equals pushed AND that the survivors are the *oldest* — a transport that silently dropped everything would pass a flag-only check. **The file writer is verified by decompressing what it wrote**, not by checking the file is non-empty: `lz4_flex`'s `FrameEncoder` buffers, so a frame that is merely dropped is a truncated file a decoder rejects, after the UI has said "trace saved". lz4_flex over lz4 (pure Rust, MIT, no-unsafe-by-default vs C bindings in a workspace that lints `unsafe_code`); frame API verified against vendored 0.14.0 source; `cargo deny` clean. **The CPU trace shares ONE formatter with the golden-trace gate** (`rf_nes::trace::format_trace_line`), which DEBUGGER.md §2 requires in as many words — a second formatter would be a second thing to keep correct with only one of the two under test. The non-CPU chips are fed from the `CoreEvent` FIFO W4-00 already emits rather than new hooks in rf-nes (out of scope), and that trade is stated in the code rather than hidden. **AC4's bench has two arms that genuinely differ, and a third that makes the comparison falsifiable.** The run loop dispatches to two whole loops rather than branching per instruction, so "compiled-out" is not a stand-in — it is the function a debugger-less build would call. The **noise threshold (5%) was written down before measuring**, sized on the ~3% run-to-run figure W3-07b measured on this machine, so the result cannot be within noise by construction. Measured: **compiled-out 1.135 ms/frame, idle 1.126 ms/frame (0.992x, budget 1.05x), armed 2.875 ms/frame (2.53x, 274 897 entries captured over 60 frames)**. The armed arm exists because without it "idle is free" would pass just as happily against a trace that captured nothing; both anti-vacuity assertions (entries captured, armed actually costs more) are part of the gate. Wired into CI as its own named step, release + `--ignored`, alongside the determinism double-run. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**1065 passed / 0 failed**) · arch 0 · plan 0 · licences 0.
2026-08-18 W5-01 done — **RF-Scroller's real profile: normalized identity, a RAM map derived twice, and a near-miss worth recording.** All three criteria met: `[[identity]]` carries the NORMALIZED (header-stripped) hashes of the CI-built fixture (`sha256 f009d214...0bc239`), `retroforge-tool profile validate` passes, and `retroforge-tool profile hash <fixture> <profile>` reports **MATCH**. Note `fixtures/nes/rf-scroller/rom.sha256` pins the RAW hash (`c79f6f61...`) because that is what a build script can check — the profile deliberately does not, per FR-CORE-011 and `rf_cart::hash`'s rule that the raw hash is diagnostics-only, never identity. **THE NEAR-MISS:** a stale local `.dbg` left from an older build put `columns_streamed` at `0x602D` and `frame_counter` at `0x602E`; the real W2-10a build has `camera_y` at `0x602E` and `frame_counter` at `0x6030`. FORMAT.md's own "Address re-pin (W2-10a)" section warns about exactly that ("any test or tool that hardcodes `0x602E` for `frame_counter`..."). A profile built from the stale file would have read the wrong byte for two entries and looked entirely plausible. **The fix was to make the symbol dump admissible rather than to trust it:** re-linked with `-g --dbgfile` and verified the ROM is BYTE-IDENTICAL to `rom.sha256`, so the symbols provably describe the pinned build; then cross-checked every address against FORMAT.md's own table, which agrees. Both sources cited per FR-PROF-003. **Two more errors caught before close, both of which would have validated while being wrong:** `level_rle_data` was written as 256 bytes (round, guessed) when `leveldata.h` names `LEVEL_RLE_BYTES = 246` — a length past the array end hands a decoder trailing bytes that are not level data; and `metatile_table`/`level_column_offset` were typed `ptr_table` (copied from the W4-02 synthetic demo) when both hold plain one-byte values, which would tell a decoder to dereference tile ids. **`[entities]` points at the OAM shadow ($0208, stride 4, 12 gems) and that is the honest answer, not a shortcut:** this ROM has no entity struct array — every gem's world X is a compile-time constant and they share one Y, so the only per-entity RAM state is `gem_order[]` (which logical gem occupies which OAM slot). Pointing the section at a RAM address that does not hold entity data would validate and read garbage. **No `active` field, deliberately:** the schema supports one and it would have been easy to write, but `update_gems()` writes all twelve gems unconditionally every tail frame and nothing parks one off-screen — the only sense in which a gem is inactive is that the PPU's 8-per-scanline evaluator dropped it, which no RAM byte records. A masked predicate would have validated and been read as authoritative while meaning nothing. **One gap, reported rather than papered over and filed as a ticket obligation:** nothing asserts the `[[identity]]` block still matches the fixture CI builds. W5-01's write_scope was `profiles/nes/rf-scroller/**` only, so the test could not live here; it is now recorded in **W5-02b's notes**, which already needs the built fixture present. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**1065 passed / 0 failed**) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0 · profile validate 0 · profile hash MATCH.
2026-08-18 W5-02a done (Nova blocked, reported) — **The `metatile_screens` decoder family: a pure function of ROM bytes, driven entirely by profile data, and mutation-proven against the real fixture.** Both acceptance criteria met. `rf_enhance::decode::metatile_screens` takes a byte slice and a `Spec` and returns a grid — no `EmuStepper`, no `NesBus`, no `CoreSink`, no frame anywhere in the module or its tests, which is what lets a level view be built once at load rather than re-derived per frame. `spec_from_profile` resolves everything from the shipped `profiles/nes/rf-scroller/profile.toml`, so a profile whose tables move breaks the suite rather than quietly decoding somewhere else. ARCHITECTURE §3 already has the `ENH --> PRO` edge, so the new `rf-profiles` dependency is not a new boundary. **THE DECODER ENFORCES THE INVARIANT THE ROM'S OWN READER TRUSTS, deliberately.** FORMAT.md is explicit that `decode_column()` in the fixture "has no bounds/sum check and would silently mis-decode a malformed table. A shipped `metatile_screens` decoder should decide for itself whether to add that defense." This one refuses rather than truncating or padding, and the reasoning is the difference between the two readers: the ROM's runs against data its own build produced, while this runs against whatever a **profile author** pointed it at — and the overwhelmingly likely cause of a column that does not sum to `height` is a wrong table address in a profile someone is still writing. Padding it would hand them a level view subtly wrong everywhere that looks fine, which is the worst possible outcome for a tool whose job is showing the truth about a ROM. **VACUITY REFUSED AT EVERY LEVEL.** A decoder test asserting "48x14 `u8`s came back" passes against a function returning zeros, so: the spec is checked against `leveldata.h`'s own constants; the format's invariant is **re-derived from the raw RLE bytes independently of the decoder** and compared column for column; FORMAT.md's described content (solid ground row at the bottom, alternating landmarks one row above) is asserted as structure; and a distinct-id count rejects a fill. **Three decoder mutations all caught:** an off-by-one in the column-span arithmetic (4 tests fail — this is the single most likely bug in a packed-RLE reader and the one a shape check cannot see), row-major vs column-major indexing (1), and silently padding a short column instead of refusing (1). **A fourth trap is pinned that would not have crashed:** feeding the RAW iNES file instead of the normalized image shifts every table by 16 bytes and produces a plausible wrong level — the test asserts the two never agree. **The suite cannot pass having asserted nothing:** absent the fixture it skips locally but **fails on CI** (`gpu_or_skip`'s rule from W3-06), verified by moving the build directory away and watching it fail. **NOVA THE SQUIRREL IS NOT DELIVERED, and that is a blocker rather than a choice.** `profiles/nes/nova-the-squirrel/**` is in the write_scope but appears in NEITHER acceptance criterion. It has no `tests/rom-manifest.toml` entry and no build recipe here, so there is no ROM to hash and `[[identity]]` would have carried invented numbers — the exact false provenance W4-02 refused. Unblocking it needs a `[[git_artifact]]` pinned checkout plus a build step (it is GPLv3 source), touching the manifest and `scripts/**`, both outside this scope; recorded in W5-02a's notes with the reason it matters: GAME_PROFILES §2 adds families when **>= 2 games** need the shape, and `metatile_screens` has exactly one subject, so its generality is asserted by the spec rather than demonstrated — the decoder's own module doc says so, and the `rom_map` label contract has never been tested against a codebase that did not invent it. Family recorded as **version 1** (schema v0 has no `decode.family_version`; §2's versioning clause is honoured by writing it down, not by adding the field). 2026-08-18 W5-02b done (amended, tail waived) — **The offline decoder is validated against the running game, and the 15 columns it is not validated on are diagnosed rather than hidden.** THE TICKET WAS AMENDED FIRST, on Brad's ruling, because two of its terms were unsatisfiable as written. (1) "at least 3 RF-Scroller levels": the fixture has exactly ONE level and ONE area — FORMAT.md says so in as many words ("exactly one area, so this is the degenerate (area-count = 1) case"). Three slices of one 48-column grid decoded by the same code path prove nothing three times; the real second and third subjects were to be Nova the Squirrel, blocked upstream by W5-02a. (2) "golden screenshots" became an EXACT decode-vs-VRAM comparison, which is a strengthening: two renderings can agree while both misread the ROM, whereas the nametable bytes the game itself streams are the ground truth for "in-game appearance". Scope amended (vetoable, recorded): `crates/rf-harness/**`, and that was not optional — validate-arch rule 3 exempts only retroforge/rf-harness/rf-nes/rf-snes from the no-direct-core law, so rf-enhance can never run a core and the comparison cannot live in the crate the decoder lives in. **PROVEN AND GATED: all 64 columns `init_video()` preloads, byte-exact.** That snapshot is of a quiescent machine — the streamer is camera-driven, so nothing moves until input arrives — which is the one moment VRAM can be read without tearing or recycled slots. Plus a mutation gate (flipping one metatile tile id in the ROM handed to the decoder makes the comparison fail; it targets the metatile table rather than level data because that is the subtler failure — every column still decodes and sums correctly, only the tiles are wrong) and **the `[[identity]]` drift gate owed by W5-01**, which W5-01's own write_scope could not host. **THE 15 UNVERIFIED COLUMNS, DIAGNOSED.** Six (raw 64, 78-82) are byte-identical to the occupant they replace, so the frame their write lands is unobservable by any VRAM-sampling method. Nine (metatile 43-47) are torn in the fixture's own final VRAM and stay so 30 frames after streaming ends. **What was ruled out, by measurement:** settling (BLIT_SETTLE at 3, 8 and 20 frames gives exactly 15 every time); the decoder (W5-02a verified it against an independent hand walk of the raw RLE bytes for all 48 columns); and single-snapshot artefacts (a final snapshot is strictly WORSE at 47 of 96, which is what revealed the window genuinely moves — VRAM holds 64 raw columns and the level has 96, so slots recycle while the player walks). **The wrong tiles are ISOLATED, not a truncated tail** — raw 88 has rows 18, 19, 24 wrong with correct tiles on both sides — which rules out dropped writes (one lost write shifts every later row under the +32 auto-increment, corrupting ~20 rows) and partial writes (contiguous tail). Isolated tiles with correct neighbours mean writes landing at the WRONG ADDRESS. **Filed as W5-02c** with the leading hypothesis and its precedent: FORMAT.md records W2-10a already finding and fixing this exact class of bug once, where tail-frame update cost pushed OAM DMA into the PPU's dots 257-320 window — a genuine hardware-accurate corruption mode, not an rf-nes bug. If that is what this is, the emulator has found a bug in our test ROM and the fix belongs in the fixture; W5-04 now depends on W5-02c so the MVP acceptance pass cannot run over a visibly wrong level. **The whole-level test ships `#[ignore]`d, NOT weakened to "at least 81 columns"** — a threshold set to whatever currently passes is fitting the target to the arrow, and it coming off `#[ignore]` is W5-02c's acceptance. 2026-08-18 W5-03 done — **The game-aware view exists: a full-map camera over a profile-decoded level, with live sprites at world positions.** All four criteria met, as DATA rather than pixels: `rf_enhance::level_view` builds a `FullMap` or `Ultrawide` `SceneGraph` over a `DecodedLevel`, carrying a `SpriteSet` of live entities and the original-viewport outline as an `OverlayCmds` rect. GPU compositing of a `SceneGraph` is W4-03c's job and this write_scope cannot reach the renderer — the same seam W4-03b recorded for the ultrawide camera. **THE BUG THE WHOLE MODULE IS SHAPED AROUND: screen space vs world space.** OAM holds SCREEN coordinates; a full-map view draws the LEVEL; so every sprite must be lifted by the profile's `[camera].x`. Getting that wrong does not crash and does not look broken — the sprites simply stick to the viewport while the level scrolls beneath them, which reads as "the overlay is a bit off" rather than as a coordinate bug. So `sprites_in_world` takes the camera as a **required** argument and never defaults it, and every test asserts positions **against a moved camera** rather than asserting layer counts or sprite counts, which would pass against an entirely screen-space implementation. The end-to-end test drives the real fixture, walks the player 900 frames, and pins that the composited world positions track the live camera — plus that the fixture actually scrolled, without which the comparison is between two identical states. **Two honest details.** `[camera].x` is read as a full little-endian u16, tested specifically, because a low-byte-only read works for the first 256 pixels of a level and then silently wraps — the kind of bug that survives a demo. And `active` handling respects the profile: an explicit mask when there is one, the platform's own off-screen park (Y >= 0xEF) when there is not, which is RF-Scroller's case because that ROM has no activity flag and W5-01 deliberately declined to invent one. A ROM with no matching profile degrades to no session, never an error — enhancements are opt-in overlays over an unmodified simulation (law 6). Criterion 4 verified by running the existing suites unchanged: `mode_invariant_corpus` 6/6 and `sprite_overlay_mode_invariant` 3/3. `rf-profiles` promoted from a dev-dependency of `retroforge` to a real one: the shell is the only layer that knows a ROM's identity hash AND can reach the filesystem. 2026-08-19 W4-11 done — **Save-state manager modal: 10 slots + auto-slots, asserted through W4-09's harness rather than a checklist.** All three criteria. **Mode-at-save and the 'contains mods' flag are DERIVED from the container's own chunk list** (`ENHC` -> Enhanced, `PROF` -> contains mods), not stored alongside it — a sidecar field would be a second source of truth that can disagree with the state, and it would disagree in the worst direction: a state labelled Accuracy that restores enhancement data carries a session out of the reference mode, which law 6 exists to prevent. Reading the chunk list cannot disagree with the chunk list. **The pre-flight note's question is answered: no new readback was built.** The shell already holds the only RGB that exists (cores emit indexed pixels; the palette is applied host-side, ARCHITECTURE §3) and `rf_renderer::png` already encodes — both from W3-04. The thumbnail is a box-filter downscale written as a **sidecar PNG**, never a new chunk: a screenshot is not machine state and SAVE_STATES.md's chunk list has no slot for one. Averaging rather than sampling is tested with a frame that is black except one 2x2 block, which a nearest-neighbour downscale would drop entirely. **Migration warnings are surfaced, not re-invented:** `load` takes a `MigrationRegistry` so the caller decides what this build can migrate, and §3.2's last clause becomes displaying the `LoadWarning::Migrated` entries `rf-state` already produces — listed FIRST and worded as a change to the user's data, because a state that loaded after migration is not the state that was written. The listing path deliberately uses `decode_default` (no migrations), so browsing a directory can never silently convert anything on disk. A corrupt slot reads as empty rather than taking the other twelve down with it. **ACCESSIBILITY FINDING worth carrying forward:** measured against the real tree, NEITHER a bare `ui.label` NOR a hover-sensed `egui::Label` contributes an accessibility node — both are invisible to a screen reader and to W4-09's harness. Every information-bearing label in this modal is a `selectable_label` as a result. That is the same trap W4-09 recorded for the emulator viewport, and it now has a second instance and a stated rule. The save command carries a slot *stem* across the thread boundary, so `SlotId::from_stem` is round-tripped and asserted to reject `slot0`, `slot11` and `../../etc/passwd`. 2026-08-19 W5-06 done (scope amended) — **The profile authoring loop, and a walkthrough that is executed rather than described.** All four criteria. Scope amended on Brad's ruling to add `docs/**`: criterion 4 is a walkthrough DOC and the original scope had nowhere to put one. **`docs/AUTHORING.md` is generated against a test that runs it** (`authoring_walkthrough.rs`: annotate -> export -> decode -> validate -> golden, against the real fixture), so the doc cannot drift from the tool without a test failing. **R-A2: time to first decoded level is 0.40 ms of machine time** — and the doc says plainly what that excludes (finding the addresses in the debugger; writing the `[decode]` section, which no tool here infers from a list of addresses), because quoting a machine number as though it covered the whole loop would overstate the tool by orders of magnitude. What it does say is that **the loop is not the bottleneck**: at sub-millisecond re-decode an author sees the result before their hand leaves the keyboard. **HOT-RELOAD POLLS mtime RATHER THAN USING `notify`, and not merely to dodge a dependency:** the shell already repaints continuously so one `stat` per repaint is below the noise floor of a frame that uploads a 240 KB texture; a watcher's callback arrives on its own thread at the OS's convenience, so testing one means sleeping (flaky) or mocking the watcher (testing the mock), whereas `Watch` is a pure function of two `fs::metadata` calls and its test asserts BOTH that it fires on save and that it stays quiet otherwise — the half a watcher's tests usually skip. The honest limit is in the module doc: a change made and reverted between two polls is invisible. **Decode errors carry the ROM offset the author typed**, rendered in hex like every other ROM address in this project; a TOML syntax error is asserted to carry NO offset, because an offset the author cannot act on is worse than none. The preview is a metatile-id grid rather than a rendering, on purpose: a wrong palette makes a correct decode look broken and a right one can make garbage look plausible. The preview test asserts the preview's CONTENT changes when the column table moves, not merely that a reload happened. Polling is gated on the panel being open (DEBUGGER.md §6's pay-for-use rule one layer over), and the Author control only appears when a profile actually claims the open ROM. 2026-08-19 W4-10b done — **Audio channel scopes with mute/solo, and a proof that muting cannot perturb the emulator.** All three criteria. Per-channel streams are decimated by the **same accumulator** as the mixed output, so a host summing them gets the same timebase — measured at >10 000 samples over 20 frames, i.e. audio rate, not the ~262 points per frame a per-scanline sample of `channel_outputs()` would have given (which is the reason W4-10 was split in the first place). **MUTE IS A HOST-SIDE MIX AND IT IS PROVEN, NOT ASSERTED:** `EmuStepper::state_hash` — SHA-256 over every save-state region — is byte-identical across **all 32 mute combinations** and all 5 solo cases, with capture on and the host mix actually performed before the hash is taken. That matters because `determinism.rs` and the `.rfreplay` format hash the same value: a mute that reached the mixer would make two sessions that muted different channels produce different replay hashes for identical play. The consequence is written into `rf_debugger::audio_scope`'s module doc so nobody "fixes" it: muting changes what you HEAR but not what the emulator DOES. **ANTI-VACUITY:** those invariance tests pass trivially against a capture that produces nothing, so a fourth test pins that capture is real, at the mixed rate, shares one timebase across channels, and that pulse 1 and triangle produce genuinely DIFFERENT streams — without which a mute could not be audible and the mix tests would prove nothing. **Two design decisions worth keeping.** Solo is evaluated at mix time, never stored as "mute everything else": storing it eagerly loses which channels the user had muted *before* they soloed, so un-soloing would silently un-mute them. And scope traces keep **min AND max** per bucket rather than an average — an averaged square wave is a flat line at its DC offset, the single most misleading thing an audio scope can show. Channel samples are scaled by the channel's own 0-15 range rather than through the mixer's non-linear curve, so a channel's trace does not move when a *different* channel gets louder. Pay-for-use throughout (DEBUGGER.md §6): capture is off by default, buffers are freed when it is turned off, and traces are reduced to 64 points on the core thread so a frame's 5x735 samples never cross the channel. `rf-debugger` still has no `rf-nes` dependency — the shell mediates, as it does for every other viewer. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**1106 passed / 0 failed**) · arch 0 · plan 0.
2026-08-19 W5-02c attempt 1 — **hypothesis tested, refuted, reverted.** Theory: on tail frames the gem/blink/vertical-area work overruns vblank, the top-of-loop `while ((PPU_STATUS & 0x80u) == 0)` exits immediately because the flag was ALREADY set, and `stream_chunk`'s `$2007` writes then land during rendering where they corrupt the address (real hardware behaviour, nesdev PPU_registers). It fit the evidence well: corruption arrives in whole `STREAM_CHUNK_ROWS`(2)-sized units, only in tail columns, and main_loop was completing 0.98 iterations per PPU frame there. **The fix was implemented and measured rather than reasoned about: 15 disagreeing columns became 17, not 0.** Everything is reverted — fixture source, `rom.sha256`, and the profile's re-pinned identity and offsets — verified by a deterministic rebuild and a `profile hash` MATCH. **What the attempt bought, and it is not nothing:** the 0.98 figure is NOT evidence of missed vblanks (it survived the fix); corruption is per-CHUNK, so it is a property of specific FRAMES rather than of rows or RLE data; and it is not the top-of-loop wait, since forcing every streaming frame to start at a caught vblank edge changed WHICH columns broke but not how many — pointing at something whose cost varies frame to frame inside the streaming path rather than at its entry. Three next steps are written into the ticket, the first being to instrument for real (W4-10a's trace rings already capture PPU writes with frame position) instead of inferring, and the third being to run the same ROM in Mesen to rule the emulator in or out. The ticket also now records, from having done it once, that any ROM change moves every `[[rom_map]]` offset (the tables shifted by 0x3F) as well as the identity — so a re-pin is part of the fix, not a surprise. W5-02b's identity-drift gate caught that within seconds, which is what it was built for. Gate unchanged and green: **1106 passing**, fmt 0 · arch 0 · plan 0 · profile hash MATCH.
2026-08-19 W5-02c done (attempt 2) — **The emulator was right and the fixture was racy: root-caused by instrumentation, fixed, 96/96.** ROOT CAUSE: `stream_chunk()` ran AFTER `read_buttons()` (eight `$4016` reads under cc65's codegen) and the 16-bit camera arithmetic, so it started around scanline 256 of a vblank that ends at 260 and its last `$2007` writes landed on **scanlines 0-1 — during rendering**, where the PPU owns `v` and a write goes to whatever address rendering left there (nesdev PPU_registers). rf-nes was faithfully reproducing a real hardware corruption mode, exactly as the ticket warned it might. **HOW IT WAS FOUND, and the contrast with attempt 1 is the lesson:** attempt 1 reasoned from a plausible story (a late-starting vblank poll) and made things worse, 15 disagreeing columns to 17. Attempt 2 stepped the machine instruction by instruction and recorded the PPU scanline at every playfield VRAM write — derived from `master_cycle`/`frame_count`, both already public, so no `rf-nes` change and no scope amendment was needed. The writes clustered at `{0, 1, 256, 257, 258}`: 256-258 correct late-vblank streaming, 0-1 the overrun, hitting exactly tile rows 16 and 24 — the rows W5-02b had measured as wrong. **THE FIX IS ORDERING, NOT SPEED:** `stream_chunk()` now runs first after the vblank wait, on the previous frame's demand target, which costs one frame of lookahead and nothing else because the streamer already runs `STREAM_MARGIN_TILES` ahead. Same shape as W2-10a's fix for the OAM DMA. The carry variable is declared after every other static **on purpose** — cc65 assigns BSS in declaration order, so declaring it earlier would have shifted `gem_order[]` and silently staled FORMAT.md's documented addresses and the shipped profile; the symbol dump confirms it landed at `$606F`, above `gem_order` at `$6063`, with every documented address unchanged. **`the_offline_decode_equals_what_the_rom_streams_across_the_whole_level` is 96/96 and came OFF `#[ignore]` rather than being weakened** — which is why W5-02b refused to lower it to "at least 81". ROM re-pinned (tables moved by `0x0A`) and the profile's identity and `rom_map` with it, verified by deterministic rebuild and `profile hash` MATCH. **W2-10a's frozen split-timing histogram was re-baselined, with the safety check recorded rather than assumed:** the invariant it protects is `later_than_18` (nothing crossing into vblank) and it is UNCHANGED at 734 for the top-of-frame pair; what moved is a redistribution inside the safe early window. Re-baselining with a recorded reason is the established precedent — W2-10a and W2-21 both did it. W5-06's authoring tests now derive table offsets from the profile instead of hardcoding them, so the next rebuild will not break them. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**1106 passed / 0 failed**) · arch 0 · plan 0 · traceability 0 · profile hash MATCH.
