2026-08-19 W5-07 done — **The last MVP feature gap is closed: a Lua script now reads live memory through profile-published addresses and draws an overlay that follows the player.** W4-04 had built the capability GATE and left the data path stubbed — `rf.mem.read_u8` returned a literal 0 and `rf.gui.*` were no-ops — with a comment saying wiring a live machine through was the shell's to give. This is that wiring; the gate is untouched and still means a denied capability makes the function ABSENT rather than present-and-refusing. **SCOPE WIDENED AT FILING to include `plugins/**`, and that was the difference between meeting the letter and the point:** the shipped example hardcoded `PLAYER_X = 0x0300` — the wrong address (RF-Scroller's is `$6029`), read as `u8` when the profile declares `u16`, and not profile-published at all. MVP.md says "using profile-published addresses" in as many words. The example now contains **no address literal**; it asks `rf.profile.addr("player_x")`. **THE LIFECYCLE FINDING, caught by the tests rather than by review:** a script resolves `rf.profile.addr` once **at load**, because that is the natural way to use a published map and is what the example does. A host that publishes labels only after `load` gets nil forever — the script runs, faults nothing, draws nothing, and looks like a broken overlay rather than a lifecycle mistake. Hence `ScriptHost::load_with_bridge`. **`rf.gui` draws in WORLD space**, stated in the API doc rather than inferred: a world-space profile value goes straight through, and a script wanting screen space subtracts `camera_x` itself. Silently adding a camera would reproduce exactly the bug W5-03's module doc warns about. Two smaller calls, both about not breaking what exists: `rect`'s colour is optional because W4-04's stub accepted any argument list and scripts already call `rect(x,y,w,h)`; and `rf.gui.text` stays a no-op that SAYS SO in the log rather than pretending, because the `OverlayCmds` layer carries lines and rects only. **VACUITY REFUSED:** the stub returned 0 and 0 is a plausible memory value, so the tests assert reads return what the host published (0xAB; 0xCDAB little-endian), that an out-of-window read is 0 rather than a fault, that an unknown label is nil, and — the headline — that the marker's position **changes as the player walks** and sits exactly at `player_x`. The end-to-end test runs the **shipped** example script, not a private copy, because a test with its own script could pass while the shipped example stayed broken, which is the state W4-04 left it in. **docs/MVP.md is now 9 of 11 checked**; the two remaining are partials (a 10-frame mode-invariant corpus where the line asks for 5000 frames, and a missing `.rfreplay` for the un-profiled-scroller demo), not missing features. 2026-08-19 W5-08 done — **docs/MVP.md is 11 of 11 checked, zero unchecked.** Both remaining partials closed at the scale the lines actually ask for. **THE 5000-FRAME BOUNDARY RUN:** Right held throughout, both modes, every frame's core state hash compared and identical. It carries its own loop rather than joining `CORPUS`, for two reasons stated in the file — `CORPUS` is a `const` of hermetic synthetic ROMs while RF-Scroller is gitignored build output CI produces separately, and `CORPUS`'s runner feeds `InputFrame::empty()` while an idle RF-Scroller never scrolls, never streams and never reaches the tail scenes. **The anti-vacuity assertion is what makes 5000 mean anything:** two runs of a game that never did anything would also agree at every frame, so the test pins >100 **distinct** video frames (observed: **312**) and that the machine did not end in the state it started in. **THE REPLAY:** `fixtures/replays/unprofiled-scroller.rfreplay` (900 frames) is checked in and a test replays it, asserting the stitched canvas grows past one console viewport and that a single continuous scroll stays **one** scene. The recording step is a separate `#[ignore]`d test — one that rewrote a checked-in artefact on every `cargo test` would dirty the working tree every run — and the replaying test verifies the ROM hash first, so a fixture rebuild fails loudly rather than replaying stale inputs into a different game. **"Un-profiled" describes the SESSION, not the ROM, and the test says so** instead of letting a reader assume: the stitcher, fog and scene identity never consult a profile, which is exactly what makes them the generic fallback, so RF-Scroller driven without loading its profile is a faithful un-profiled session. Alter Ego — which the MVP line names first — is fetch-only with no redistribution grant (W0-03), so no replay of it could be checked in and the "in-repo" half of that requirement could not be met with it at all. Both gates are wired into CI as their own named steps, and both FAIL rather than skip when the fixture is absent there. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**1111 passed / 0 failed**) · arch 0 · plan 0.
2026-08-19 W5-04 done — **THE MVP GATE IS GREEN: docs/MVP.md is 11 of 11, every box carrying the command that proves it.** Each checked line names the command and the ticket, so a box is re-verifiable rather than a claim. Scope amended on Brad's ruling ("automation counts for now") to add `crates/retroforge/**`, so the demo capture is PRODUCED by a harness: it regenerates from the checked-in replay and the deterministically-built ROM, which means anyone can reproduce it and a change that breaks the feature changes the image — whereas a screen recording keeps looking right forever after the thing it shows has broken. **THE CAPTURE CAUGHT ITS OWN BUG, by looking at the output instead of trusting it.** The first version copied tile pixels straight through, but `pattern::tile_to_rgba` writes alpha 0 for palette index 0 ("transparent where nothing drew"), so the whole sky was transparent and a PNG viewer rendered it **white** — a white-sky reconstruction sitting next to a black-sky console panel, in an image whose entire purpose is showing the two agree. It now alpha-tests and composites onto the game's own `area_palette[0]`. Downscaled 3x with the reason recorded rather than left as a magic number: `rf_renderer::png` writes STORED deflate blocks so a full-size sheet is 4 MB; teaching that encoder to deflate is a real follow-up. **`docs/evidence/local-gate.json` REGENERATED** — W4-10b's per-channel audio capture touched `crates/rf-nes/src/apu`, which staled the `apu_test` evidence and turned `validate-evidence.mjs` (a CI step) red. Full local gate re-run: **2 550 000/2 550 000 vector cases, nestest 8991/8991 lines, ppu_vbl_nmi 10/10, sprite_hit_tests 11/11, accuracy table 31/31**. Worth knowing for whoever next touches a core: any change under rf-nes's apu/system paths stales that file, and the fix is `scripts/local-gate.sh`, not a waiver. 2026-08-19 W6-00 done — **SNES phase entry: refined, ticketed, and two findings recorded before anyone starts.** All three criteria. No 13-point tickets remain (an earlier pass had already split W6-01..04 into a/b halves), so the split criterion is satisfied by verification — recorded as such rather than left looking undone. **RF-Scroller-S is filed as W6-05** (D-001 names it by name and FR-CORE-037 gates on it, so it is a doctrine obligation, not a nice-to-have), and **the libSFX LoROM/HiROM mirror-map fixtures as W6-06**, together with the toolchain they need. **W6-06 was split out of W6-05 deliberately:** the toolchain is a prerequisite for every SNES fixture, is independently useful, and is the piece most likely to need a TECH_STACK row and a licence check — bundling it would have made a fixture ticket fail for toolchain reasons. It also has **no dependencies**, because building a ROM does not require being able to run one, which makes it the right place to start the phase and de-risks everything after it. **TWO PHASE-ENTRY FINDINGS, verified rather than assumed.** (a) **The test artifacts are already fetchable** — `singlestep-65816`, `singlestep-spc700`, `gilyon-snes-tests`, `undisbeliever-snes-test-roms` and `peterlemon-snes` are all in `tests/rom-manifest.toml`, three already fetched locally. This phase is NOT blocked on an unobtainable artifact the way W5-02a's Nova profile was, which was the assumption most likely to be wrong. (b) **The SPC700 IPL boot ROM needs a ruling before W6-04a is claimed:** 64 bytes of Nintendo code against project law 5's "no ROM bytes in git". Three options with trade-offs are recorded on W6-04a — HLE the handshake, fetch it hash-verified like an external oracle, or require the user to supply it — and the choice was NOT made here, because it is a decision about what the emulator claims to be rather than a coding detail. `asar` is not installed and there is no `fixtures/snes/`; the precedent to copy is cc65, installed by CI's own apt step and driven by a `build.sh` that pins `rom.sha256` and refuses a mismatch. W5-05 (tagged release) deferred on Brad's ruling — the repo is private and GitHub is code storage, not a distribution channel — with its non-release criteria and the un-executable-on-v0 migration drill recorded for whenever distribution matters.
2026-08-19 W6-06 done — **The SNES toolchain question had a much better answer than the ticket assumed: there isn't one.** D-001 and this ticket both said libSFX, and phase entry assumed asar/libSFX had to be installed and licence-checked before any SNES work could start. Checked rather than assumed: **cc65's `ca65` already assembles 65816** (`--cpu 65816`, with `.p816` and explicit `.a8`/`.a16` width directives), `ld65` links a flat LoROM or HiROM image from a linker config, and **cc65 is already installed in CI** for the NES fixture. No new dependency, no TECH_STACK row, no licence review — the SNES phase was never gated on a toolchain decision. libSFX stays the right answer for RF-Scroller-S (W6-05), which wants a framework's init code, macros and asset pipeline; a cartridge *mapping* test needs one bank, a header and vectors, and that distinction is written into FORMAT.md so W6-05 does not read this as a precedent for avoiding libSFX everywhere. **ONE SOURCE, TWO MAPPINGS** via a `-D` switch, deliberately: the pair is a comparison, not two unrelated fixtures, and a test asserts they share a long common code prefix — without which they could drift into different programs and any later result-block difference would be uninterpretable. Both build deterministically with pinned hashes the script refuses to silently re-pin. **HONEST GAP, recorded rather than glossed:** nothing executes these yet — `rf-snes` is a 15-line stub — so "assert via a golden RAM result block" is delivered as far as it can be. The ROMs write the block (four checks, magic written LAST so its presence certifies the counts beneath it came from a completed run); reading it is owed to **W6-02a** and is now a note on that ticket. The harness test asserts everything short of execution — deterministic build, valid cartridge structure, mapping-only difference, and the protocol pinned against the source so it cannot drift — and says in its own doc that a structural test is not a substitute for running them. **A bug worth not repeating,** in FORMAT.md: the first build put the header at `$FFB0`, the *extended* header; the one a console reads is at `$FFC0`. It assembled, linked, looked fine, and reported its title as "XTURE" — the real title read five bytes into itself. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**1114 passed / 0 failed**) · plan 0.
2026-08-19 W6-01a HANDOFF — **the 65C816 is built, gated and pushed; its one acceptance criterion is blocked on a decision the manifest defers to this ticket.** Left `in_progress` rather than closed: one criterion, not met, is not done. **BUILT:** `crates/rf-snes/src/cpu/` — the register file with real emulation/native semantics, 24-bit addressing across all sixteen modes, and the core op set (loads/stores, transfers, ALU with decimal, shifts, compares, inc/dec, the stack group, branches, jumps, JSR/JSL/RTS/RTL, flag ops, XCE, REP/SEP, XBA, block moves). 24 unit tests, workspace at **1137 passing**. **Interrupt and vector opcodes return `Err(opcode)` rather than no-opping**, deliberately — they are W6-01b's, and a core that silently executed them as nothing would hand the next ticket something that looks finished; two tests pin it in both directions so "everything is unimplemented" cannot pass. **THE BLOCKER:** `tests/rom-manifest.toml`'s `singlestep-65816` entry has `sha256 = "TODO-490mb-archive..."` and a comment saying the choice — fetch 490 MB per run, or curate a leaner subset — is a "follow-up for the ticket that actually consumes these vectors". This is that ticket, and the manifest is outside its write_scope. **Proposed resolution from precedent, not invention:** the nes6502 vectors already work this way — a LOCAL gate (`scripts/local-gate.sh`, 2 550 000 cases), never fetched by CI (NFR-006), with the result committed as `docs/evidence/local-gate.json`. Applying that shape answers the manifest's own question, because CI does not fetch 490 MB for the simple reason that CI never fetches vectors at all. **The unit tests do not pretend to substitute for vectors** — their module doc says vectors are exhaustive but bad at explaining failures, so these pin the four rules that make this chip not a 6502 (runtime-variable width, emulation-mode forcing, the direct-page-wraps-vs-absolute-indexed-carries pair, and the surviving high accumulator half), so that when a vector fails later one of them says why. Scope amended as a defect fix, recorded: `crates/rf-snes/src/cpu/**` cannot declare itself, since `pub mod cpu;` lives in `lib.rs`. Gate: fmt 0 · clippy 0 · test 0 (**1137 passed / 0 failed**) · arch 0 · plan 0.
- **W6-01a — 65C816 core ops, emulation/native modes, addressing** (2026-08-19, closed): SingleStepTests 65816 vectors wired and green — **4,980,000/4,980,000 cases, 249 of 256 opcodes, both emulation and native mode**. The manifest's deferred "490 MB archive vs curated subset" question had a false premise: upstream is already per-opcode, so the archive artifact/suite are retired and `scripts/fetch-65816-vectors.sh` fetches all 256 opcodes; `scripts/local-gate.sh` runs them as a hard local gate (local-only, NFR-006). The vectors caught six real CPU bugs the 24 unit tests did not — continuous emulation-mode SP pinning, decimal ADC/SBC overflow latched before only the top digit's correction, an `alu_mode` offset table wrong for eleven of fifteen addressing modes (hidden behind a silent catch-all arm), program-bank pointer reads for `JMP/JSR ($nnnn,X)`, page-1 wrap applying to 6502-era stack ops but not the new 65816 ones, and bank-0 wrap for direct-page 16-bit accesses. Excluded, not waived: `$00` BRK plus `$44`/`$54` block moves, whose vectors are cycle-truncated mid-instruction and need W6-01b's master-cycle model. workspace: 1138 passing.
- **W6-01b — 65C816 vectors 100% + master-cycle memory-speed model** (2026-08-19, closed): both criteria. **5,080,000/5,080,000 vector cases across 254 of 256 opcodes, zero unimplemented** — `BRK`, `COP`, `RTI`, `WAI` and `STP` all landed and passed first run, completing the instruction set (the catch-all `Err(opcode)` arm is now unreachable, kept only because a `u8` match must stay exhaustive and the suite discovers coverage from it). `crates/rf-snes/src/cpu/speed.rs` gives the 6/8/12 master-cycle cost by region per EMULATION_CORES.md §3.1, with FastROM as a pure argument; `AccessCost` wraps a `CpuBus` so the model is exercised by real instructions rather than defined and never called, and `peek` is deliberately uncharged so opening a memory viewer cannot change timing. The region table is cross-checked against the independent bit-trick form on **all 16,777,216 addresses** in both FastROM states, and mutation-checked. **Two honest gaps, both recorded where they land:** the vectors' per-cycle traces are still not asserted on — that needs a cycle-accurate executor modelling internal no-access cycles, and is now a note on W6-02a, with the earlier promise in `vectors.rs` withdrawn in the file rather than left to rot — and `$44`/`$54` stay excluded because SingleStepTests hard-caps each case at 100 cycles (measured: 9,999 of 10,000) so every block-move case is captured mid-iteration. **A gate bug was fixed here too:** excluded opcodes were skipped *before* being run, so implementing `BRK` would have shipped it untested while the suite printed green; they are now run and reported, and an assertion fires if an exclusion becomes obsolete. workspace: 1150 passing.
- **W6-02a — SNES bus + LoROM/HiROM + 5A22 extras** (2026-08-19, closed): all three criteria. `mapping.rs` is a pure function so mapping bugs report themselves as mapping bugs; LoROM/HiROM both mapped with undersized-ROM mirroring (the 32 KiB fixture's own reset vector depends on it). FR-CORE-013/035 is propagated from `rf-cart` through `SnesSystem::load` and tested across **twelve chipset bytes covering every named coprocessor family**, not one. 5A22 extras: mul/div with real 8/16-step latency and readable intermediates, NMITIMEN, H/V IRQ with `$4211` acknowledge-on-read, WRAM port reaching all 128 KiB. MDMA: eight channels, seven patterns, `$420B` triggering, 8 master cycles/byte — tested as a unit **and through the wired path with real 65816 code**. **The gap W6-06 recorded is closed:** both mirror-map images boot and report magic + 4/4 checks. **And running them found the LoROM fixture's own check 1 was vacuous** — `ca65` had shortened `lda $008000` to absolute addressing, which resolves through DBR (`$7E` here), so it compared WRAM against ROM and could never have proved the alias it names; HiROM hid it because its banks (`$40`/`$C0`) left `ca65` no short form to pick. Fixed with `f:`, verified by byte-level disassembly and by `hirom.sfc` rebuilding byte-identical to its existing pin. The lesson generalises: a fixture asserting from inside the machine is the strongest evidence available *and* is not self-verifying — an assembler sat between intent and assertion for a whole ticket — so the same aliases are now asserted directly in unit tests too. workspace: 1187 passing; 65816 vectors: 5,080,000 passing (re-verified, no regression).
- **W6-02b — Auto-joypad + interrupt timing: gilyon cputest green** (2026-08-19, closed): both criteria. **gilyon `cputest-full` is green — all 1610 tests, with the ROM writing its own verdict "Success" into VRAM.** Two independent readings on purpose: `test_num` (a zero-page word, which names the failing test on failure for lookup in the shipped `tests-full.txt`) and the ASCII text the ROM writes through `$2118`. `timing.rs` models the frame clock in master cycles (1364/line × 262 = 60.1 Hz, the same arithmetic `speed.rs` pins), vblank at line 225, `$4210` RDNMI clear-on-read, `$4212` HVBJOY, and the ~3-scanline auto-joypad window — which latches on the window's **closing edge**, not whenever idle, since continuous latching would make `$4218` track the pad in real time and hide any error in the window's timing. `$4210` clear-on-read is load-bearing rather than an edge case: without it `wait_for_vblank`'s first loop never exits and the ROM hangs before running a single test. **A documentation contradiction resolved against primary evidence:** gilyon's README documents a `(direct,X)` emulation-mode page-wrap this core doesn't implement, implying a bug — but the SingleStepTests bus traces give **88 discriminating cases, every one reading the high byte from the next page**, and gilyon's own ROM reports Success against this implementation. Two behavioural oracles against one line of prose; a test now pins the verified behaviour and quotes the README so nobody "fixes" correct code later. workspace: 1206 passing; 65816 vectors re-verified at 5,080,000 (no regression).
- **W6-03a — SNES PPU: modes 0/1 + OAM basics** (2026-08-20, closed): both criteria. BG modes 0/1 with per-BG char bases, per-BG 8×8/16×16 tile sizes, all four tilemap geometries, the shared write-twice scroll latches, and **BG3 priority asserted in both directions**; OBJ with all eight size pairs, signed 9-bit X, OAMADDR priority rotation, and both per-line limits. **The two OBJ limits are independent and are tested separately** — a 64-wide sprite costs eight slivers and an 8-wide one costs one, so five large sprites blow the 34-sliver budget while passing the 32-sprite count entirely; a single combined test would pass with either limit implemented alone. `$213E` range-over/time-over gives a second independent reading. **`dropped_by_limit` stays `false` on the SNES path too:** the W1-05a ruling makes `video_scanline` accuracy-exact, so suppressed sprites go out through the `OverlayPixel` channel — which means the field is now dead on *both* paths, flagged as an open question for whoever owns it. **A bug I introduced and caught:** wiring the PPU left two VRAM buffers (the `$2118` port filled the bus's, the renderer read the PPU's) — all 104 unit tests and gilyon cputest still passed, since they poke VRAM directly or only ever write it; the symptom would have been every game drawing a black screen, first noticed in W6-03b. VRAM is now single-sourced, with an end-to-end test. workspace: 1221 passing; 65816 vectors and gilyon cputest re-verified.
- **W6-03b — SNES PPU: first golden frames (PeterLemon subset)** (2026-08-20, closed): golden frames green on the four 2BPP BGMAP tests, one per background layer, in BG mode 0 — each layer exercised independently through real ROM code against a photographic test image. **The blocker dissolved exactly as W6-01a's did:** the manifest's `TODO-network-not-attempted` archive is 240 MB (verified), and is unnecessary — individual `.sfc` files fetch from the pinned commit, so the subset costs four 32 KiB files. **I looked at every frame before pinning its hash, and it mattered twice:** the first attempt produced four identical **all-black** frames that would have hashed consistently forever (these ROMs fade in over 15 vblanks; composition was happening after ~1 frame with VRAM still empty — the runner now waits for the fade semantically), and the "4BPP" ROM turned out to run in **mode 3**, not mode 1, rendering visible garbage — so the runner now *asserts the BG mode is implemented before hashing*, making "pin a wrong picture" impossible rather than dependent on someone noticing. An anti-vacuity test requires the four ROMs to produce four **distinct** frames, since identical hashes would mean the layer under test isn't the one being rendered. workspace: 1221 passing.
- **W6-04a — SPC700 core + ARAM + timers** (2026-08-20, closed): both criteria. **SPC700 vectors 256,000/256,000 across all 256 opcodes, zero unimplemented.** 64 KiB ARAM, `$F1` IPL banking, timers T0/T1 (8 kHz) and T2 (64 kHz) with their 4-bit read-to-clear counters. **IPL ruling taken (HLE — no Nintendo bytes in git *or* in a fetch list), recorded as reversible:** the `$F1` banking mechanism is implemented in full, backed by a clean-room stub; the boot protocol it serves is W6-04b's. The six ALU groups are table-driven off their shared low-nibble addressing family, which reached 237/256 opcodes in one pass; the 19 stragglers were one coherent family (memory-destination `dp,dp` / `dp,#imm` / `(X),(Y)` forms plus `XCN`) whose operand bytes arrive **source first**, opposite to how the mnemonic reads. Writes pass *through* the IPL window into ARAM — looks like a bug, isn't, and is how a boot loader stashes data in the region it executes from. **Found a latent bug in `scripts/validate-arch.sh` (not fixed here — out of scope):** its determinism lint greps for the RNG crate name with no word boundary, so any identifier ending in those letters (`Operand::`) trips ARCH VIOLATION — as did the first comment *explaining* the false positive, since the lint reads comments too. workspace: 1238 passing.
- **W6-04b — CPU↔APU ports + boot handshake** (2026-08-20, closed): all three criteria. `$2140-$2143` ↔ `$F4-$F7` in **catch-up sync, never free-running** — the APU accrues debt per master cycle and is settled *before* any port access, so the CPU can only observe a state the APU actually reached; `peek` deliberately does not catch up, since a debugger reading a port would otherwise advance the machine it's inspecting. A commercial-style init handshake driven by **real 65816 machine code** completes, uploads a byte, and starts the APU at the supplied entry. S-DSP skeleton: BRR decode (all four filters, the range 13-15 collapse-to-sign case, sign-extended nibbles) plus eight-voice mixing. **The W6-04a HLE ruling is now cashed out**, with its consequence stated in the code rather than buried: while the handshake runs the SPC700 does not execute; the uploaded program runs on the real core the moment it hands over. **The counter skip caught my own test:** the transfer counter is an *acknowledgement*, not an index — after byte N the APU expects N+1, so *ending* a block means skipping to N+2; my driver wrote N+1 and the handshake just stored another byte and never finished. workspace: 1253 passing.
- **W6-05 — RF-Scroller-S** (2026-08-20, HANDOFF — criteria 1–2 complete, criterion 3 half-delivered): the fixture builds deterministically from source in CI with `rom.sha256` pinned and verified, FORMAT.md documents the level format/RAM map/toolchain provenance, and **the scripted 5-minute log runs without faults with two independent machines reaching bit-identical reachable state at all 30 checkpoints**. Left `in_progress`: it is not gated as a `.rfreplay`, and cannot be — **`rf-input`'s replay format is NES-only** (`PortLogKey::buttons` is `Vec<NesButton>`; there is no `SnesButton` type), so a SNES button outside the NES 8-bit set — Right is bit 8 — is *silently dropped* on serialisation. Found the hard way: the recorded log diverged at the first checkpoint because run 2 received `buttons == 0`. The emulator was deterministic throughout; the log was lossy. A test pins the gap and fails *with instructions* once SNES support lands. **Deviation flagged:** built with cc65, not libSFX — W6-06 proved `ca65` assembles 65816 and it's already in CI, and this fixture needs none of what a framework offers (init is ~40 lines; the four characters are generated in-source so the build is reproducible from text). workspace: 1256 passing.
- **W7-01 — SNES phase 7 planning ticket** (2026-08-20, closed): phase 7 expanded into **W7-02..W7-13**, with acceptance drawn from `EMULATION_CORES.md` §3.2/§3.3/§3.4 and `TESTING.md`'s gate rows — DMA/HDMA, BG modes 2-6, Mode 7 + HD-Mode-7, colour math/windows/mosaic, interlace/overscan/hires, SNES save states, S-DSP echo/gaussian, the `[mods]` patch engine, PAL timing, AxROM + Action 53, and the PeterLemon/undisbeliever golden breadth. No ticket exceeds 8 points. **One ticket filed that this acceptance did not anticipate: W7-02**, SNES support in `rf-input`'s `.rfreplay` format — found during W6-05, which cannot finish its replay-gate criterion without it, so it blocks an already-open ticket. **A note for whoever edits plan.json next:** its unicode escaping is *mixed*, so no `json.load`/`json.dumps` round trip reproduces it at any `ensure_ascii` setting — a round trip churned 4027 lines before being reverted; the same twelve tickets inserted by targeted text surgery were 296 insertions and zero deletions.
- **W7-02 — rf-input: SNES button set + generalised replay log key** (2026-08-20, closed): `SnesButton` with the `$4218`/`$4219` layout the core already uses (A is bit 7, Right is bit 8), `PortLogKey::buttons` widened to a console-spanning `Button` enum, every SNES button surviving a record→serialize→parse→replay round trip. **Parsing is console-directed, and that's the load-bearing decision:** `A`, `B`, `Select`, `Start` and the four directions exist on *both* consoles at *different* bits, so there is deliberately no `Button::from_name` — the header's `console` field picks the table, and an unknown console falls back to NES because that's what every existing file already decodes as. Backward compatibility is **pinned literally** rather than round-tripped (a round trip would pass even if both sides changed together): the exact NES bytes `P1:A,B,Select,Start,Up,Down,Left,Right` and `|A......R|........|`.
- **W6-05 — RF-Scroller-S** (2026-08-20, closed): criterion 3 completed once W7-02 removed the blocker — the 5-minute log is now gated as a real `.rfreplay`, replayed on a fresh machine with bit-identical state at all 30 checkpoints. The test also asserts the log carries the SNES button table and that replayed buttons equal recorded ones, so a future format regression fails loudly instead of looking like an emulator divergence.
- **W7-14 filed (not fixed): RF-Scroller (NES) goldens have been stale since W5-02c.** Found while proving W7-02's backward compatibility, and verified *not* to be W7-02's doing (stashed the change, and re-ran at the pre-session commit in a worktree). `rom.sha256` last changed in `226b137` (W5-02c); the golden hashes gating it last changed in `3d9607e` (W2-21) — earlier. It hid because the test is `#[ignore]`d and CI runs plain `cargo test --workspace`. **The ticket says diagnose before re-pinning:** frames 1200 through 15000 all hash identically today, which is a static screen, not a scroller.
- **W7-14 — RF-Scroller (NES) goldens stale since W5-02c** (2026-08-20, closed): all four criteria, **diagnosis before re-pinning**. It was never a freeze: the level is 96 raw tile columns, so constant Right walks the player to the end at ~frame 900 and parks him (`player_x` 752, `columns_streamed` 95 — the level's last column and the fixture's own wraparound witness), and the screen legitimately stops changing. **Every golden was rendered to a PNG through the real NES palette and looked at** before being pinned — 655 shows mid-level scrolling, 1200 and 15000 the same coherent end-of-level screen. The five identical hashes are coverage, not waste: spread over four minutes they prove the emulator produces a bit-identical frame from identical state indefinitely, and a new test pins the *reason* so "the screen stopped changing" can't be confused with "the game froze". **The durable fix:** both five-minute replays are now wired into `scripts/local-gate.sh`, and `docs/TESTING.md` gained a "Hidden gates" section naming the class — CI runs `cargo test --workspace`, which skips `#[ignore]`d tests, so a Tier-A gate nothing runs is not a gate. workspace: 1267 passing.
- **W7-03 — SNES PPU: BG modes 2-6 + offset-per-tile** (2026-08-20, closed): all four criteria. Modes 2-6 with their documented depths, offset-per-tile in modes 2/4/6, the `$2130` direct-colour flag, and **ten PeterLemon goldens green, up from four**. 8bpp is four bitplane pairs 8 words apart (not two 4bpp tiles end to end), and it **ignores the tilemap's palette field** since it addresses all 256 CGRAM entries — applying it would fold a 256-colour image into a 32-colour block, which reads as a palette bug rather than a depth bug. Offset-per-tile *replaces* a column's scroll: modes 2/6 read two BG3 entries per column, mode 4 reads one and uses bit 15 — confusing them applies vertical offsets horizontally, a picture that looks almost right. **`32x64` and `64x32` hash identically, and that needed proving rather than explaining away:** a new test scrolls both a screen right, where the geometries genuinely diverge, so "they look the same" can't be confused with `tilemap_size` never being read. Every new golden was looked at first — the 8bpp ROMs render a detailed castle scene, TileFlip a perfectly symmetric seamless pattern. workspace: 1277 passing.
- **W7-04 — SNES PPU: Mode 7 + HD-Mode-7** (2026-08-20, HANDOFF — criteria 1-3 complete, criterion 4 partial): the 8.8 matrix with its shared write-twice latch, 13-bit signed centre registers, the 1024×1024 **interleaved** playfield (tilemap in even VRAM bytes, characters in odd bytes of the same words), all four screen-over behaviours, the `$2134-$2136` multiplier, sprites composing over mode 7, and HD-Mode-7 internal resolution. **RotZoom is pinned and was verified by eye** — a racing track with road, sand and red/white kerbing. **A disconnected pipeline of my own making:** `$2134-$2136` was implemented as `product()` and never wired to the bus; RotZoom computes its *rotation matrix* with that multiplier, so reads returned open bus (`$21`, the register address's own high byte) and every matrix element came back as `$2121`, rendering diagonal stripes. It looked like a mode-7 maths bug and wasn't — found by tracing the actual register writes. **Perspective and StarWars are excluded with evidence**, not waived: Perspective enables four HDMA channels (`$420C` = `$0F`) to rewrite the matrix per scanline — that *is* the effect — and StarWars renders fully transparent for ~170 frames with a static matrix, NMI off, CPU looping at `$00:8269`. **The board didn't record that W7-04's fourth criterion depends on W7-07.** workspace: 1285 passing.
- **W7-07 — SNES DMA/HDMA** (2026-08-20, HANDOFF — criteria 1-3 complete, criterion 4 blocked by design): the full HDMA table walker — per-frame init latching HDMAEN, line-counter reload with the repeat flag, the `$00` terminator, indirect mode through the `$43x7` bank, eight independent channels, master-cycle cost — plus a pattern-5 fix (it *alternates* between two registers). **The real work wasn't in the acceptance: per-scanline register latching.** §3.3 specifies each scanline composes from state "latched at line start"; until HDMA existed that was indistinguishable from using the frame's final values. The first wiring made Perspective *worse* — a coherent flat track became uniform sand, because all 224 lines composed from the last line's matrix. `Ppu::line_state` now latches the mode-7 matrix, BG mode and per-layer scroll per visible line. **That unblocked W7-04's Perspective golden**, now pinned and verified by eye: a ground plane receding to a horizon. RotZoom's hash is unchanged — the control. **Criterion 4 is not achievable with a scanline-composed PPU:** all 18 undisbeliever DMA/HDMA ROMs execute but none leaves forced blank in 12M instructions; they're per-*dot* timing tests, and §3.3 states the v1 PPU is "scanline-based … per-dot deferred". Recommend moving that criterion to a per-dot ticket. workspace: 1295 passing.
- **W7-05 — SNES PPU: windows, colour math, mosaic** (2026-08-20, HANDOFF — criteria 1-3 complete, criterion 4 unsatisfiable as written): windows WH0-WH3 with per-layer enable/invert, all four combiners, `$212E`/`$212F` mask gating; colour math `$2130-$2132` with add/sub/half and the four clip/prevent modes; mosaic `$2106` wired into the fetch so it actually blockifies. **The combiner only matters with both windows enabled** — with one, all four agree, which is why hard-coding OR passes most content; the test drives four x-positions against all four combiners with a counter-test. **Colour math collides with law 4, and the split is recorded rather than fudged:** it produces an RGB value that need not exist in CGRAM, but cores emit *indexed* pixels — so clip-to-black runs on the indexed path (black *is* index 0) and is asserted there, while the add/sub/half arithmetic is implemented and unit-tested in RGB but **cannot enter the pixel stream**. Carrying a sub-screen across `CoreSink` is an `rf-core-api` contract change; without it colour math can never be verified by a golden frame. **Criterion 4 names ROMs that do not exist in the pinned archive:** the undisbeliever 20210217 archive holds 29 `.sfc` files, all `hdma-*`, `hdmaen-*`, `inidisp-*` or `scpu-a-dma-bug-*` — no window ROM, no gradient ROM. workspace: 1308 passing.
- **W7-08 — S-DSP: echo, gaussian, ADSR/GAIN** (2026-08-20, HANDOFF — criterion 1 complete, 2 and 3 blocked): Gaussian interpolation, ADSR/GAIN envelopes, echo with its ARAM ring buffer and 8-tap FIR, pitch modulation and the noise LFSR, all wired into the mixer. **The unity-gain test earned its keep immediately** — a kernel whose taps don't sum to a constant changes *volume with pitch*, so a sustained note swells as it bends; my first table summed to 4080 and the test caught it on the first run. The kernel is now a cubic B-spline (a partition of unity by construction) plus an exact integer correction: fractions `f` and `255-f` read the *same* four indices, so there are 128 independent sums and the correction loop runs to 128, not 256. Named honestly as a bell-shaped kernel of the same family as hardware's, **not** the S-DSP's exact ROM table. **Criterion 2 is blocked on a real deadlock — with good news attached:** gilyon's `spctest.sfc` shows the **HLE boot handshake completes with a real commercial-style ROM** (boot reaches `Running`, the APU executes uploaded code), the first end-to-end validation of W6-04b — but the two processors then deadlock, the 65816 spinning at `$00:818E` and the SPC700 looping `$0306`-`$0309`. That's a post-boot port-protocol bug, not a DSP one. **Criterion 3 has no material:** no SPC set is designated anywhere and no reference audio exists. workspace: 1322 passing.
- **W7-11 — NES mappers: AxROM (7) and Action 53 (28)** (2026-08-20, HANDOFF — criteria 1-2 complete, 3 has no material): AxROM already existed (added at Brad's request during W2), so the work was **Action 53 (mapper 28)** — implemented, wired into the factory, added to `SUPPORTED_MAPPERS`, 12 unit tests. **It needed a bus change:** mapper 28 is the first with a register *outside* `$8000-$FFFF` — its select latch is at `$5000-$5FFF`, which `NesBus` dropped as open bus. Added as `Mapper::cpu_write_expansion` with a **default no-op**, so the other six mappers are byte-for-byte unchanged. **The spec was fetched from nesdev, not recalled** (law 2) — the banking arithmetic isn't guessable. Two details separate this from plain UNROM, both tested: a **fixed bank temporarily forces the outer size to 32 KiB** (letting mode 3's fixed `$C000` be bank 1/3/5/7 of a 128 KiB game, not just the last), and while 1-screen, **mirroring bit 0 is writable from three registers** — but D4 is *ignored* under H/V mirroring, which is the obvious bug and only shows on games doing both. **Criterion 3 has no material:** there is no mapper-28 test ROM in the manifest, TESTING.md, or anything fetched. Also flagged: the "~96%" figure is FR-CORE-027's own claim, and Action 53 is a *homebrew* multicart, so commercial coverage is unlikely to move much. workspace: 1335 passing.
- **W8-01 — Advanced-enhancements planning ticket** (2026-08-20, closed): phase 8 expanded into **W8-02..W8-12** — rewind, video recording + screenshot polish, gamepad UI navigation + accessibility, SNES widescreen per-BG policies, HUD separation, loading fast-forward, smooth-camera + room-stitching, the design-first frame-interpolation experiment, the AI upscaling cache + pack format, the HD-pack loader with Mesen identity and hires.txt import, and translation/accessibility overlays. **Two phase-7 tickets were filed here too, because they block *open* work:** W7-15 (per-dot PPU timing) unblocks both W7-07's and W7-05's undisbeliever criteria, and W7-16 (a `CoreSink` sub-screen channel) unblocks colour-math verification *and* answers the `dropped_by_limit` question W6-03a raised — the same missing mechanism is behind both, so they're one ticket. **Acceptance was written against artifacts that exist**, deliberately: five phase-7 tickets stalled on criteria naming ROMs absent from the pinned archive, an SPC set designated nowhere, and a mapper-28 test ROM that doesn't exist. The implementation landed in every one; the acceptance is what blocked. Hard-won lessons are cited on the tickets that will hit them — W5-04's white-PNG capture bug on W8-03, W4-09's zero-accessible-nodes finding on W8-04.
- **W8-02 — Rewind** (2026-08-20, closed): all three criteria. `rf-state/src/rewind.rs` implements SAVE_STATES.md §4's ring of delta-compressed snapshots (XOR + zstd), configurable interval and depth, **off by default** since §4 is explicit the memory cost is real. **The delta direction is the design decision:** the obvious reading — XOR against predecessor, reconstruct *forward* from a base — doesn't survive ring eviction, because dropping the base makes every chained delta unreadable. Storing deltas the other way (keep the live state uncompressed, each entry is the XOR to the *next*, rewind walks backwards since XOR is its own inverse) means eviction costs nothing but depth. **Criterion 2 in executable form:** a reference machine runs 300 frames straight; a second rewinds 100 and replays forward, compared **byte for byte**. **Criterion 3 measured, not estimated:** 64 snapshots of a 4 KiB machine cost 4,608 bytes vs 266,760 whole — **57.9×**, printed by the test. Deliberately not here: the input-replay half (rf-state owns no machine) and §5's hold-key scrub UI. workspace: 1343 passing.
- **W8-03 — Video recording + screenshot polish** (2026-08-20, closed): all three criteria. **The backend decision FRONTEND_UI.md §5 left open ("ffmpeg sidecar or gstreamer TBD") is taken, and it's APNG** — because APNG is PNG plus three chunk types, so it reuses this workspace's existing dependency-free stored-deflate encoder wholesale. That's exactly W3-04's reasoning for writing that encoder rather than adding a `png`/`image` crate, applied one step further; a sidecar would depend on an external binary, gstreamer on a native dep with a licence review. Plays in browsers, Preview, and ffmpeg/mpv. **The honest cost, stated up front:** frames are uncompressed (~`w*h*4` each; a 64×64 30-frame sample is 495 KB), so `estimated_bytes` reports size as it accrues and `with_limit` stops *before* filling a disk. **Criterion 3 said inspect the output, so every test does** — frame count read from the file's own `acTL`, chunk order asserted literally (get it wrong and a viewer shows *one frame*, which looks like "captured one frame" rather than a broken file), sequence numbers checked gapless, and a sample fed to an **external decoder** then rendered and looked at. One test ties the halves together: frame 0 of a recording must encode byte-identical `IDAT` to the still screenshot of the same image. Extension is `.png` on purpose — an APNG *is* a PNG and degrades gracefully. workspace: 1355 passing.
- **W8-04 — Gamepad UI navigation + accessibility** (2026-08-20, HANDOFF — model and harness complete, app-wide wiring remains): `ui_nav.rs` (11 tests) + `gamepad_nav.rs` (5 accessibility-tree tests). **It drives egui's focus system rather than replacing it** — egui's focus is what AccessKit reports, so it's what a screen reader *and* the headless harness both see; a parallel model would have been more code, invisible to the tree, and would have drifted from keyboard behaviour. `Back`/`Menu` deliberately produce **no** key event, since forging an Escape would also reach widgets that handle Escape themselves. **Auto-repeat has a test per failure mode:** repeating every frame makes a list uncontrollable, repeating from the first frame makes one tap jump two entries, activation must *not* repeat (scrolling a list vs. launching thirty games), and a pad unplugged mid-hold must not scroll forever. **W4-09's two traps are guarded explicitly:** the harness runs at 1600×1000 because zero nodes render at the default size, the label test requires ≥3 interactive nodes so it can't pass vacuously, and the fixture uses `selectable_label` since a plain label is reachable by neither gamepad nor screen reader. Reachability walks the *whole* order requiring every entry visited — an order that skips one is exactly the unreachability bug. **Remaining:** routing app.rs's own surfaces (mechanical but touches every window), and §6's contrast/UI-scale theme items. workspace: 1371 passing.
- **W8-07 — Profile-declared loading fast-forward** (2026-08-20, closed): all three criteria. `rf-enhance/src/loading.rs` decides pacing from `[loading].wait_loops` (PC + condition), off by default, every window ledgered by the profile author's own label. **It cannot alter simulation because it's given no way to:** `FastForward` owns no machine — read-only probe in, `Pacing` decision out. But *almost structural* isn't a test, so the acceptance drives a real deterministic machine through identical input twice, enabled and not, requiring **byte-identical** end states. **The determinism test caught a real semantic bug on its first run** — and precisely the one this module's own doc had predicted: `until = { addr, equals }` means "wait *until* addr equals this", so the wait runs while it does **not**. My first version compared with `==` and flagged exactly one frame: the one the wait *finished* on (1 unpaced frame instead of 30, and it was the last, not the first). The doc turned out to be describing the bug rather than guarding against it. **Anti-vacuity matters more than usual here:** "enabled and disabled runs match" is trivially true if the enabled run never fast-forwarded, so the test also requires a non-zero unpaced count. workspace: 1383 passing.
- **W8-10 — AI upscaling cache + replacement pack format** (2026-08-20, HANDOFF — criteria 2-3 complete, criterion 1 partial): `rf-ai` was a 15-line stub; it now has the replacement-pack manifest, the FR-AI-002 `(rom, asset, model, settings)` cache key, all-or-nothing validation, and §6's animation-set grouping. 20 tests. **One format for AI and artist packs** — what produced the pixels is *one field*, so both validate through the same path and cache side by side. **The palette is part of an asset's identity:** the same tile bytes under two palettes are two different pictures, and keying on pixels alone gives the classic HD-pack artefact where a recoloured enemy wears its sibling's skin. **Criterion 3 is all-or-nothing** — a mismatched pack is refused *whole*, because half-applied means the player can't tell which pixels are the game's; every fault is reported at once so an author fixes them in one pass. **A detail that would have silently grouped nothing:** the position test needs slack — a walking character moves a pixel or two per frame, so zero tolerance would split every walk cycle into single-frame sets, producing exactly the per-frame packs the feature exists to avoid. **Criterion 1 still owes the inference half:** a local ONNX runtime (`ort`) is a TECH_STACK row + licence review + model artifact — a dependency decision on the scale of the wgpu and egui rows, and outside this ticket's write_scope. workspace: 1402 passing.
- **W9-01 — Modding/profile-ecosystem planning ticket** (2026-08-20, closed): phase 9 expanded into **W9-02..W9-08** — profile editor GUI, wasmtime component tier + capability sandbox, SDK docs and compiled examples, the community distribution format with its intake CI, Mesen HD-pack import, the documentation site, and the second and third game profiles. Export tools are folded into the editor rather than filed separately: same surface, same data, and splitting them would create two tickets that can't be worked independently. **ROADMAP P9's exit criterion is quoted onto the ticket that owns it** — "a third party ships a profile + pack + script without touching Rust or asking us questions" — and W9-07/W9-04 require every code sample to be *compiled or executed by CI*, because documentation that isn't compiled goes stale silently (this repo has already seen that with §3.3's superseded `dropped_by_limit` line). **Two tickets carry explicit check-before-claiming warnings**, since phase 7 lost five tickets to criteria naming things that didn't exist: W9-03 (wasmtime TECH_STACK row + licence review first) and W9-06 (confirm a real Mesen pack is obtainable and licence-clear first).
- **W9-02 — Profile editor GUI** (2026-08-20, closed): all three criteria. `retroforge/src/profile_editor.rs` (headless, 9 unit tests) plus the editor pane in the author workspace and a kittest end-to-end test that creates, breaks, fixes and saves a profile through the real widgets. **The text buffer is the single source of truth, deliberately:** a typed model behind the form fields would be a second writer for one file, and the moment the author types into the text pane the two disagree with no way to see which wins on save. **An emitter built from `schema.rs`'s Rust field names would have emitted `ty = "u16"`** — the wire name is `type`, via a serde rename — and `ty` is not a hard error but an unknown-key *warning*, so criterion 2 would have failed quietly. One pinned skeleton template with wire names, checked by a test that runs the real loader and asserts zero warnings. **Validation is `loader::load_str` itself, never a copy:** the pane cannot drift from what the loader will say, and Save runs the same check before touching the filesystem. **Criterion 3's real content is the file, not the message** — the tests assert a refused save creates no file and leaves an existing one byte-identical, since a diagnostic-equality assertion alone is `f(x) == f(x)` and passes forever. **The panel's old `return` when no profile matched had to go:** a profile editor that needs a profile before it opens cannot create the first one. Mutation-checked — enabling Save unconditionally fails the harness test. **Honest limit recorded in the module doc:** the loader is fail-fast, so the pane shows one error at a time, and warnings only exist on the success path. workspace: 1412 passing.
- **Board unblock ruling** (2026-08-20, Brad): the four rulings that gated all 17 remaining tickets. **wasmtime Tier 2 is authorised** (TECH_STACK.md:34's "only when third-party demand is real" declared met) — W9-03 gets the row, the licence pass and the WIT/capability sandbox. **`ort` inference is authorised** — W8-10's criterion 1 gets the real pipeline; no model weights in git, fetch-and-pin like the ROM and vector fetchers. **W9-08 takes both options** — `room_grid` gets implemented (example-mode7 has declared it since W4-02 while decoding nothing) with a fixture that exercises it, AND profiles for documented commercial titles are permitted, with law 5 (no ROM bytes, no titles in engine code) and NFR-011 clean-room provenance still absolute. **W7-05 and W7-07's criterion 4 are amended to artifacts that exist**: the GitHub contents API at the pinned PeterLemon commit 350b394e lists `PPU/Window/{WindowHDMA,WindowMultiHDMA}`, `PPU/Mosaic/{Mode3,Mode5}` and `PPU/HDMA/{WaveHDMA,RedSpaceHDMA,RedSpaceIndirectHDMA,Mode7HDMA}` — **the ROMs both tickets wanted were in the other pinned source all along**, not missing from the world. The undisbeliever per-dot set stays W7-15's and colour-math goldens stay W7-16's, both of which already exist. 5 tickets unblocked, 12 more freed transitively.
- **W7-05 — SNES PPU: windows, colour math, mosaic** (2026-08-20, closed): criterion 4, against the amended acceptance. The ROMs the original criterion wanted were never in the undisbeliever archive it named — they were in the **already-pinned PeterLemon source all along**, found via the contents API at the same commit the fetcher has always used. **Looking at the frames changed three of the four outcomes.** (1) **Windows and mosaic were not latched per scanline**: W7-07 latched the mode-7 matrix, BG mode and scroll and stopped there, so WindowHDMA masked an identical 28 pixels on all 224 lines — an HDMA window rendered as a constant band, measured before and after. Fixed in `latch_line`, and unit-tested in `tests::ppu` so an `#[ignore]`d golden is not the only cover. (2) **MosaicMode3's golden would have been vacuous**: it settles with `$2106` at size 1 — mosaic *enabled* and doing nothing. The size is not on a timer; probing all twelve buttons showed **L and R alone drive it**, so the harness now holds R and captures at size 8. (3) **A latent i32 overflow in the mode-7 transform** that panics a debug build on legitimate registers — Perspective's line 0 gives `a * cx_fixed = -2,684,354,560`. No picture changes, because those samples fall outside the playfield whether the product wraps or not, which is exactly why a golden could be pinned over it. **MosaicMode5 is excluded, not pinned**: mode 5 is hi-res 512 and this PPU has no hi-res path, so it draws a half-width character on backdrop grey — its mosaic is correct, the mode it is drawn in does not exist yet. **W7-17 filed** to own hi-res. Every previously-pinned hash is byte-identical, which is the control. 15 goldens green; workspace: 1413 passing.
- **W7-07 — SNES DMA/HDMA: edge cases and the HDMA table walker** (2026-08-20, closed): criterion 4, against the amended acceptance. **WaveHDMA** (per-line `$210D` scroll → a rippling water surface) and **Mode7HDMA** (BG mode switched mid-frame → sky, sun and trees above a mode-7 ground plane) are pinned, each rendered and looked at. **The RedSpace pair is excluded, and the reason is the interesting part: their HDMA is verifiably correct.** The trace shows `$2121 ← $00`, then `$2122 ← $1F, $1E, $1D` — one step every 7 lines, 32 steps over exactly 224 — a red gradient down the screen, and the indirect variant dereferences its pointer to the identical values, which is **direct evidence that criterion 1's indirect-mode bank register works**. But these demos draw *nothing but the backdrop*: VRAM is legitimately empty, the CPU parks in a one-instruction loop at `$00:818B` with NMI off, and every pixel of the frame is palette index 0. This suite hashes palette **indices**, so a per-line CGRAM gradient is invisible to it by construction — the same index-vs-colour boundary that keeps colour math out of the pixel stream (law 4), not a defect. Pinned instead as a unit test that needs no fetched ROM, and **noted on W7-16**, which owns making colour-domain output reachable: per-line CGRAM is not a test-only concern, it is how real games do sunsets and water. 17 goldens green; workspace: 1414 passing.
